\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{|i|\cdot|\mathbf{v}|+\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}}w^{\mathbf{v}_{i}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in},i}-\sum_{j\to i}\mathbf{v}_{j}}c_{-1/w}\left(\mathsf{V}_{i}-\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in},i}-\sum_{b:j\to i}t_{b}^{-1}\mathsf{V}_{j}\right)\pi^{+}_{*}\left(\frac{\prod_{b:i\to i}(w+t_{b}-c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(-1)))}{(w-c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(-1)))(z-c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(-1)))}\right)_{w^{-s-1},z^{-r-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{\mathbf{v}^{+}_{i}+|i|\cdot|\mathbf{v}|+\sum_{i\to j}\mathbf{v}_{j}}z^{\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}-\mathbf{v}_{i}+\sum_{i\to j}\mathbf{v}_{j}}w^{\mathbf{v}_{i}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in},i}-\sum_{j\to i}\mathbf{v}_{j}}c_{-1/w}\left(\mathsf{V}_{i}-\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in},i}-\sum_{b:j\to i}t_{b}^{-1}\mathsf{V}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\prod_{b:i\to i}(w+t_{b}-z)}{w-z}c_{-1/z}\left(\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{out},i}+\sum_{a:i\to j}t_{a}\mathsf{V}_{j}-\mathsf{V}_{i}\right)\Bigg|_{w^{-s-1},z^{-r-1}}.

Then,
e_{i,r}f_{i,s}(1)
and
(-1)^{|i|}f_{i,s}e_{i,r}(1)
are given by the coefficients of
w^{-s-1}z^{-r-1}
in the same rational function:

(-1)^{\mathbf{v}^{+}_{i}+|i|\cdot|\mathbf{v}|+\sum_{i\to j}\mathbf{v}_{j}}z^{\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}-\mathbf{v}_{i}+\sum_{i\to j}\mathbf{v}_{j}}w^{\mathbf{v}_{i}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in},i}-\sum_{j\to i}\mathbf{v}_{j}}\\
\cdot c_{-1/w}\left(\mathsf{V}_{i}-\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in},i}-\sum_{b:j\to i}t_{b}^{-1}\mathsf{V}_{j}\right)c_{-1/z}\left(\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{out},i}+\sum_{a:i\to j}t_{a}\mathsf{V}_{j}-\mathsf{V}_{i}\right)\frac{\prod_{b:i\to i}(w+t_{b}-z)}{w-z},

but expanded in different orders. Namely, for
e_{i,r}f_{i,s}(1)
, we expand as

\frac{1}{w-z}=\frac{1}{w}+\frac{z}{w^{2}}+\cdots,

and for
(-1)^{|i|}f_{i,s}e_{i,r}(1)
, we expand as

\frac{1}{w-z}=-\frac{1}{z}-\frac{w}{z^{2}}+\cdots.

The difference is given by coefficient of
z^{-r-s-1}
in the residue of the rational function at
w=z
, that is,

\displaystyle e_{i,r}f_{i,s}(1)-(-1)^{|i|}f_{i,s}e_{i,r}(1)=(-1)^{1+\mathbf{v}_{i}+|i|\cdot|\mathbf{v}|+\sum_{i\to j}\mathbf{v}_{j}}\left(\prod_{b:i\to i}t_{b}\right)z^{\operatorname{rk}\mathsf{U}_{i}}c_{-1/z}\left(\mathsf{U}_{i}\right)\Bigg|_{z^{-r-s-1}}=\gamma_{i}h_{i,r+s}.

This finishes the proof.
∎

In the above proof, we have used the following lemma, whose proof is left to the interested readers.

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
X
be a variety with a complex of vector bundles
[\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{U}]
in cohomology degrees
-1
and
0
,
p\colon\mathbb{P}([\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{U}])\to X
denote the projection, then

\displaystyle p_{*}\left(\frac{1}{z-c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(1))}\right)=\left[z^{\operatorname{rk}\mathcal{V}-\operatorname{rk}\mathcal{U}}\frac{c_{-1/z}(\mathcal{V})}{c_{-1/z}(\mathcal{U})}\right]_{<0},

where the subscript
<0
means taking the negative power part of the power series.

3.2.
The case of symmetric quivers

When
Q
is symmetric, we can “twist” the multiplications in
\mathcal{DSH}_{\infty}(Q,\mathsf{W})
in order to make comparison with known Yangian type algebras.

Notation 3.5
.

We say
i\in Q_{0}^{\mathrm{b}}
(resp.
i\in Q_{0}^{\mathrm{f}}
) if
|i|=0
(resp.
|i|=1
), where
|\cdot|
is given by (
3.2
). Here
b
stands for
bosonic
and
f
stands for
fermionic
.
Fix a splitting
Q_{1}=\mathcal{A}\sqcup\mathcal{A}^{*}\sqcup\mathcal{E}
such that

•

there is an isomorphism
\iota\colon\mathcal{A}\cong\mathcal{A}^{*}
,
(a:i\to j)\mapsto(a^{*}:j\to i)
,

•

\mathcal{E}
is a set of edge loops, such that the tail map
t\colon\mathcal{E}\to Q_{0}
identifies
\mathcal{E}
with
Q_{0}^{\mathrm{b}}
.

Moreover, we fix a total order
\prec
on
Q_{0}^{\mathrm{f}}
.

Given the above data, we endow
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}
(
3.1
) with the
twisted Hall product
:

\displaystyle f\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}g=(-1)^{(\mathbf{v}^{(1)}|\mathbf{v}^{(2)})}f\star g,\,\,\,\;\text{for }\,\,f\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}(\mathbf{v}^{(1)}),\,\>g\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}(\mathbf{v}^{(2)}),

where
f\star g
is the usual Hall product, and

(3.18)

\displaystyle(\mathbf{v}^{(1)}|\mathbf{v}^{(2)}):=\sum_{(i\to j)\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbf{v}^{(1)}_{i}\cdot\mathbf{v}^{(2)}_{j}+\sum_{k\prec l\in Q_{0}^{\mathrm{f}}}\mathbf{v}^{(1)}_{k}\cdot\mathbf{v}^{(2)}_{l}.

Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}
denote the CoHA with the above twisted product,
\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}
denote its spherical subalgebra, and
\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}^{\mathrm{nil},\mathrm{op}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}
denote the
\mathbb{Z}/2
-graded opposite algebra. Note that the opposite product is given by

\displaystyle f\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}^{\mathrm{op}}g=(-1)^{(\mathbf{v}^{(2)}|\mathbf{v}^{(1)})+|\mathbf{v}^{(1)}|\cdot|\mathbf{v}^{(2)}|}g\star f,\,\,\,\;\text{for }\,f\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}(\mathbf{v}^{(1)}),\,\>g\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}(\mathbf{v}^{(2)}).

Definition 3.6
.

When
Q
is symmetric, the
Cartan doubled Yangian

\mathcal{D}\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\infty}(Q,\mathsf{W})
is the
\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]
-algebra obtained from the free product
\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}*\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}^{\mathrm{nil},\mathrm{op}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}*\mathcal{H}^{0}_{Q}
subject to the relations:

(3.19)

\displaystyle e_{i,r}f_{j,s}-(-1)^{|i|\cdot|j|}f_{j,s}e_{i,r}=(-1)^{(\delta_{i}|\delta_{i})}\delta_{ij}\gamma_{i}h_{i,r+s},\,\,\;\text{where }\,\gamma_{i}:=\prod_{e:i\to i}t_{e},

(3.20)

\displaystyle h_{i}(z)e_{j}(w)=\zeta_{ij}(z)e_{j}(w)h_{i}(z),\quad h_{i}(z)f_{j}(w)=\zeta_{ij}(z)^{-1}f_{j}(w)h_{i}(z),

where
\zeta_{ij}(z)
is the “bond factor”:

(3.21)

\displaystyle\zeta_{ij}(z)=\frac{\prod_{a:i\to j}(z-t_{a}-\sigma_{j})}{\prod_{b:j\to i}(z+t_{b}-\sigma_{j})},\;\text{where $\sigma_{j}$ is the operator }e_{j,r}\mapsto e_{j,r+1},\;f_{j,r}\mapsto f_{j,r+1},\;(r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}).

For
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}^{Q_{0}}
, the
Drinfeld type

\mu
-shifted Yangian

\mathcal{D}\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
is the quotient of
\mathcal{D}\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\infty}(Q,\mathsf{W})
by the relations

(3.22)

\displaystyle h_{i,t}=0\>\text{ for }\>t<-\mu_{i}-1,\quad h_{i,-\mu_{i}-1}=1.

Theorem 3.7
.

Let
\mu:=\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{Q_{0}}
. The following assignments

(3.23)

\displaystyle e_{i}(u)\mapsto\frac{(-1)^{(\delta_{i}|\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i})}}{u-c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i})}\,[\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})],

(3.24)

\displaystyle f_{i}(u)\mapsto\frac{(-1)^{1+\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}+|i|\cdot|\mathbf{v}|+(\mathbf{v}-\delta_{i}|\delta_{i})}}{u-c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i})}\,[\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}-\delta_{i},\underline{\mathbf{d}})]^{\mathrm{t}},

(3.25)

\displaystyle h_{i}(u)\mapsto u^{\mu_{i}}c_{-1/u}\left(\mathsf{U}_{i}\right),\;\text{ for }\;\mathsf{U}_{i}=\sum_{a:i\to j}t_{a}\mathsf{V}_{j}-\sum_{b:j\to i}t_{b}^{-1}\mathsf{V}_{j}+\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{out},i}-\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in},i}\>,

give rise to a
\mathcal{D}\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
action on
\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
.

Proof.

This follows directly from Theorem
3.3
.
∎

Remark 3.8
.

To have a well-defined notion of
shifted
Yangian, it is important to assume the quiver to be symmetric, so the rank of
\mathsf{U}_{i}
in (
3.25
)
only depends on the dimensions of framings.

Example 3.9
.

Let the pair
(A,D)
be a
generalized Cartan matrix with symmetrizer
, one can construct a quiver
Q
, whose associated tripled quiver
\widetilde{Q}
has a potential
\mathsf{W}
, see Appendix
A.1
.

Let
Y^{+}(\mathfrak{g}_{A,D})
be the
positive
part of the Yangian of the
symmetrizable Kac-Moody Lie algebra

\mathfrak{g}_{A,D}
associated to
(A,D)

[
GTL
,
YZ2
]
,
see Appendix
A.1
for a review of its definition.
Y^{+}(\mathfrak{g}_{A,D})
is generated by
\{x^{+}_{i,r}\}_{i\in Q_{0}}^{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
, and by Proposition
A.3
, there is a surjective map of
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
-algebras:

Y^{+}(\mathfrak{g}_{A,D})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\widetilde{Q},\mathsf{W}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar),\quad x^{+}_{i}(z):=\sum_{r\geqslant 0}x^{+}_{i,r}z^{-r-1}\mapsto e_{i}(z).

Given
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}^{Q_{0}}
, the
shifted Yangian

Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{g}_{A,D})
is the
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
-algebra obtained from the free product

Y^{+}(\mathfrak{g}_{A,D})*Y^{+}(\mathfrak{g}_{A,D})^{\mathrm{op}}*\mathbb{C}[\psi_{i,s}\>|\>i\in Q_{0},s\in\mathbb{Z}]

subject to relations

\displaystyle[x^{+}_{i,r},x^{-}_{j,s}]=\delta_{ij}\psi_{i,r+s},

(3.26)

\displaystyle\psi_{i}(z)x^{+}_{j}(w)=\zeta_{ij}(z)x^{+}_{j}(w)\psi_{i}(z),\quad\psi_{i}(z)x^{-}_{j}(w)=\zeta_{ij}(z)^{-1}x^{-}_{j}(w)\psi_{i}(z),

\displaystyle\psi_{i,r}=0\>\text{ for }\>r<-\mu_{i}-1,\quad\psi_{i,-\mu_{i}-1}=1.

Here
\psi_{i}(z)=\sum_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}\psi_{i,r}z^{-r-1}
, and
\zeta_{ij}(z)
is the bond factor

\displaystyle\zeta_{ij}(z)=\frac{z+d_{i}a_{ij}\hbar/2-\sigma_{j}}{z-d_{i}a_{ij}\hbar/2-\sigma_{j}},\;\text{where $\sigma_{j}$ is the operator }x^{\pm}_{j,r}\mapsto x^{\pm}_{j,r+1},\;(r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}).

Then there is a
surjective
map of
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
-algebras:

\displaystyle Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{g}_{A,D})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{D}\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\mu}(\widetilde{Q},\mathsf{W})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar),

\displaystyle x^{+}_{i}(z)\mapsto e_{i}(z),\quad x^{-}_{i}(z)\mapsto\frac{1}{d_{i}\hbar}f_{i}(z),\quad\psi_{i}(z)\mapsto h_{i}(z).

Example 3.10
.

Let
m,n\in\mathbb{N}
with
\max\{mn,m,n\}\geqslant 3
, and set
Q_{0}=\mathbb{Z}/(m+n)\mathbb{Z}
. A
parity sequence
of type
(m,n)
is a tuple
\mathbf{s}=(s_{1},s_{2},\ldots,s_{m+n})
of
\pm 1

with
m
entries equal to
1
.
Given a parity sequence
\mathbf{s}
, one can define a symmetric quiver
Q
with potential
\mathsf{W}
, see Appendix
A.2
.

Let
Y^{+}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_{n|m})
be the
positive
part of the
affine Yangian
of
\mathfrak{sl}_{n|m}
defined in
[
U
,
VV1
]
, which we review in
Appendix
A.2
. Then by Proposition
A.7
, there is a surjective
\mathbb{C}(\hbar,\varepsilon)
-algebra map

\displaystyle Y^{+}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_{n|m})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar,\varepsilon]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar,\varepsilon)\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar,\varepsilon]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar,\varepsilon),\quad x^{+}_{i}(z)\mapsto e_{i}(z),

For
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}^{Q_{0}}
, the
shifted affine Yangian

Y_{\mu}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_{n|m})
is the
\mathbb{C}[\hbar,\varepsilon]
-algebra obtained from the free product

Y^{+}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_{n|m})*Y^{+}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_{n|m})^{\mathrm{op}}*\mathbb{C}[\psi_{i,s}\>|\>i\in Q_{0},s\in\mathbb{Z}]

subject to relations

\displaystyle[x^{+}_{i,r},x^{-}_{j,s}]=\delta_{ij}\psi_{i,r+s},

(3.27)

\displaystyle\psi_{i}(z)x^{+}_{j}(w)=\zeta_{ij}(z)x^{+}_{j}(w)\psi_{i}(z),\quad\psi_{i}(z)x^{-}_{j}(w)=\zeta_{ij}(z)^{-1}x^{-}_{j}(w)\psi_{i}(z),

\displaystyle\psi_{i,r}=0\>\text{ for }\>r<-\mu_{i}-1,\quad\psi_{i,-\mu_{i}-1}=1.

Here
\psi_{i}(z)=\sum_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}\psi_{i,r}z^{-r-1}
, and
\zeta_{ij}(z)
is the bond factor

\displaystyle\zeta_{ij}(z)=\frac{z-a_{ij}\hbar-m_{ij}\varepsilon-\sigma_{j}}{z+a_{ij}\hbar-m_{ij}\varepsilon-\sigma_{j}},\;\text{where $a_{ij}$ and $m_{ij}$ are defined in \eqref{aij and mij}, and $\sigma_{j}$ is the operator }x^{\pm}_{j,r}\mapsto x^{\pm}_{j,r+1},\;(r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}).

Then there is a
surjective
map of
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
-algebras:

\displaystyle Y_{\mu}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_{n|m})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar,\varepsilon]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar,\varepsilon)\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{D}\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar,\varepsilon]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar,\varepsilon),

\displaystyle x^{+}_{i}(z)\mapsto e_{i}(z),\quad x^{-}_{i}(z)\mapsto\begin{cases}\frac{-1}{2s_{i}\hbar\>}f_{i}(z),&i\in Q_{0}^{\mathrm{b}}\\
f_{i}(z),&i\in Q_{0}^{\mathrm{f}}\end{cases},\quad\psi_{i}(z)\mapsto h_{i}(z).

Example 3.11
.

Let
Q
and
Q^{\circ}
with potential
\mathsf{W}
and
\mathsf{W}^{\circ}
be given in Appendix §
A.3.1
and §
A.3.2
, respectively.

Let
Y^{+}(D_{\lambda})
and
Y^{+}(D^{\circ}_{\lambda})
be the two versions of
positive
part of the Yangians of the
exceptional Lie superalgebra

D(2,1;\lambda)
defined in
[
LZ
]
, see Appendix §
A.3
for a review. Then by Proposition
A.10
and
A.11
, there are surjective
\mathbb{C}
-algebra maps

\displaystyle Y^{+}(D_{\lambda})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar,\lambda]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar,\lambda)\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]}\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0}),\quad Y^{+}(D^{\circ}_{\lambda})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar,\lambda]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar,\lambda)\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{Q^{\circ},\mathsf{W}^{\circ}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]}\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0}),

given by

\displaystyle x^{+}_{i}(z)\mapsto e_{i}(z),\quad\hbar\mapsto 2\hbar_{1},\quad\lambda\mapsto\hbar_{2}/\hbar_{1}.

For
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}^{Q_{0}}
, the
shifted Yangian

Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{g})
for
\mathfrak{g}=D_{\lambda}
or
D^{\circ}_{\lambda}
is the
\mathbb{C}[\hbar,\lambda]
-algebra obtained from the free product

Y^{+}(\mathfrak{g})*Y^{+}(\mathfrak{g})^{\mathrm{op}}*\mathbb{C}[\psi_{i,s}\>|\>i\in Q_{0}\text{ or }Q^{\circ}_{0},s\in\mathbb{Z}]

subject to relations

\displaystyle[x^{+}_{i,r},x^{-}_{j,s}]=\delta_{ij}\psi_{i,r+s},

(3.28)

\displaystyle\psi_{i}(z)x^{+}_{j}(w)=\zeta_{ij}(z)x^{+}_{j}(w)\psi_{i}(z),\quad\psi_{i}(z)x^{-}_{j}(w)=\zeta_{ij}(z)^{-1}x^{-}_{j}(w)\psi_{i}(z),

\displaystyle\psi_{i,r}=0\>\text{ for }\>r<-\mu_{i}-1,\quad\psi_{i,-\mu_{i}-1}=1.

Here
\psi_{i}(z)=\sum_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}\psi_{i,r}z^{-r-1}
, and
\zeta_{ij}(z)
is the bond factor

\displaystyle\zeta_{ij}(z)=\frac{z-a_{ij}\hbar-\sigma_{j}}{z+a_{ij}\hbar-\sigma_{j}},\;\text{where $a_{ij}$ is defined in \eqref{Cartan matrix for D(2,1)}, and $\sigma_{j}$ is the operator }x^{\pm}_{j,r}\mapsto x^{\pm}_{j,r+1},\;(r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}).

Then there are
surjective
maps of
\mathbb{C}
-algebras:

\displaystyle Y_{\mu}(D_{\lambda})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar,\lambda]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar,\lambda)\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{D}\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]}\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0}),\quad Y_{\mu}(D^{\circ}_{\lambda})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar,\lambda]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar,\lambda)\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{D}\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\mu}(Q^{\circ},\mathsf{W}^{\circ})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]}\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})

given by

\displaystyle x^{+}_{i}(z)\mapsto e_{i}(z),\quad x^{-}_{i}(z)\mapsto f_{i}(z),\quad\psi_{i}(z)\mapsto h_{i}(z),\quad\hbar\mapsto 2\hbar_{1},\quad\lambda\mapsto\hbar_{2}/\hbar_{1}.

Quivers with potentials related to affinizations
\widehat{D}_{\lambda}
and
\widehat{D}^{\circ}_{\lambda}
have been considered in
[
Bao
]
.

Remark 3.12
.

More generally, for a symmetric quiver with potential, we expect that the (shifted) quiver Yangian introduced by Galakhov-Li-Yamazaki in
[
GLY
,
LY
]
also has an algebra map to
the Drinfeld type Yangian
\mathcal{D}\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
(which is defined only when the quiver is symmetric, see Remark
3.8
). Moreover, we expect that the crystal representations of (shifted) quiver Yangian studied in
[
GLY
,
LY
,
L
]
can be identified with
\mathcal{D}\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
representations
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}
such that the crystals correspond to the torus fixed points of
\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\underline{\mathbf{d}})}(\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})
.

3.3.
Raising/lowering operators via stable envelopes

Definition 3.13
.

We call a framing
\underline{\mathbf{d}}

minuscule symmetric
if
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}=\delta_{i}
for some
i\in Q_{0}
; in the case of a minuscule symmetric framing
\underline{\delta_{i}}
, we say a framed potential
\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}

admissible
if

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}\big|_{X_{a}=0,\,\forall\,a\in Q_{1}}=0.

Example 3.14
.

A nonadmissible example is
\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}=\mathsf{W}+A_{i}B_{i}
where
A_{i}
(resp.
B_{i}
) is the in-coming (resp. out-going) framing at the node
i
. In this case, for all
\mathbf{v}\neq\mathbf{0}
, we have
\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\delta_{i})}(\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})=\emptyset
, and in particular
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\delta_{i}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})=0
. The admissibility condition ensures that the cohomology is nontrivial at least for
\mathbf{v}=\delta_{i}
, shown in the next lemma.

Lemma 3.15
.

Assume that the framed potential

\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}
on
\mathcal{M}(\delta_{i})
is admissible, and that
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-equivariant weights on every edge loop at
i\in Q_{0}
is nonzero (equivalently
\gamma_{i}=\prod_{e:i\to i}t_{e}
is nonzero), then

\displaystyle\dim_{\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})}H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}\left(\mathcal{M}(\delta_{i},\delta_{i}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}\right)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]}\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})=1.

Proof.

We have
\mathcal{M}(\delta_{i},\delta_{i})\cong\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{g}_{i}+1}
, where
\mathsf{g}_{i}=\#(i\to i\in Q_{1})
is the number of loops at
i
. Decompose
\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{g}_{i}+1}=\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{g}_{i}}\times\mathbb{C}
, then
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-weights on
\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{g}_{i}}
are nonzero, and
\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}\big|_{\mathbb{C}}=0
.
By equivariant localization, we have

H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}\left(\mathcal{M}(\delta_{i},\delta_{i}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}\right)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]}\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]}\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})=\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0}).\qed

Notation 3.16
.

In the following discussions, we use the notations:

|\delta_{i}\rangle:=\operatorname{can}([\mathbb{C}])\in H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}\left(\mathcal{M}(\delta_{i},\delta_{i}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}\right),\quad|\mathbf{0}\rangle:=[\operatorname{pt}]\in H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}\left(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{0},\delta_{i}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}\right)=H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\operatorname{pt}),

where
\mathbb{C}
is the component in the decomposition
\mathcal{M}(\delta_{i},\delta_{i})\cong\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{g}_{i}}\times\mathbb{C}
in the proof of Lemma
3.15
.

Consider a framing vector space
u\underline{\delta_{i}}+\underline{\mathbf{d}}
, where
u
is the equivariant variable of
\mathsf{A}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
. The next result is an explicit description of the first off-diagonal term of stable envelope
\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}
3
3

3

This coincides with Hall envelope (
2.1
) by
[
COZZ1
, Prop. 9.23]
.
. Let

(3.29)

F=\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{0},\delta_{i})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\quad F^{\prime}=\mathcal{M}(\delta_{i},\delta_{i})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})

be two connected components in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\underline{\delta_{i}}+\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}
, and

\langle\mathbf{0}|\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}|\delta_{i}\rangle\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]}\left(\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}(\mathbf{v}),\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i})\right)

be the matrix element of
\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}\big|_{F\times F^{\prime}}
in the basis
|\delta_{i}\rangle\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}(\mathbf{v})
and
|\mathbf{0}\rangle\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i})
.

Proposition 3.17
.

Assume that the framed potential on
\mathcal{M}(\delta_{i})
is admissible, and assume
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in},i}\geqslant\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}
for all
i\in Q_{0}
, then the matrix element
\langle\mathbf{0}|\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}|\delta_{i}\rangle
is induced by the correspondence:

\displaystyle(-u)^{\mathbf{v}_{i}}c_{-1/u}(\mathsf{V}_{i})\cap[\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})].

Here
c_{-1/u}(-)
denotes the
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-equivariant Chern polynomial with variable
-1/u
.

The proof of Proposition
3.17
follows from similar arguments for
K
-theory of Nakajima quiver varieties by Neguţ
[
N
, Prop. 3.16]
, which we now explain.
First of all, we have the following analog of
[
N
, Prop. 2.19]
.

Lemma 3.18
.

Fix dimension vectors
\mathbf{v},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},\underline{\mathbf{d}},\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{d}}}
, and linear maps
p_{i}\colon\widetilde{D}_{\operatorname{in},i}\to{D}_{\operatorname{in},i}
,
q_{i}\colon\widetilde{D}_{\operatorname{out},i}\to{D}_{\operatorname{out},i}
for all
i\in Q_{0}
. Consider the following complex of vector bundles on
\mathcal{M}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{d}}})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
:

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{Hom}(\widetilde{\mathsf{V}}_{i},\mathsf{V}_{i})\xrightarrow{f}\bigoplus_{(a\colon i\to j)\in Q_{1}}\operatorname{Hom}(\widetilde{\mathsf{V}}_{i},\mathsf{V}_{j})\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in Q_{0}}\left(\operatorname{Hom}(\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}_{\operatorname{in},i},\mathsf{V}_{i})\oplus\operatorname{Hom}(\widetilde{\mathsf{V}}_{i},\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{out},i})\right),

where the cohomological degrees are
-1
and
0
, and the map
f
is given by

\displaystyle f(\{\psi_{i}\}_{i\in Q_{0}})=(X_{a}\psi_{t(a)}-\psi_{h(a)}X_{a},\psi_{i}\widetilde{A}_{i},B_{i}\psi_{i}),

where
A_{i}
,
B_{i}
,
X_{a}
are maps in (
2.1
) for
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
, similarly for
\widetilde{A}_{i}
in
\mathcal{M}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{d}}})
.

Then the map
f
is fiberwise injective, so the cohomology of the complex defines a vector bundle
\mathcal{F}
. Moreover, the assignment

(3.30)

\displaystyle s\left((\widetilde{X}_{a},\widetilde{A}_{i},\widetilde{B}_{i}),(X_{a},A_{i},B_{i})\right)=(0,A_{i}p_{i},q_{i}\widetilde{B}_{i})

induces a section
s\in\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},\underline{\widetilde{\mathbf{d}}})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathcal{F})
whose zero locus is the set of pairs of framed quiver representations
(\widetilde{V}_{i},\widetilde{D}_{\operatorname{in},i},\widetilde{D}_{\operatorname{out},i}),(V_{i},D_{\operatorname{in},i},D_{\operatorname{out},i})
together with a framed quiver representation homomorphism

\displaystyle(\widetilde{V}_{i},\widetilde{D}_{\operatorname{in},i},\widetilde{D}_{\operatorname{out},i})\xrightarrow{(\psi_{i},p_{i},q_{i})}(V_{i},D_{\operatorname{in},i},D_{\operatorname{out},i}).

\{\psi_{i}\}_{i\in Q_{0}}
is uniquely determined if it exists. If
p_{i}
are surjective for all
i\in Q_{0}
, then so are
\psi_{i}
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of
[
N
, Prop. 2.19]
and we omit the details.
∎

For dimension vector
\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}=\delta_{i}+\mathbf{v}
,
by applying Lemma
3.18
to the projection

\mathbb{C}^{\underline{\delta_{i}}}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}\to\mathbb{C}^{\underline{\mathbf{d}}},

we get a vector bundle
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\underline{\delta_{i}}+\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
together with a section

s\in\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\underline{\delta_{i}}+\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathcal{F}).

By applying Lemma
3.18
to the identity map

\mathbb{C}^{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}}\mathbb{C}^{\underline{\mathbf{d}}},

we get a vector bundle
\overline{\mathcal{F}}
on
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
together with a section

\bar{s}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\overline{\mathcal{F}}).

Then we have the following analogue of
[
N
, Claim 3.17]
,
which we omit the proof.

Lemma 3.19
.

The attracting set closure
\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{u>0}(\Delta_{F^{\prime}})\subset\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\underline{\delta_{i}}+\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}(\delta_{i},\delta_{i})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
is a local complete intersection, cut out by the section
s\in\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\underline{\delta_{i}}+\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathcal{F})
, together with
(\mathsf{g}_{i}+1)
extra equations, where
\mathsf{g}_{i}
is the number of loops at node
i
.

The key ingredient of the proof of Proposition
3.17
is the following description of the matrix element
\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}\big|_{F\times F^{\prime}}
to the product of connected components
F
,
F^{\prime}
(
3.29
).

Lemma 3.20
.

The intersection
\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{u>0}(\Delta_{F^{\prime}})\bigcap\,(F\times F^{\prime})
is smooth, cut out by the section
\bar{s}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\overline{\mathcal{F}})
, together with
(\mathsf{g}_{i}+1)
extra equations.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.19
,
\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{u>0}(\Delta_{F^{\prime}})\bigcap\,(F\times F^{\prime})
is cut out by the restriction of
s\in\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\underline{\delta_{i}}+\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathcal{F})
to
F\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\cong\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
together with
(\mathsf{g}_{i}+1)
extra equations. Notice that

(3.31)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\big|_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})}\cong\overline{\mathcal{F}}\oplus\operatorname{Hom}(u\,\mathbb{C},\mathsf{V}_{i})

and under which we have

\displaystyle s\big|_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})}=(\bar{s},0).

Thus
\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{u>0}(\Delta_{F^{\prime}})\bigcap\,(F\times F^{\prime})
is cut out by
\bar{s}
together with
(\mathsf{g}_{i}+1)
extra equations.

The smoothness is the consequence of the following observation: the projection

\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}(\delta_{i},\delta_{i})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\to\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})

induces an isomorphism

(3.32)

\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{u>0}(\Delta_{F^{\prime}})\bigcap\,(F\times F^{\prime})\cong{\mathfrak{P}}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}).

In fact, the zero locus of
\bar{s}
on
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
is exactly

{\mathfrak{P}}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})=\left\{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{surjective maps between framed quiver representations }\\
\text{ $(V_{j}^{+},D_{\operatorname{in},j},D_{\operatorname{out},j})\twoheadrightarrow(V_{j},D_{\operatorname{in},j},D_{\operatorname{out},j})$ which are identity on framing}\end{subarray}\right\},

and the extra
(\mathsf{g}_{i}+1)
equations on
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}(\delta_{i},\delta_{i})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\to\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
are the following:

•

B_{i}
vanishes on the framed quiver representation
(\mathbb{C}^{\delta_{i}},\mathbb{C}^{\delta_{i}},\mathbb{C}^{\delta_{i}})
,

•

for every loop
a:i\to i
,
X_{a}
action on the gauge vector space
\mathbb{C}^{\delta_{i}}
agrees with the
X_{a}
action on the kernel of
(V_{j}^{+},D_{\operatorname{in},j},D_{\operatorname{out},j})\twoheadrightarrow(V_{j},D_{\operatorname{in},j},D_{\operatorname{out},j})
.

This proves (
3.32
). Note that
{\mathfrak{P}}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\cong\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{g}_{i}}
, and that

\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\cong\mathbb{P}(H^{0}\mathcal{C}^{-}_{i}),

where
H^{0}\mathcal{C}^{-}_{i}
is the zeroth cohomology of the complex
\mathcal{C}^{-}_{i}
on
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
defined in (
3.9
). The smoothness
follows from the local freeness of
H^{0}\mathcal{C}^{-}_{i}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.17
.

In the decomposition (
3.31
),
\operatorname{Hom}(u\mathbb{C},\mathsf{V}_{i})
is the excess vector bundle for the intersection between
\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{u>0}(\Delta_{F^{\prime}})
and
F\times F^{\prime}
, so
\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}\big|_{F\times F^{\prime}}
is induced by the correspondence

(-u)^{\mathbf{v}_{i}}c_{-1/u}(\mathsf{V}_{i})\cap\operatorname{can}\left[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{u>0}(\Delta_{F^{\prime}})\bigcap\,(F\times F^{\prime})\right].

Recall that
\mathcal{M}(\delta_{i},\delta_{i})\cong\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{g}_{i}}\times\mathbb{C}
, where
\mathbb{C}
accounts for the map
B_{i}
on the framed representation
(\mathbb{C}^{\delta_{i}},\mathbb{C}^{\delta_{i}},\mathbb{C}^{\delta_{i}})
. Then

\displaystyle\quad\,\,\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{u>0}(\Delta_{F^{\prime}})\bigcap\,(F\times F^{\prime})\bigcap\left(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\{0\}\times\mathbb{C}\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\right)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{surjective maps between framed quiver representations }\\
\text{ $(V_{j}^{+},D_{\operatorname{in},j},D_{\operatorname{out},j})\twoheadrightarrow(V_{j},D_{\operatorname{in},j},D_{\operatorname{out},j})$ which are identity on framing}\\
\text{and for all edge loops $a:i\to i$, $X_{a}$ action on the kernel is trivial}\end{subarray}\right\}=\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}).

And
\langle\mathbf{0}|\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}|\delta_{i}\rangle
is induced by the correspondence:

\displaystyle(-u)^{\mathbf{v}_{i}}c_{-1/u}(\mathsf{V}_{i})\cap[\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})].

This finishes the proof.
∎

With the same convention as in Section
3.2
, we define a virtual vector bundle on
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
:

(3.33)

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}=\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{Hom}(\mathsf{V}_{i},\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{out},i})+\sum_{(i\to j)\in\mathcal{A}^{*}}\operatorname{Hom}(\mathsf{V}_{i},\mathsf{V}_{j})+\sum_{k\prec l\in Q_{0}^{\mathrm{f}}}\operatorname{Hom}(\mathsf{V}_{l},\mathsf{V}_{k}),

and define stable envelope with a normalizer

(3.34)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},\epsilon}=\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}\cdot\,\epsilon,\;\text{with }\;\epsilon|_{F}=(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}\mathsf{P}|_{F}^{+}},

where
F
is a fixed component and
\mathsf{P}|_{F}^{+}
is the
\mathfrak{C}
-attracting part of the restriction of
\mathsf{P}
to
F
.

Theorem 3.21
.

Let
R(u)=\operatorname{Stab}_{u<0,\epsilon}^{-1}\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0,\epsilon}\in\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}\left(\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\delta_{i}},\mathrm{loc}}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathrm{loc}}\right)(u)
be the root
R
-matrix
and

R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u):=\Sigma\cdot R(u),

where
\Sigma
is the diagonal operator that acts on
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\delta_{i}}}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime\prime})
by the scalar
(-1)^{|\mathbf{v}^{\prime}|\cdot|\mathbf{v}^{\prime\prime}|}
.

Assume that
\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}
on
\mathcal{M}(\delta_{i})
is admissible, then the first
2
by
2
block of
R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)
admits Gauss decomposition:

(3.35)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\langle\mathbf{0}|R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)|\mathbf{0}\rangle&\langle\mathbf{0}|R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)|\delta_{i}\rangle\\
\langle\delta_{i}|R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)|\mathbf{0}\rangle&\langle\delta_{i}|R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)|\delta_{i}\rangle\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{(-1)^{|i|}t_{i}}{\gamma_{i}}f_{i}(u)&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}g_{i}(u)&0\\
0&g_{i}(u)h_{i}(u)\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&e_{i}(u)\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},

where
e_{i}(u)
,
f_{i}(u)
,
h_{i}(u)
are the linear operators in Theorem
3.7
acting on
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}
,
\gamma_{i}=\prod_{e:i\to i}t_{e}
for
t_{e}=

\mathsf{T}_{0}
-weight of the edge
e
,
t_{i}
is the
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-weight of the out-going framing
B_{i}
in
\mathcal{M}(\delta_{i})
, and
g_{i}(u)
is the following diagonal operator (with equivariant parameter written multiplicatively):

(3.36)

g_{i}(u)=c_{-1/u}((1-t_{i}^{-1})\mathsf{V}_{i}).

Proof.

By Proposition
3.17
, the first
2
by
2
block of
\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}
reads:

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\langle\mathbf{0}|\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}|\mathbf{0}\rangle&\langle\mathbf{0}|\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}|\delta_{i}\rangle\\
\langle\delta_{i}|\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}|\mathbf{0}\rangle&\langle\delta_{i}|\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}|\delta_{i}\rangle\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}e^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(N^{-}_{F/X})&0\\
0&e^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(N^{-}_{F^{\prime}/X})\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{[\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})]}{c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i})-u}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}.

By
[
COZZ1
, Lem. 3.29]
,
\operatorname{Stab}_{u<0}^{-1}
is induced by the transpose correspondence
[\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}]^{\mathrm{t}}
, so the first
2
by
2
block of
\operatorname{Stab}_{u<0}^{-1}\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}
admits Gauss decomposition

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{t_{i}}{\gamma_{i}}\frac{[\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})]^{\mathrm{t}}}{c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i})-u}&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{e^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(N^{-}_{F/X})}{e^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(N^{+}_{F/X})}&0\\
0&\frac{e^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(N^{-}_{F^{\prime}/X})}{e^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(N^{+}_{F^{\prime}/X})}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&\frac{[\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(\mathbf{v}+\delta_{i},\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})]}{c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i})-u}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}.

Taking normalizers into account, explicit computation gives (
3.35
).
∎

Remark 3.22
.

The
R
matrices
R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)
satisfy the
super Yang-Baxter equation
in the following sense. Let

R_{\alpha\beta}(u)=\operatorname{Stab}_{u<0,\epsilon}^{-1}\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0,\epsilon}\in\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}\left(\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{(\alpha)},\mathrm{loc}}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{(\beta)},\mathrm{loc}}\right)(u)

be the root
R
matrix, and
\Sigma
be the linear map that acts on
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{(\alpha)}}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{(\beta)}}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime\prime})
by the scalar
(-1)^{|\mathbf{v}^{\prime}|\cdot|\mathbf{v}^{\prime\prime}|}
. Define
R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\alpha\beta}(u):=\Sigma\cdot R_{\alpha\beta}(u)
, then the equation

(3.37)

\displaystyle R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{12}(u-v)R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{13}(u)R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{23}(v)=R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{23}(v)R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{13}(u)R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{12}(u-v)

holds in
\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}\left(\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{(1)},\mathrm{loc}}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{(2)},\mathrm{loc}}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{(3)},\mathrm{loc}}\right)(u,v)
. Here endomorphisms are
\mathbb{Z}/2
graded since they are taken in the category of super vector spaces, and
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}(\mathbf{v})
is of homogeneous degree
|\mathbf{v}|
(
3.2
). In dealing with endomorphisms on tensor products of super vector spaces, we have

\displaystyle\left(\operatorname{id}^{\otimes a}\otimes X\otimes\operatorname{id}^{\otimes b}\right)\left(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{a+b+1}v_{i}\right)=(-1)^{|X|\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{a}|v_{i}|}\left(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{a}v_{i}\right)\otimes Xv_{i}\>\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{j=a+1}^{a+b+1}v_{j}\right)

for homogeneous endomorphism
X
and homogeneous elements
v_{1},\ldots,v_{a+b+1}
.

4.
Reshetikhin type shifted Yangians for symmetric quivers with potentials

In this section, given a symmetric quiver
Q
with potential
\mathsf{W}
, and an auxiliary set
\mathcal{C}
of framing data, we construct Reshetikhin type (anti-dominant) shifted Yangians

\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
using the matrix elements of the
R
-matrices, which naturally act on the critical cohomology of (framed) quiver varieties with potentials.

We introduce the concept of admissibility of
\mathcal{C}
(Definition
4.1
), and show that
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
is independent of those admissible choices (Theorem
4.44
), and will be simply denoted
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
.
This is remarkable as one can use a ‘small’ (finite) admissible auxiliary set to generate the shifted Yangian assoicated
to a ‘large’ (infinite) admissible auxiliary set.
We also construct natural maps
from Drinfeld type shifted Yangians of the previous section to the Reshetikhin type shifted Yangians (Theorem
4.8
),
define coproducts by splitting
R
-matrices (§
4.2
) and shift homomorhisms relating Yangians
for different shifts (§
4.5
).

In the zero shift case, we construct Maulik-Okounkov (MO) type Lie superalgebra
\mathfrak{g}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}
out of
(Q,\mathsf{W})
and study their properties (Proposition
4.23
). This recovers the MO Lie algebra for tripled quiver with canonical cubic potential (Theorem
4.45
). We conjecture that the positive part of
\mathfrak{g}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}
equals to the BPS Lie algebra of Davison-Meinhardt
[
DM
]
(Conjecture
4.25
). This generalizes a conjecture of the second author, proven recently by Botta-Davison
[
BD
]
.

We prove a PBW basis theorem for
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
, i.e. there is a filtration on
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
such that the associated graded is the enveloping algebra of
\mathfrak{g}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}[u]
(Theorem
4.26
).
This generalizes a theorem of Maulik-Okounkov
[
MO
, Thm. 5.5.1]
from Nakajima quivers to symmetric quivers with potentials.

Several explicit formulas of coproducts are presented in §
4.6
. For quivers with potentials assoicated with symmetrizable Kac-Moody Lie algebras (Appendix
A.1
), we expect the coproduct constructed using algebraic method by Guay-Nakajima-Wendlandt
[
GNW
]
is compatible with the coproduct constructed from critical stable envelopes (Remark
4.42
), which we prove for all finite dimensional simple Lie algebras (Theorem
4.43
).

We also study the Casimir operators (Definition
4.47
) and show that they are given by
Steinberg correspondences (Proposition
4.48
). The corresponding shifted version is studied in Proposition
4.49
.

4.1.
Reshetikhin type shifted Yangians

4.1.1.
Definitions

We fix a symmetric quiver
Q
with
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-action and
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-invariant potential
\mathsf{W}
, as in the beginning of §
3
, and we assume that

•

\mathsf{T}_{0}
-equivariant weights on every edge loop at
i\in Q_{0}
is nonzero, equivalently
\gamma_{i}=\prod_{e:i\to i}t_{e}
is nonzero for every
i\in Q_{0}
, where
t_{a}
is the
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-equivariant weight of the arrow
a\in Q_{1}
.

•

For any dimension vectors
\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}\in\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}
, the quiver variety
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
is defined with respect to the cyclic stability.

Definition 4.1
.

An
auxiliary data set

\mathcal{C}=\{(\mathbf{d},\mathsf{T}_{0}\curvearrowright\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}})\}
is a (possibly infinite) set of the following triples: (1) a framing dimension
\mathbf{d}
, (2) a choice of
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-action on the symmetric quiver variety

\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{d})=\bigsqcup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}}\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),

extending the
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-action on the unframed quiver, (3) a
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-invariant framed potential
\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}
on
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{d})
extending
\mathsf{W}
.

For an element
\mathfrak{c}=(\mathbf{d},\mathsf{T}_{0}\curvearrowright\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}})\in\mathcal{C}
, the
auxiliary space
is the equivariant critical cohomology:

(4.1)

\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}}:=H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}).

An auxiliary data set
\mathcal{C}
is said to be
admissible
if for every
i\in Q_{0}
, there exists an element
\mathfrak{c}_{i}=(\delta_{i},\mathsf{T}_{0}\curvearrowright\mathcal{M}(\delta_{i}),\mathsf{W}_{i})\in\mathcal{C}
such that
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-weight on the out-going framing
B_{i}
in
\mathcal{M}(\delta_{i})
is nonzero (here we may assume
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-weight of in-coming framing
A_{i}
in
\mathcal{M}(\delta_{i})
is zero without loss of generality).
In this case,
\{\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{i}}\}_{i\in Q_{0}}
is called a collection of
admissible auxiliary spaces
.

Remark 4.2
.

If an auxiliary datum
(\delta_{i},\mathsf{T}_{0}\curvearrowright\mathcal{M}(\delta_{i}),\mathsf{W}_{i})
is admissible, then
\mathsf{w}_{i}
is automatically admissible in the sense of Definition
3.13
. In fact, if we write
\mathsf{W}_{i}\big|_{X_{a}=0,\>\forall a\in Q_{1}}=\sum_{k\geqslant 1}a_{k}(B_{i}A_{i})^{k}
, then the
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-invariance condition forces all
a_{k}
to be zero.

Remark 4.3
.

Admissible auxiliary data set always exists, since for each
i\in Q_{0}
, we can take the framed potential to be the trivial extension of
\mathsf{W}
, and choose a nonzero
\mathsf{T}_{0}
weight on
B_{i}
(
\mathsf{W}
is
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-invariant automatically).

Definition 4.4
.

A
state space
is the equivariant critical cohomology:

(4.2)

\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}:=H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}),

where the
\mathsf{T}_{0}
action on
\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}})
extends the
\mathsf{T}_{0}
action on the unframed quiver,
\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}
acts on
\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}})
as a framing torus, and
\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}=\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}})
for a
\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}
invariant framed potential
\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}
.

Remark 4.5
.

The spaces (
4.1
), (
4.2
) have natural
\mathbb{Z}^{Q_{0}}
-grading, whose degree zero part is spanned by

[\operatorname{pt}]\in H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\left(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{0},{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}\right)=H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt}),\quad[\operatorname{pt}]\in H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\left(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{0},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}\right)=H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt}).

Definition 4.6
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an auxiliary data set. For a given
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leqslant 0}^{Q_{0}}
, the
Reshetikhin type

\mu
-
shifted Yangian
with respect to
\mathcal{C}
is the
\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
-subalgebra

(4.3)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})\subset\prod_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}_{\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})[\mathsf{a}^{\mathrm{fr}}]}\left(\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}},\mathrm{loc}}\right),

generated by endomorphisms of the form

(4.4)

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}(m):=\underset{u\to\infty}{\operatorname{Res}}\operatorname{Str}_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c},\mathrm{loc}}}\left((m\otimes\operatorname{id})\cdot R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}},\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}}(u)\right),\quad m\in\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}_{\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}})[u],\quad\mathfrak{c}\in\mathcal{C},

where the product in (
4.3
) is taken for all state spaces with
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mu
, and the subscript “loc” means
-\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]}\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
. The superscript
\mathbb{Z}/2
means endowing state spaces with super vector space structures in the sense of Remark
3.22
, and “
\operatorname{Str}
” stands for the super trace.
Here the
R
-matrix can also be defined using the stable envelope as it is equal to the Hall envelope (Remark
2.6
).

We define the (
big
)
Reshetikhin type

\mu
-
shifted Yangian
of
(Q,\mathsf{W})
to be

(4.5)

\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W}):=\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\bigcup_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{C})\subset\prod_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}_{\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})[\mathsf{a}^{\mathrm{fr}}]}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}},\mathrm{loc}}),

where the union is taken over all auxiliary data sets.

By definition,
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
is a
(\mathbb{Z}^{Q_{0}}\times\mathbb{Z}/2)
-graded
\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
-algebra, where
\mathbb{Z}^{Q_{0}}
grading comes from the
\mathbb{Z}^{Q_{0}}
grading on
m\in\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}_{\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c},\mathrm{loc}})
.

The following is obvious by definition.

Lemma 4.7
.

If
\mathcal{C}\subset\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, then
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})\subseteq\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
.

Theorem 4.8
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is admissible, then for any
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leqslant 0}^{Q_{0}}
, the image of the map

\mathcal{D}\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})\to\prod_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}_{\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})[\mathsf{a}^{\mathrm{fr}}]}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}},\mathrm{loc}})

induced by the action in Theorem
3.7
is contained in
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
. In particular, the image of the above map is contained in
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
.

Proof.

Using the Gauss decomposition (
3.35
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}z^{-k-1}\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{E}(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|u^{k})&\mathsf{E}(|\delta_{i}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|u^{k})\\
\mathsf{E}(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\delta_{i}|u^{k})&\mathsf{E}(|\delta_{i}\rangle\langle\delta_{i}|u^{k})\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}g_{i}(z)&(-1)^{|i|}g_{i}(z)e_{i}(z)\\
\frac{(-1)^{|i|}t_{i}}{\gamma_{i}}f_{i}(z)g_{i}(z)&\frac{t_{i}}{\gamma_{i}}f_{i}(z)g_{i}(z)e_{i}(z)+(-1)^{|i|}g_{i}(z)h_{i}(z)\end{pmatrix}\;,

where
\langle\mathbf{0}|
denotes the dual vector of
|\mathbf{0}\rangle
in the (
\mathbb{Z}^{Q_{0}}
-graded) dual space,

|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|u^{k}\in\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}_{\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}})[u]
for some
\mathfrak{c}
,
and
g_{i}(z)
is given by (
3.36
).
It follows that the images of
e_{i}(z),f_{i}(z),h_{i}(z)
are contained in
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
.
∎

Remark 4.9
.

(
4.4
) makes sense for any rational function
m(u)
of
u
, in particular,

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}(m\>u^{-k})=\begin{cases}\operatorname{Str}_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c},\mathrm{loc}}}\left(m\cdot P_{Q_{0}\setminus\operatorname{Supp}(\mu)}\right)\>,&k=1\>,\\
0\>,&k>1\>,\end{cases}

where
P_{Q_{0}\setminus\operatorname{Supp}(\mu)}
is the projector from
\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}}
to the subspace

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\mu\cdot\mathbf{v}=0}\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathbf{v}).

4.1.2.
Triangular decomposition

Write the Gauss decompositions
R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)=F(u)G(u)E(u)
, where

(4.6)

\displaystyle F(u)=\pm\operatorname{Stab}^{-1}_{u<0,\epsilon}\cdot\>e^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(N^{+}),\quad G(u)=\pm\frac{e^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(N^{-})}{e^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(N^{+})},\quad E(u)=\pm e^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(N^{-})^{-1}\cdot\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0,\epsilon}\>,

where signs are determined by
\mathsf{P}
. Matrices elements of
F(u)
,
G(u)
, and
E(u)
can be written in terms of matrix elements of
R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)
by Gauss elimination.

Definition 4.10
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an auxiliary data set. For
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathcal{C}
,
m\in\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}_{\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}})[u]
, define the following elements in
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
:

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}^{+}(m):=\underset{u\to\infty}{\operatorname{Res}}\operatorname{Str}_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c},\mathrm{loc}}}\left((m\otimes\operatorname{id})\cdot E(u)\right),

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}^{0}(m):=\underset{u\to\infty}{\operatorname{Res}}\operatorname{Str}_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c},\mathrm{loc}}}\left((m\otimes\operatorname{id})\cdot G(u)\right),

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}^{-}(m):=\underset{u\to\infty}{\operatorname{Res}}\operatorname{Str}_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c},\mathrm{loc}}}\left((m\otimes\operatorname{id})\cdot F(u)\right).

Define
\mathsf{Y}^{+}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
,
\mathsf{Y}^{0}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
, and
\mathsf{Y}^{-}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
to be
\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
-subalgebras of
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
generated by
\mathsf{E}^{+}(m)
,
\mathsf{E}^{0}(m)
, and
\mathsf{E}^{-}(m)
respectively. And define
\mathsf{Y}^{\geqslant}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
(resp.
\mathsf{Y}^{\leqslant}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
) to be
\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
-subalgebra of
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
generated by
\mathsf{Y}^{+}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
(resp.
\mathsf{Y}^{-}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
) and
\mathsf{Y}^{0}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
.

Lemma 4.11
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is admissible, then
\mathsf{Y}^{0}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
is generated by the multiplication by tautological classes

(4.7)

\displaystyle\left\{\mathrm{ch}_{k}(\mathsf{V}_{i}),\>\mathrm{ch}_{k+1}(\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in},i}-\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{out},i})\right\}_{i\in Q_{0}}^{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}.

Here Chern characters are
(\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\text{fr}})
-equivariant.

Proof.

Observe that
\frac{e^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(N^{-})}{e^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(N^{+})}
is a power series in
u
whose coefficients on the state space are given by Chern classes of
\mathsf{V}_{i}
and
\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in},i}-\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{out},i}
. Thus
\mathsf{Y}^{0}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
is contained in the algebra generated by multiplication by tautological classes (
4.7
). For an admissible
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathcal{C}
, take the elements
|\mathbf{0}\rangle,|\delta_{i}\rangle\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}}
, and we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k!}\mathsf{E}^{0}(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\>u^{k})

\displaystyle=-t_{i}\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathrm{ch}_{k}(\mathsf{V}_{i})+\cdots\>,

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{|i|}}{k!}\mathsf{E}^{0}(|\delta_{i}\rangle\langle\delta_{i}|\>u^{k})

\displaystyle=-t_{i}\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathrm{ch}_{k}(\mathsf{V}_{i})+\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathrm{ch}_{k+1}(\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in},i}-\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{out},i})-\sum_{i,j\in Q_{0}}\boldsymbol{Q}_{ij}\>\mathrm{ch}_{k}(\mathsf{V}_{j})+\cdots\>,

for
\boldsymbol{Q}_{ij}=\sum_{a:i\to j}t_{a}+\sum_{b:j\to i}t_{b}
, where
t_{a}
is the
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-weight of an arrow
a
. Here the dot term stands for lower degree tautological classes. Inductively on
k
, we see that all elements in (
4.7
) are contained in
\mathsf{Y}^{0}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
.
∎

It follows from the Gauss decomposition and the definition that
\mathsf{Y}^{+}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
,
\mathsf{Y}^{0}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
, and
\mathsf{Y}^{-}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
generate
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
. When
\mathcal{C}
is admissible,
the following stronger statement holds.

Proposition 4.12
.

Assume
\mathcal{C}
is admissible, then
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
is generated by
\{\mathsf{E}^{\pm}(m)\>|\>m\text{ is constant in }u\}
and
\mathsf{Y}^{0}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
.

Proof.

\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
is filtered by degree in
u
: for
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}
,
F_{k}\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
is spanned by monomials
\mathsf{E}(m_{1}u^{n_{1}})\cdots\mathsf{E}(m_{r}u^{n_{r}})
with
m_{i}\in\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{i}})
,
\mathfrak{c}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}
, and
\sum n_{i}\leqslant k
. Note that in the Gauss decomposition

R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)=F(u)G(u)E(u),

F(u)
and
E(u)
are triangular with
u
-expansions of form
\operatorname{id}+O(u^{-1})
, and
G(u)
is diagonal with
u
-expansion of form

\displaystyle u^{\mu\cdot\mathbf{v}_{1}}+\text{lower degree in }u

on the component
\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathbf{v}_{1})\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathbf{v}_{2})
. As a consequence of the
u
-expansion being nonpositive, for a
\mathbb{Z}^{Q_{0}}
-homogeneous
m
constant in
u
, if
\mathsf{E}(m)\neq 0
, then
\deg(m)
must be either positive or negative or zero, and there must be equality

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}(m)=\begin{cases}\mathsf{E}^{+}(m),&\deg(m)>0,\\
\mathsf{E}^{0}(m),&\deg(m)=0,\\
\mathsf{E}^{-}(m),&\deg(m)<0.\\
\end{cases}

Then it is clear that
F_{0}\>\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
is contained in the subalgebra generated by
\{\mathsf{E}^{\pm}(m)\>|\>m\text{ is constant in }u\}
and
\mathsf{Y}^{0}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
.

\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
has another filtration labelled by
\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}^{Q_{0}}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}^{Q_{0}}
. Define
G_{\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2}}\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
to be the subalgebra generated by
\mathsf{E}(m)
for
m\in\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime}_{1}),\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime}_{2}))[u]
with

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{1}\geqslant\mathbf{v}^{\prime}_{1}\geqslant\mathbf{0},\;\text{ and }\>\mathbf{v}_{2}\geqslant\mathbf{v}^{\prime}_{2}\geqslant\mathbf{0}.

Note that
G_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0}}\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
is generated by multiplication by tautological classes
\{\mathrm{ch}_{k}(\mathsf{V}_{i})\}_{i\in Q_{0}}^{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
, and in particular
G_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0}}\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})\subset\mathsf{Y}^{0}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
. We also define
G^{<}_{\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2}}\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
to be the subalgebra generated by

\displaystyle\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{v}_{1}\geqslant\mathbf{v}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2}\geqslant\mathbf{v}^{\prime}_{2}\\
(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2})\neq(\mathbf{v}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}_{2})\end{subarray}}\>G_{\mathbf{v}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}_{2}}\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C}).

We prove that
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
is generated by
\mathsf{Y}^{0}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
and
\{\mathsf{E}^{\pm}(m)\>|\>m\text{ is constant in }u\}
by using double induction on the filtrations
G_{\bullet,\bullet}
and
F_{\bullet}
. The initial cases
G_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0}}\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
and
F_{0}\>\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
are known.

Assume that
G^{<}_{\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2}}\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
can be generated. Take arbitrary
X\in\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathbf{v}_{1}),\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathbf{v}_{2}))
and arbitrary
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}
, we need to show that
\mathsf{E}(X\>u^{k})
can be generated.

If
\deg(X)=\mathbf{v}_{2}-\mathbf{v}_{1}=0
, then
[
MO
, (4.34)]
still applies to the shifted case and we get

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}(X\>u^{k})\equiv\mathsf{E}^{0}(X\>u^{k})\mod G^{<}_{\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2}}\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C}),

so
\mathsf{E}(m\>u^{k})
can be generated.

If
\deg(X)_{i}=\mathbf{v}_{2,i}-\mathbf{v}_{1,i}\neq 0
for some
i\in Q_{0}
, then we prove by induction on
k
that
\mathsf{E}(X\>u^{k})
can be generated. The initial case
k=0
is known from the above discussions. Take the vacuum state
|\mathbf{0}\rangle
in an admissible auxiliary space
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}_{i}}_{\underline{\delta_{i}}}
, and let
t_{i}
be the
\mathsf{T}_{0}
weight of the out-going arrow on the node
i
for
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}_{i}}_{\underline{\delta_{i}}}
, which is nontrivial by admissibility. Then

\displaystyle-t_{i}\deg(X)_{i}\>\mathsf{E}(X\>u^{k})

\displaystyle=[\mathsf{E}(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|),\mathsf{E}(X\>u^{k})]

by Lemma
4.13

\displaystyle=[\mathsf{E}(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\>u),\mathsf{E}(X\>u^{k-1})]+\mathsf{E}(X^{\prime}\>u^{k-1})+\mathsf{E}(X^{\prime\prime}\>u^{k-1})\cdot\mathsf{E}(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|)+\text{term in }G^{<}_{\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2}}\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C}),

for certain
X^{\prime},X^{\prime\prime}\in\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathbf{v}_{1}),\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathbf{v}_{2}))
. Here we have used Lemma
4.13
which is proven below. Note that for any
n
,
\mathsf{E}(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\>u^{n})\in\mathsf{Y}^{0}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
. Then
\mathsf{E}(X\>u^{k})
can be generated by induction. This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.13
.

Let
\mathfrak{c}_{1},\mathfrak{c}_{2}
be arbitrary auxiliary data,
|\mathbf{0}\rangle
be the vacuum of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{1}}(\mathbf{0})
,
m\in\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}(\mathbf{v}_{1}),\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}(\mathbf{v}_{2}))
. Then for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}
, we have

(4.8)

\displaystyle[\mathsf{E}(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\>u),\mathsf{E}(m\>u^{k})]-[\mathsf{E}(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|),\mathsf{E}(m\>u^{k+1})]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{E}(m^{\prime}\>u^{k})+\mathsf{E}(m^{\prime\prime}\>u^{k})\>\mathsf{E}(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\mathbf{v}_{2}\geqslant\mathbf{v}>0}\mathsf{E}(f_{\mathbf{v}}\>u^{k})\mathsf{E}(g_{\mathbf{v}})+\sum_{\mathbf{v}_{1}\geqslant\mathbf{v}>0}\mathsf{E}(f^{\prime}_{\mathbf{v}}\>u^{k})\mathsf{E}(g^{\prime}_{\mathbf{v}}),

for certain
m^{\prime},m^{\prime\prime}\in\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}(\mathbf{v}_{1}),\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}(\mathbf{v}_{2}))
,
g_{\mathbf{v}}\in\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{1}}(\mathbf{0}),\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{1}}(\mathbf{v}))
,
f_{\mathbf{v}}\in\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}(\mathbf{v}_{1}),\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}(\mathbf{v}_{2}-\mathbf{v}))
,
g^{\prime}_{\mathbf{v}}\in\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{1}}(\mathbf{v}),\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{1}}(\mathbf{0}))
,
f^{\prime}_{\mathbf{v}}\in\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}(\mathbf{v}_{1}-\mathbf{v}),\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}(\mathbf{v}_{2}))
.

Proof.

By the RTT=TTR equation, we have

\displaystyle[\mathsf{E}(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\>v),\mathsf{E}(m\>u^{k})]=\underset{v\to\infty}{\operatorname{Res}}\underset{u\to\infty}{\operatorname{Res}}\operatorname{Str}_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}}\left[\left(R_{12}(v-u)(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\>v\>\otimes m\>u^{k})R_{12}(v-u)^{-1}-|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\>v\>\otimes m\>u^{k}\right)T_{2}(u)T_{1}(v)\right],

where
T_{i}

(i=1,2)
is the
R
-matrix that braids
\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{i}}
with a state space. By taking expansions

R_{12}(v-u)=\operatorname{id}+\frac{\boldsymbol{r}_{12}}{v-u}+\frac{\boldsymbol{s}}{(v-u)^{2}}+O((v-u)^{-3}),

R_{12}(v-u)^{-1}=\operatorname{id}-\frac{\boldsymbol{r}_{12}}{v-u}+\frac{\boldsymbol{s}^{\prime}}{(v-u)^{2}}+O((v-u)^{-3}),

we get

[\mathsf{E}(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\>v),\mathsf{E}(m\>u^{k})]=\underset{v\to\infty}{\operatorname{Res}}\underset{u\to\infty}{\operatorname{Res}}\operatorname{Str}_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}}\\
\left[\left([\boldsymbol{r}_{12},|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\otimes m](u^{k}+v^{-1}u^{k+1})+\boldsymbol{s}\cdot(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\otimes m)v^{-1}u^{k}+(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\otimes m)\cdot\boldsymbol{s}^{\prime}\>v^{-1}u^{k}\right)T_{2}(u)T_{1}(v)\right].

We ignore the higher order in
u/v
terms in the above expansion because
T_{1}(v)
has no pole as
v\to\infty
. The same argument shows that

\displaystyle[\mathsf{E}(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\>v),\mathsf{E}(m\>u^{k})]=\underset{v\to\infty}{\operatorname{Res}}\underset{u\to\infty}{\operatorname{Res}}\operatorname{Str}_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}}\left[[\boldsymbol{r}_{12},|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\otimes m]v^{-1}u^{k+1}\>T_{2}(u)T_{1}(v)\right].

It follows that

\text{LHS of \eqref{fund induction}}=\underset{v\to\infty}{\operatorname{Res}}\underset{u\to\infty}{\operatorname{Res}}\operatorname{Str}_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}}\\
\left[\left([\boldsymbol{r}_{12},|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\otimes m]\>u^{k}+\boldsymbol{s}\cdot(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\otimes m)v^{-1}u^{k}+(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\otimes m)\cdot\boldsymbol{s}^{\prime}\>v^{-1}u^{k}\right)T_{2}(u)T_{1}(v)\right].

Since the nonvanishing terms in
\lim_{v\to\infty}T_{1}(v)
are concentrated in the diagonal, we have

\displaystyle\underset{v\to\infty}{\operatorname{Res}}\underset{u\to\infty}{\operatorname{Res}}\operatorname{Str}_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}}\left[\left(\boldsymbol{s}\cdot(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\otimes m)v^{-1}u^{k}+(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\otimes m)\cdot\boldsymbol{s}^{\prime}\>v^{-1}u^{k}\right)T_{2}(u)T_{1}(v)\right]=\mathsf{E}\left(m^{\prime}\>u^{k}\right),

where
m^{\prime}=\langle\mathbf{0}|\boldsymbol{s}|\mathbf{0}\rangle\cdot m+m\cdot\langle\mathbf{0}|\boldsymbol{s}^{\prime}|\mathbf{0}\rangle
is a homomorphism from
\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}(\mathbf{v}_{1})
to
\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}(\mathbf{v}_{2})
. On the other hand, we can write the other term in the LHS of (
4.8
) in homogeneous components and get

\displaystyle\underset{v\to\infty}{\operatorname{Res}}\underset{u\to\infty}{\operatorname{Res}}\operatorname{Str}_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}}\left[\boldsymbol{r}_{12}\>(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\otimes m)u^{k}\>T_{2}(u)T_{1}(v)\right]=\sum_{\mathbf{v}_{2}\geqslant\mathbf{v}\geqslant 0}\mathsf{E}(f_{\mathbf{v}}\>u^{k})\mathsf{E}(g_{\mathbf{v}}),

where
g_{\mathbf{v}}\otimes f_{\mathbf{v}}
is the short-hand notation for the
\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{1}}(\mathbf{0})\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}(\mathbf{v}_{1}),\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{1}}(\mathbf{v})\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}(\mathbf{v}_{2}-\mathbf{v}))
component of
\boldsymbol{r}_{12}\>(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\otimes m)
.
g_{\mathbf{v}}\otimes f_{\mathbf{v}}
should be actually a linear combination of this form but we suppress the summation for simplicity. Similarly,

\displaystyle\underset{v\to\infty}{\operatorname{Res}}\underset{u\to\infty}{\operatorname{Res}}\operatorname{Str}_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}}\left[(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\otimes m)\>\boldsymbol{r}_{12}\>u^{k}\>T_{2}(u)T_{1}(v)\right]=\sum_{\mathbf{v}_{2}\geqslant\mathbf{v}\geqslant 0}\mathsf{E}(f^{\prime}_{\mathbf{v}}\>u^{k})\mathsf{E}(g^{\prime}_{\mathbf{v}})

for
g^{\prime}_{\mathbf{v}}\otimes f^{\prime}_{\mathbf{v}}
being the
\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{1}}(\mathbf{v})\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}(\mathbf{v}_{1}-\mathbf{v}),\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{1}}(\mathbf{0})\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}_{2}}(\mathbf{v}_{2}))
component of
(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\otimes m)\>\boldsymbol{r}_{12}
. Combine the above three equations and let
\mathsf{E}(m^{\prime\prime}\>u^{k})\>\mathsf{E}(|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|)=\mathsf{E}(f_{\mathbf{0}}\>u^{k})\mathsf{E}(g_{\mathbf{0}})+\mathsf{E}(f^{\prime}_{\mathbf{0}}\>u^{k})\mathsf{E}(g^{\prime}_{\mathbf{0}})
, we get (
4.8
).
∎

4.2.
Coproducts

Suppose there is a decomposition of framing torus
\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}=\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{1}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{2}
with corresponding decomposition of framing:
\underline{\mathbf{d}}=\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}+\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}
. Consider the
+
chamber
\{\mathsf{a}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{1}>\mathsf{a}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{2}\}
and the corresponding stable envelope

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{+,\epsilon}\colon\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{1},\mathrm{loc}}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{2},\mathrm{loc}}\to\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{1}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{2},\mathrm{loc}}.

The triangle lemma implies that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{+,\epsilon}^{-1}R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}\operatorname{Stab}_{+,\epsilon}=R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}}\cdot R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}},

where
R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}
stands for the
R
-matrix braiding on auxiliary space
\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}}
with
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{1}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{2}}
, and similarly for
R_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}}
and
R_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}}
.
It follows that
\mathsf{E}(m\>u^{k})
’s of
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
transform as

(4.9)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{+,\epsilon}^{-1}\mathsf{E}(m\>u^{k})\operatorname{Stab}_{+,\epsilon}=\sum_{i=-1}^{k}\sum(-1)^{|m_{1}|\cdot|m_{2}|}\mathsf{E}(m_{2}\>u^{i})\otimes\mathsf{E}(m_{1}\>u^{k-1-i})\>,

where
\sum m_{1}\otimes m_{2}
is the image of
m
under the dual multiplication map
\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c},\mathrm{loc}})\to\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c},\mathrm{loc}})\otimes\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c},\mathrm{loc}})
. Explicitly,
\sum m_{1}\otimes m_{2}
is determined by requiring

\displaystyle\operatorname{Str}(m\cdot x\cdot y)=\sum\operatorname{Str}(m_{1}\cdot x)\>\operatorname{Str}(m_{2}\cdot y)\>,

for all
x,y\in\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c},\mathrm{loc}})
.

Fix
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leqslant 0}^{Q_{0}}
. Collecting all decompositions
\underline{\mathbf{d}}=\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}+\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}
with

\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{\operatorname{out}}-\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mu_{1},\quad\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}_{\operatorname{out}}-\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mu_{2},

and all framed potentials
\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}
, we obtain a map

\displaystyle\prod\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{1}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{2},\mathrm{loc}})\to\prod\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{1},\mathrm{loc}})\otimes\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{2},\mathrm{loc}}),

which maps the subalgebra
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
to

\displaystyle\mathsf{Y}_{\mu_{1}}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})\widehat{\otimes}\mathsf{Y}_{\mu_{2}}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})\subset\prod\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{1},\mathrm{loc}})\otimes\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{2},\mathrm{loc}}).

This induces a
coproduct
:

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mu_{1},\mu_{2}}\colon\mathsf{Y}_{\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})\to\mathsf{Y}_{\mu_{1}}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})\widehat{\otimes}\mathsf{Y}_{\mu_{2}}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})\>.

When
\mu_{1}=\mu_{2}=0
, we also write
\Delta:=\Delta_{0,0}
.

By the triangle lemma, we have:

Proposition 4.14
.

The coproduct is
coassociative
, that is,

\displaystyle(\Delta_{\mu_{1},\mu_{2}}\otimes\operatorname{id})\circ\Delta_{\mu_{1}+\mu_{2},\mu_{3}}=(\operatorname{id}\otimes\Delta_{\mu_{2},\mu_{3}})\circ\Delta_{\mu_{1},\mu_{2}+\mu_{3}},\quad\mu_{1,2,3}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leqslant 0}^{Q_{0}}\>.

Remark 4.15
.

(
\Delta
is counital): Let
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{0}}}=\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]
be a state space with zero shift, and
\varepsilon\colon\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})\to\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
be the map induced by projection from
\prod\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}},\mathrm{loc}})
to
\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{0}},\mathrm{loc}})=\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
. Then

\displaystyle(\varepsilon\otimes\operatorname{id})\circ\Delta_{0,\mu}=\operatorname{id},\quad(\operatorname{id}\otimes\,\varepsilon)\circ\Delta_{\mu,0}=\operatorname{id}.

The following is obvious from definition:

Lemma 4.16
.

If
\mathcal{C}\subset\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, then the subalgebra
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})\subseteq\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
is closed under coproduct, that is, the diagram commutes

\textstyle{\mathsf{Y}_{\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{\mu_{1},\mu_{2}}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Y}_{\mu_{1}}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})\widehat{\otimes}\mathsf{Y}_{\mu_{2}}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Y}_{\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{\mu_{1},\mu_{2}}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Y}_{\mu_{1}}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C}^{\prime})\widehat{\otimes}\mathsf{Y}_{\mu_{2}}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}).}

Remark 4.17
.

Define the stable envelope with opposite normalizer
\bar{\epsilon}
be

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},\bar{\epsilon}}:=\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}\cdot\,\bar{\epsilon},\;\text{ with }\;\bar{\epsilon}|_{F}=(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}N^{+}_{F/\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})}+\operatorname{rk}\mathsf{P}|_{F}^{-}}\;,

then
R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)
can be written as
\operatorname{Stab}_{-,\bar{\epsilon}}^{-1}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{+,\epsilon}
.
The triangle lemma implies that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{-,\bar{\epsilon}}^{-1}R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}\operatorname{Stab}_{-,\bar{\epsilon}}=R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}}\cdot R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}}.

Then
\mathsf{E}(m\>u^{k})
’s of
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
transform as

(4.10)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{-,\bar{\epsilon}}^{-1}\mathsf{E}(m\>u^{k})\operatorname{Stab}_{-,\bar{\epsilon}}=\sum_{i=-1}^{k}\sum\mathsf{E}(m_{1}\>u^{i})\otimes\mathsf{E}(m_{2}\>u^{k-1-i})\>,

where
\sum m_{1}\otimes m_{2}
is determined by requiring
\operatorname{Str}(m\cdot x\cdot y)=\sum\operatorname{Str}(m_{1}\cdot x)\>\operatorname{Str}(m_{2}\cdot y)
for all
x,y\in\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c},\mathrm{loc}})
.
Then
\operatorname{Stab}_{-,\bar{\epsilon}}^{-1}(\cdots)\operatorname{Stab}_{-,\bar{\epsilon}}
induces a map

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mu_{1},\mu_{2}}^{\mathrm{op}}\colon\mathsf{Y}_{\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})\to\mathsf{Y}_{\mu_{1}}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})\widehat{\otimes}\mathsf{Y}_{\mu_{2}}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})\>.

which we call the
opposite coproduct
.
\Delta_{\mu_{1},\mu_{2}}^{\mathrm{op}}
is coassociative and counital, and it is related to
\Delta_{\mu_{1},\mu_{2}}
by

\displaystyle R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}}\cdot\Delta_{\mu_{1},\mu_{2}}(x)\cdot(R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}})^{-1}=\Delta_{\mu_{1},\mu_{2}}^{\mathrm{op}}(x),\quad\forall\,x\in\mathsf{Y}_{\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C}),

where
R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}}=\operatorname{Stab}_{-,\bar{\epsilon}}^{-1}\operatorname{Stab}_{+,\epsilon}
is the
R
-matrix braiding
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{1}}
with
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{2}}
.

Remark 4.18
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{\operatorname{out}}
,
\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}_{\operatorname{out}}
,
and let
\mathcal{L}
be a Steinberg correspondence on
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times_{\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})}\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
(see Definition
2.17
), then Theorem
2.21
implies that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{+,\epsilon}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{L}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{+,\epsilon}=\operatorname{Stab}_{-,\bar{\epsilon}}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{L}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{-,\bar{\epsilon}}

equivalently,

\displaystyle\left[R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}}\;,\;\operatorname{Stab}_{+,\epsilon}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{L}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{+,\epsilon}\right]=0

4.3.
The Lie superalgebra
\mathfrak{g}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}

Throughout this subsection, we focus on the symmetric framing
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}
, or equivalently the zero shift
\mu=0
case.

Definition 4.19
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}\subset\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
be
\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})}\{\mathsf{E}(m_{0})\>|\>m_{0}\text{ is constant in }u\}
.

Using the expansion (
2.15
), we see that
\mathfrak{g}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}
consists of matrix elements of the classical
R
-matrix
\boldsymbol{r}
. This implies the following.

Proposition 4.20
.

Every element
x\in\mathfrak{g}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}
is primitive:

\displaystyle\Delta(x)=x\otimes 1+1\otimes x.

In particular,
\Delta(x)
commutes with
R
matrices:

\displaystyle[\Delta(x),R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)]=0,\quad[\Delta(x),\boldsymbol{r}]=0.

Lemma 4.21
.

\mathfrak{g}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}
is a Lie superalgebra with the Lie brackets being the super commutators in
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
.

Proof.

The same argument as
[
MO
, Prop. 5.2.1]
shows that

(4.11)

\displaystyle[\mathsf{E}(m\>u^{i}),\mathsf{E}(m^{\prime}\>u^{j})]=\mathsf{E}\left((\operatorname{tr}\otimes\operatorname{id})\>[\boldsymbol{r},m\otimes m^{\prime}]\>u^{i+j}\right)+\text{lower degree in }u.

The lemma follows by plugging
i=j=0
into the above equation.
∎

The Lie superalgebra

\mathfrak{g}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}
inherits the
\mathbb{Z}^{Q_{0}}
-grading from
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{Q_{0}}}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha},\quad[\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha},\mathfrak{g}_{\beta}]\subseteq\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha+\beta}\>.

Definition 4.22
.

We call
\mathfrak{g}_{0}
the
Cartan subalgebra
. A nonzero vector
\eta
with
\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}\neq 0
is called a
root
of
\mathfrak{g}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}
is called the
root space
. A root
\eta
is a
positive root
if
\eta\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}^{Q_{0}}
;
\eta
is a
negative root
if
-\eta
is a positive root.

Proposition 4.23
.

We have the following facts for
\mathfrak{g}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}
.

(1)

The Cartan subalgebra decomposes as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{0}=\mathfrak{v}\oplus\mathfrak{d},\quad\mathfrak{v}=\bigoplus_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})\cdot\mathsf{v}_{i},\quad\mathfrak{d}=\bigoplus_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})\cdot\mathsf{d}_{i},

where
\mathsf{v}_{i}
is the linear functional whose value on
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}(\mathbf{v})
is
\mathbf{v}_{i}
,
\mathsf{d}_{i}
is the linear functional whose value on
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}(\mathbf{v})
is
c_{1}(\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in},i})-c_{1}(\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{out},i})
.

(2)

\mathfrak{g}_{0}
is a maximal super commutative subalgebra of
\mathfrak{g}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}
.

(3)

All root spaces are finite dimensional.

(4)

All roots are either positive or negative.

(5)

There exists a nondegenerate super symmetric (adjoint action) invariant bilinear form

(\cdot,\cdot)_{\mathfrak{g}}\colon\mathfrak{g}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}\times\mathfrak{g}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}\to\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})\>.

With respect to this form, we have
\mathfrak{g}_{-\eta}\cong\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}^{\vee}
.

(6)

The invariant tensor of
(\cdot,\cdot)_{\mathfrak{g}}
is the classical
R
-matrix
\boldsymbol{r}
, explicitly

(4.12)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{r}=\boldsymbol{r}_{\operatorname{diag}}+\sum_{\alpha>0}\sum_{s}\left(e_{\alpha}^{(s)}\otimes e_{-\alpha}^{(s)}+(-1)^{|\alpha|}e_{-\alpha}^{(s)}\otimes e_{\alpha}^{(s)}\right),

where the sum is taken for all positive roots
\alpha
,
and for each
\alpha
,

s
is summed over an index finite set depending on
\alpha
, and
|\alpha|
is given as (
3.2
).
The elements
