\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{-1}=[\operatorname{Stab}_{-\mathfrak{C}}]^{\mathrm{t}}\circ\,\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})_{\mathrm{loc}}\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w})_{\mathrm{loc}}\,,

where
(-)_{\mathrm{loc}}
stands for
(-)\otimes_{H_{\mathsf{A}}(\operatorname{pt})}\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{A}}(\operatorname{pt})
. Note that the convolution
[\operatorname{Stab}_{-\mathfrak{C}}]^{\mathrm{t}}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)]^{\mathrm{vir}}\circ[\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]
is a class in
H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}}\times_{X_{0}}X^{\mathsf{A}})
by the proof of
[
COZZ1
, Lem. 3.29]
, so
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{-1}\circ\,\mathrm{Q}_{\beta}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}
is defined without localization.

Theorem 5.5
.

Let
X
be a symmetric quiver variety (Definition
2.1
) with a torus
\mathsf{T}
-action, and a
\mathsf{T}
-invariant function
\mathsf{w}
. Let

\mathsf{A}\subset\mathsf{T}
be a self-dual subtorus,
\mathfrak{C}
be a chamber.

For any
\mathsf{A}
-fixed component
F\subseteq X^{\mathsf{A}}
, and
\beta\in H_{2}(F,\mathbb{Z})
a nonzero effective class, we have a commutative diagram:

(5.7)

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(F,\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\scriptstyle{(-1)^{(c_{1}(N^{-}_{F/X}),\beta)}\cdot\mathrm{Q}_{\beta}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}_{\beta}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(F,\mathsf{w})}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}),\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\,\,(-)|_{F}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{-1}}

up to a scalar operator of
\mathsf{T}/\mathsf{A}
-weight. Here
(-)|_{F}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(F,\mathsf{w})
is the projection to component
F
.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.22
, it suffices to show that

(5.8)

[\operatorname{Stab}_{-\mathfrak{C}}]^{\mathrm{t}}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)]^{\mathrm{vir}}\circ[\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]=(-1)^{(c_{1}(N^{-}_{F/X}),\beta)}\cdot\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{M}_{0,2}(F,\beta)]^{\mathrm{vir}}\in H^{\mathsf{A}}(F\times_{X_{0}}F).

Below we use the following properties of a symmetric quiver variety
X
:

(1)

the affinization map
X\to X_{0}
is birational and small (ref.
[
COZZ2
, Thm. 1.1]
),

(2)

there is an
\mathsf{A}
-equivariant flat map
\pi\colon X\to B
to an affine space such that generic fibers are affine (ref.
[
COZZ2
, Thm. 1.1]
),

(3)

the virtual dimension satisfies
\mathrm{vdim}(\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta))=\dim X-1
.

By (3) and the definition of convolution product, both sides of (
5.8
) have homological degree
(2\dim F-2)
in
H^{\mathsf{A}}(F\times_{X_{0}}F)
. By (1),
\dim F\times_{X_{0}}F\leqslant\dim F
and there is a unique irreducible component of
F\times_{X_{0}}F
with dimension equal to
\dim F
. By (2), both sides of (
5.8
) are supported away from an
\mathsf{A}
-invariant dense open subset
U
of the diagonal
\Delta_{F}
. So both sides of (
5.8
) are
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combinations of top-dimensional cycles in
(F\times_{X_{0}}F)\setminus U
, in particular, they are independent of the
\mathsf{A}
-equivariant parameters. Then it is enough to prove (
5.8
) in
\mathsf{A}
-localized cohomology with the
\mathsf{A}
-equivariant parameters
\mathsf{a}
taken to infinity in some direction.

We recall the notion of (un-)broken curves in
[
MO
, §7.3]
,
[
OP
, §3.8.2]
. By deformation theory, a connected component in
\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)^{\mathsf{A}}
is either broken or unbroken. By Proposition
5.6
and Lemma
5.7
below, only unbroken components for which two marked points do not lie in a contracted connected subcurve contribute to the
\mathsf{A}
-localization in the
\mathsf{a}\to\infty
limit.
We also do not have unbroken chains connecting two points in the same component of
X^{\mathsf{A}}
by
[
MO
, Lem. 7.3.3]
.
Hence the only nonzero contribution comes from the component whose elements consist of stable maps to
F
. In the
\mathsf{a}\to\infty
limit, we have

\displaystyle[\operatorname{Stab}_{-\mathfrak{C}}]^{\mathrm{t}}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)]^{\mathrm{vir}}\circ[\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]

\displaystyle\sim e^{\mathsf{A}}(N_{F/X})\cdot\operatorname{ev}_{*}\left(\frac{[\overline{M}_{0,2}(F,\beta)]^{\mathrm{vir}}}{e^{\mathsf{A}}\left(N^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\overline{M}_{0,2}(F,\beta)/\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\sim(-1)^{(c_{1}(N^{-}_{F/X}),\beta)}\mathsf{a}^{\operatorname{rk}N_{F/X}-\operatorname{rk}N^{\mathrm{vir}}}\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{M}_{0,2}(F,\beta)]^{\mathrm{vir}}

where the sign comes from the fact that
\mathsf{A}
is a self-dual torus,

\operatorname{rk}N^{\mathrm{vir}}
is the rank of virtual normal bundle of
\overline{M}_{0,2}(F,\beta)
in
\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)
, which equals to

\displaystyle\chi(C,f^{*}N_{F/X})

\displaystyle=\chi(C,f^{*}N^{+}_{F/X})+\chi(C,f^{*}N^{-}_{F/X})=\chi(C,f^{*}N^{+}_{F/X})+\chi(C,f^{*}(N^{+}_{F/X})^{\vee})

\displaystyle=2\operatorname{rk}N^{+}_{F/X}=\operatorname{rk}N_{F/X}\>.

Therefore,
[\operatorname{Stab}_{-\mathfrak{C}}]^{\mathrm{t}}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)]^{\mathrm{vir}}\circ[\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\sim(-1)^{(c_{1}(N^{-}_{F/X}),\beta)}\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{M}_{0,2}(F,\beta)]^{\mathrm{vir}}
in the
\mathsf{a}\to\infty
limit. This finishes the proof.
∎

Proposition 5.6
.

In the
\mathsf{a}\to\infty
limit, a broken component for which two marked points do not lie in a contracted connected subcurve contributes trivially to the left hand side of (
5.8
).

Proof.

Let
\overline{M}
be a broken components for which two marked points do not lie in contracted connected subcurve, and
f\colon C\to X
be a stable map in
\overline{M}
, then
there are two possibilities:

(a)

C=C_{L}\cup C_{R}
, such that
C_{L}
and
C_{R}
are two connected genus zero (possibly reducible) curves intersecting at a node
p
with
f(p)\in F^{\prime}
(
F^{\prime}
is a connected component of
X^{\mathsf{A}}
), and
T_{C_{L},p}\otimes T_{C_{R},p}
has nonzero
\mathsf{A}
weight, and the marked points satisfy
v_{1}\in C_{L}
,
v_{2}\in C_{R}
.

(b)

C=C_{0}\cup C_{1}\cup\cdots\cup C_{n}
with
n\geqslant 1
such that every
C_{i}
is a connected genus zero (possibly reducible) curve,
f(C_{0})\subseteq F
with
\deg f|_{C_{0}}\neq 0
, and the two marked points
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
lie in
C_{0}
, and for every
i>0
,
C_{i}
intersects with
C_{0}
at a node
p_{i}
, and the tangent vector
T_{C_{i},p_{i}}
has nonzero
\mathsf{A}
weight.

By deformation theory, only one of (a) or (b) can happen in this component.

Case (a).
In this situation,
\overline{M}
is of the shape

\displaystyle\overline{M}\cong\overline{M}_{L}\times_{F^{\prime}}\overline{M}_{R}\,,

where
\overline{M}_{L}
(resp.
\overline{M}_{R}
) is the stable map moduli stack corresponding to
C_{L}
(resp. to
C_{R}
), and the maps
\overline{M}_{L}\to F^{\prime}
and
\overline{M}_{R}\to F^{\prime}
are evaluations at the node
p
.

The normalization sequence for the universal domain at the node
p
is

0\to\mathcal{O}_{C}\to\mathcal{O}_{C_{L}}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{C_{R}}\to\mathcal{O}_{p}\to 0\,,

and it follows that the tangent complex of
\overline{M}
is related to the tangent complexes of
\overline{M}_{L}
and
\overline{M}_{R}
by the exact triangle

(5.9)

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}_{\overline{M}}\to\mathrm{pr}_{L}^{*}\mathbb{T}_{\overline{M}_{L}}\oplus\mathrm{pr}_{R}^{*}\mathbb{T}_{\overline{M}_{R}}\to\operatorname{ev}_{p}^{*}\mathbb{T}_{F^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{T}_{\overline{M}}[1],

\mathrm{pr}_{L\text{ or }R}
is the projection from
\overline{M}
to
\overline{M}_{L\text{ or }R}
.

The virtual normal contribution
N^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\overline{M}/\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)}
breaks into

(5.10)

\displaystyle N^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\overline{M}/\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)}\big|_{[f\colon C\to X]}=N_{L}\big|_{[f|_{C_{L}}]}\oplus N_{R}\big|_{[f|_{C_{R}}]}-T_{X}|_{f(p)}^{\mathrm{moving}}+T_{C_{L},p}\otimes T_{C_{R},p}\,,

where
N_{L\text{ or }R}\big|_{[f|_{C_{L\text{ or }R}}]}=H^{*}(C_{L\text{ or }R},f^{*}T_{X})^{\mathrm{moving}}
.

It follows from (
5.9
) and (
5.10
) that the contribution of
\overline{M}
breaks into sum of convolution products:

\displaystyle e^{\mathsf{A}}\left(N_{F/X}^{+}\right)\operatorname{ev}_{*}\left(\frac{[\overline{M}]^{\mathrm{vir}}}{e^{\mathsf{A}}\left(N^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\overline{M}/\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)}\right)}\right)e^{\mathsf{A}}\left(N_{F/X}^{-}\right)=\sum_{\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R}}\operatorname{pr}_{*}\Delta_{F^{\prime}}^{*}\left(\operatorname{ev}_{*}(\gamma_{L}\cdot[\overline{M}_{L}]^{\mathrm{vir}})\boxtimes\operatorname{ev}_{*}(\gamma_{R}\cdot[\overline{M}_{R}]^{\mathrm{vir}})\right),

where
\Delta_{F^{\prime}}\colon F\times F^{\prime}\times F\hookrightarrow F\times F^{\prime}\times F^{\prime}\times F
is given by the diagonal embedding
F^{\prime}\hookrightarrow F^{\prime}\times F^{\prime}
,
\operatorname{pr}\colon F\times F^{\prime}\times F\to F\times F
is the projection to the first and last factor,
\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R}
are cohomology classes on
\overline{M}_{L}
and
\overline{M}_{R}
respectively which come from equivariant Euler classes of
-T_{X}|_{f(p)}^{\mathrm{moving}}+T_{C_{L},p}\otimes T_{C_{R},p}
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to B
be the flat map to the affine space
B
as mentioned above. According to
[
COZZ2
, Lem. 3.22]
, for any
b\in B
,

\dim F\times_{X_{0}}F^{\prime}\times_{B}\{b\}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\left(\dim F+\dim F^{\prime}\right)-\dim B.

Note that
\pi\colon\pi^{-1}(U)\to U
is affine for an open dense subset
U\subset B
, so
\operatorname{ev}(\overline{M}_{L})
is away from
F\times_{X_{0}}F^{\prime}\times_{B}U
, and we see that
\operatorname{ev}(\overline{M}_{L})
is contained in a closed subset of
F\times_{X_{0}}F^{\prime}
of dimension
\leqslant\frac{\dim F+\dim F^{\prime}}{2}-1
. Therefore

\displaystyle\operatorname{ev}(\gamma_{L}\cdot[\overline{M}_{L}]^{\mathrm{vir}})\in H_{\leqslant\dim F+\dim F^{\prime}-2}(F\times_{X_{0}}F^{\prime})\otimes\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{A}}(\operatorname{pt}).

Similarly,

\displaystyle\operatorname{ev}(\gamma_{R}\cdot[\overline{M}_{R}]^{\mathrm{vir}})\in H_{\leqslant\dim F^{\prime}+\dim F-2}(F^{\prime}\times_{X_{0}}F)\otimes\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{A}}(\operatorname{pt}).

After taking convolution, we have

\displaystyle e^{\mathsf{A}}\left(N_{F/X}^{+}\right)\operatorname{ev}_{*}\left(\frac{[\overline{M}]^{\mathrm{vir}}}{e^{\mathsf{A}}\left(N^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\overline{M}/\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)}\right)}\right)e^{\mathsf{A}}\left(N_{F/X}^{-}\right)\in H_{\leqslant 2\dim F-4}(F\times_{X_{0}}F)\otimes\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{A}}(\operatorname{pt}).

The
\mathsf{A}
-degree in the above is negative (since the LHS has homological degree
2\dim F-2
as the LHS of (
5.8
)), and hence
vanishes when
\mathsf{a}\to\infty
.

Case (b).

In this situation,
\overline{M}
is of the shape

\displaystyle\overline{M}\cong\overline{M}_{0}\times_{F^{n}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\overline{M}_{i}\,,

where
\overline{M}_{i}
is the stable map moduli stack corresponding to
C_{i}
for
0\leqslant i\leqslant n
, and the maps
\overline{M}_{0}\to F^{n}
and
\overline{M}_{i}\to F
are evaluations at the nodes
p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}
.
Notice that the
\mathsf{A}
-action on
\overline{M}_{0}
is trivial as
f(C_{0})\subseteq F
.

The same splitting curve argument as in case (a) shows that the tangent complex of
\overline{M}
is related to the tangent complexes of
\overline{M}_{i}
by the exact triangle

(5.11)

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}_{\overline{M}}\to\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}\mathrm{pr}_{i}^{*}\mathbb{T}_{\overline{M}_{i}}\to\bigoplus_{j=1}^{n}\operatorname{ev}_{p_{j}}^{*}\mathbb{T}_{F}\to\mathbb{T}_{\overline{M}}[1],

where

\mathrm{pr}_{i}
is the projection from
\overline{M}
to
\overline{M}_{i}
. The virtual normal contribution
N^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\overline{M}/\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)}
breaks into

(5.12)

\displaystyle N^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\overline{M}/\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)}\big|_{[f\colon C\to X]}=\sum_{i=0}^{n}N_{i}\big|_{[f|_{C_{i}}]}-\sum_{j=1}^{n}T_{X}|_{f(p_{j})}^{\mathrm{moving}}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}T_{C_{0},p_{j}}\otimes T_{C_{j},p_{j}}\,,

where
N_{i}\big|_{[f|_{C_{i}}]}=H^{*}(C_{i},f^{*}T_{X})^{\mathrm{moving}}
. Let
\mathsf{a}_{i}
denote the
\mathsf{A}
-weight of
T_{C_{j},p_{j}}
.

It follows from (
5.11
) and (
5.12
) that
the contribution of
\overline{M}
is of form

(5.13)

\begin{split}&\quad\,\,e^{\mathsf{A}}\left(N_{F/X}^{+}\right)\operatorname{ev}_{*}\left(\frac{[\overline{M}]^{\mathrm{vir}}}{e^{\mathsf{A}}\left(N^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\overline{M}/\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)}\right)}\right)e^{\mathsf{A}}\left(N_{F/X}^{-}\right)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}\\
s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{pr}_{*}\Delta_{F^{n}}^{*}\left(\operatorname{ev}_{*}(\psi_{1}^{r_{1}}\cdots\psi_{n}^{r_{n}}\alpha_{0})\boxtimes\left(\boxtimes_{i=1}^{n}e^{\mathsf{A}}(N_{F/X})\cdot\operatorname{ev}_{i*}(\psi_{i}^{s_{i}}\cdot[\overline{M}_{i}]^{\mathrm{vir}})\right)\right)\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathsf{a}_{i}^{-r_{i}-s_{i}-1}\binom{r_{i}+s_{i}}{r_{i}},\end{split}

where
\Delta_{F^{n}}\colon F\times F\times F^{n}\hookrightarrow F\times F\times F^{n}\times F^{n}
is given by the diagonal embedding
F^{n}\hookrightarrow F^{n}\times F^{n}
,
\operatorname{pr}\colon F\times F\times F^{n}\to F\times F
is the projection to the first and second factor,

\operatorname{ev}=\operatorname{ev}_{v_{1}}\times\operatorname{ev}_{v_{2}}\times\prod_{i=1}^{n}\operatorname{ev}_{i}\colon\overline{M}_{0}\to F\times_{X_{0}}F\times_{X_{0}}\cdots\times_{X_{0}}F,\quad\operatorname{ev}_{i}\colon\overline{M}_{i}\to F

are evaluation maps,

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}=\frac{e^{\mathsf{A}}\left(\operatorname{ev}_{v_{1}}^{*}N_{F/X}^{+}\right)e^{\mathsf{A}}\left(\operatorname{ev}_{v_{2}}^{*}N_{F/X}^{-}\right)}{e^{\mathsf{A}}\left(N_{0}\right)}[\overline{M}_{0}]^{\mathrm{vir}}\in H_{*}(\overline{M}_{0})\otimes\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{A}}(\operatorname{pt}),

and
\psi_{i}
is the
\psi
-class corresponding to
p_{i}
.

Notice that

\displaystyle\operatorname{ev}_{*}(\psi_{1}^{r_{1}}\cdots\psi_{n}^{r_{n}}\alpha_{0})\in H_{\leqslant 2\cdot\mathrm{vdim}\overline{M}_{0}}(\underbrace{F\times_{X_{0}}F\times_{X_{0}}\cdots\times_{X_{0}}F}_{n+2\text{ copies}})\otimes\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{A}}(\operatorname{pt}).

On the other hand, the image of
\operatorname{ev}_{i}\colon\overline{M}_{i}\to F
is contained in the closed subset
F\cap\pi^{-1}(B\setminus U)
(notation is the same as in case (a)), because the restriction of
\pi\colon X\to B
to
\pi^{-1}(U)
is an affine morphism therefore the image of any stable map
f\colon C\to X
is in
\pi^{-1}(B\setminus U)
. Therefore,

\displaystyle e^{\mathsf{A}}(N_{F/X})\cdot\operatorname{ev}_{i*}(\psi_{i}^{s_{i}}\cdot[\overline{M}_{i}]^{\mathrm{vir}})\in H_{\leqslant 2(\dim F-1)}(F\cap\pi^{-1}(B\setminus U))\otimes\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{A}}(\operatorname{pt}).

We claim that the image of
H_{2(\dim F-1)}(F\cap\pi^{-1}(B\setminus U))
in
H_{2(\dim F-1)}(F)
is zero. Since
F
is a symmetric quiver variety, there is a flat morphism
\pi^{\prime}\colon F\to B^{\prime}
to an affine space
B^{\prime}
with irreducible fibers by
[
COZZ2
, Thm. 1.1]
. By construction in
[
COZZ2
]
, there is a finite morphism
p\colon B^{\prime}\to B

6
6

6

B=\prod_{j}\mathfrak{gl}_{n_{j}}/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}_{n_{j}}
,
B^{\prime}=\prod_{j}\mathfrak{gl}_{n^{\prime}_{j}}/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}_{n^{\prime}_{j}}\times\mathfrak{gl}_{n_{j}^{\prime\prime}}/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}_{n_{j}^{\prime\prime}}
with
n_{j}^{\prime}+n_{j}^{\prime\prime}=n_{j}
. The map
p\colon B^{\prime}\to B
is induced by the diagonal embedding
\mathfrak{gl}_{n_{j}^{\prime}}\times\mathfrak{gl}_{n_{j}^{\prime\prime}}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{gl}_{n_{j}}
.
such that
p\circ\pi^{\prime}=\pi|_{F}
. Let
U^{\prime}:=p^{-1}(U)
, and let
C_{1},C_{2},\ldots
be codimension one (in
B^{\prime}
) irreducible components of
B^{\prime}\setminus U^{\prime}
. Since
\pi^{\prime}
is flat with irreducible fibers, codimension one
(in
F
) irreducible components of
F\cap\pi^{\prime-1}(B^{\prime}\setminus U^{\prime})
are exactly
\pi^{\prime-1}(C_{1}),\pi^{\prime-1}(C_{2}),\ldots
. Thus
H_{2(\dim F-1)}(F\cap\pi^{-1}(B\setminus U))
is spanned by
[\pi^{\prime-1}(C_{1})]=\pi^{\prime*}[C_{1}],[\pi^{\prime-1}(C_{2})]=\pi^{\prime*}[C_{2}],\ldots
. Since
B^{\prime}
is an affine space,
H^{2}(B^{\prime})=0
, so
[C_{1}]=[C_{2}]=\cdots=0
, and the claim follows. Then we have

\displaystyle e^{\mathsf{A}}(N_{F/X})\cdot\operatorname{ev}_{i*}(\psi_{i}^{s_{i}}\cdot[\overline{M}_{i}]^{\mathrm{vir}})\in H_{<2(\dim F-1)}(F)\otimes\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{A}}(\operatorname{pt}).

After taking convolution, we see that

\displaystyle\text{LHS of \eqref{split into factor}}\in H_{<2(\mathrm{vdim}\overline{M}_{0}-n)}(F\times_{X_{0}}F)\otimes\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{A}}(\operatorname{pt}).

Since
\mathrm{vdim}\overline{M}_{0}=\dim F+n-1
and the homological degree of LHS of (
5.13
) is
2\dim F-2
, the
\mathsf{A}
-degree of LHS of (
5.13
) is negative, and hence vanishes when
\mathsf{a}\to\infty
.
∎

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
\overline{M}_{\delta}\subseteq\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)^{\mathsf{A}}
be a connected component
whose elements consist of curves for which both marked points lie
on a contracted component.
Then it contributes zero to the left hand side of (
5.8
).

Proof.

A stable map in
\overline{M}_{\delta}
is of form
f\colon C=C_{0}\cup C_{1}\cup\cdots\cup C_{n}\to X
, where two marked points lie in
C_{0}
which is contracted to a point in a connected component
F\subseteq X^{\mathsf{A}}
. Here
F
is also a symmetric quiver variety. Then

\operatorname{ev}(\overline{M}_{\delta})\subseteq\Delta_{F}.

As mentioned above, there is a flat map
\pi\colon F\to B
to an affine space such that generic fibers are affine, and

\mathsf{A}
-action on the base is trivial. Hence
there is a closed subscheme
S\subsetneq B
such that

\operatorname{ev}(\overline{M}_{\delta})\subseteq\Delta_{\pi^{-1}(S)}.

Therefore, we have

(5.14)

\dim(\operatorname{ev}(\overline{M}_{\delta}))\leqslant\dim F-1.

By virtual localization
[
GP
]
with respect to
\mathsf{A}
,

\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{-1}\,\mathrm{Q}_{\beta}\,\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}
receives contribution from component
F
by

(5.15)

e^{\mathsf{A}}\left(N_{F/X}^{+}\right)\operatorname{ev}_{*}\left(\frac{[\overline{M}_{\delta}]^{\mathrm{vir}}}{e^{\mathsf{A}}\left(N^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\overline{M}_{\delta}/\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)}\right)}\right)e^{\mathsf{A}}\left(N_{F/X}^{-}\right).

We may expand the middle term
\operatorname{ev}_{*}(-)
into a sum

\sum_{i}a_{i}[C_{i}],

where
a_{i}
’s are equivariant parameters and
[C_{i}]
’s are cycles in
\operatorname{ev}(\overline{M}_{\delta})
.

•

If the power of
a_{i}
is
>-(\dim X-\dim F)
, then
\dim C_{i}>\dim F-1
, making
[C_{i}]
to be zero by (
5.14
).

•

If the power of
a_{i}
is
=-(\dim X-\dim F)
, then
\dim C_{i}=\dim F-1
, so
[C_{i}]
is a linear combination of
(\dim F-1)
-dimensional
cycles in
\Delta_{\pi^{-1}(S)}
. Then
[C_{i}]=0
as
A_{n}(B)=0
for any
n\neq\dim B
.

•

If the power of
a_{i}
is
<-(\dim X-\dim F)=-\deg_{\mathsf{A}}(e^{\mathsf{A}}(N_{F/X}))
, by taking equivariant parameters to infinity,
we know it contributes zero to (
5.15
).

To sum up, we conclude that (
5.15
) vanishes in
H^{\mathsf{A}}(X\times_{X_{0}}X)
.
∎

We note also that the same idea in the proof of Proposition
5.6
implies the following analog of
[
MO
, Prop. 7.5.1]
.

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
X=X_{1}\times X_{2}
be the product of two symmetric quiver varieties. Then

\mathrm{Q}_{X}=\mathrm{Q}_{X_{1}}\otimes 1+1\otimes\mathrm{Q}_{X_{2}}.

5.3.
Formula on symmetric quiver varieties

Given a symmetric quiver
Q
with potential
\mathsf{W}
preserved by a torus
\mathsf{T}_{0}
, recall that there is a Lie superalgebra
\mathfrak{g}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}

(Definition
4.22
) with a decomposition

\mathfrak{g}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{Q_{0}}}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}.

Let
\{e_{\alpha}^{(s)}\}
denote the basis in the root space
\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}
for each positive root
\alpha
that appearing in Proposition
4.23
.

By choosing a gauge dimension vector
\mathbf{v}
and a framing dimension vector
\mathbf{d}
, we have a symmetric quiver variety

X=\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}).

We take a
\mathsf{T}_{0}
action on
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
extending the
\mathsf{T}_{0}
action on the unframed quiver.
Let
\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}
act on
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
as a framing torus, and
\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}
be a
\mathsf{T}=\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}
invariant framed potential. Denote
\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}=\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}})
.
Then
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
(and hence
\mathfrak{g}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}
) acts on
H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})
.
For
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}^{Q_{0}}
, we define

c_{1}(\lambda)=\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}\lambda_{i}\>c_{1}(\mathsf{V}_{i}),

where
\mathsf{V}_{i}
is the
i
-th tautological bundle of
X
.

Definition 5.9
.

We define the
modified quantum multiplication

\gamma\,\widetilde{\star}\,\cdot
by substitution

\displaystyle z^{\beta}\mapsto(-1)^{(c_{1}(\mathsf{P}),\beta)+|\mathbf{v}|\cdot|\beta|}z^{\beta}\;\text{ in }\eqref{equ on Mgamma as map}\text{ for }\text{each}\,\,\beta\,\,\text{on}\,\,\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\,,

where
\mathsf{P}
is given by (
3.33
),
|\mathbf{v}|
is given by (
3.2
), and
|\beta|:=\sum_{i\in Q_{0}^{\mathrm{f}}}\beta_{i}\pmod{2}

is the parity of
\beta
with
\beta_{i}=(c_{1}(\mathsf{V}_{i}),\beta)
.

Theorem 5.10
.

Notations as above, then for the zero potential (
\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}=0
), we have

c_{1}(\lambda)\,\widetilde{\star}\,\cdot=c_{1}(\lambda)\cup\cdot-\sum_{\alpha>0}\alpha(\lambda)\frac{z^{\alpha}}{(-1)^{|\alpha|}-z^{\alpha}}\mathrm{Cas}_{\alpha}+\cdots\,,

where
\mathrm{Cas}_{\alpha}=\sum_{s}e_{\alpha}^{(s)}e_{-\alpha}^{(s)}
is the Casimir operator for
\mathfrak{g}_{Q,\mathsf{W}=0}
,
|\alpha|
is given as (
3.2
), and dots denote a scalar operator.

The scalar operator is fixed by the requirement that the purely quantum
part of
c_{1}(\lambda)\,\widetilde{\star}\,\cdot
annihilates the identity.

Proof.

This is parallel to the proof of
[
MO
, Thm. 10.2.1]
, which we sketch as follows.
By (
4.20
), we have

(5.16)

\Delta c_{1}(\lambda)=c_{1}(\lambda)\otimes 1+1\otimes c_{1}(\lambda)-\sum_{\alpha>0}(-1)^{|\alpha|}\alpha(\lambda)\sum_{s}\>e_{-\alpha}^{(s)}\otimes e_{\alpha}^{(s)}.

Combining with Remark
4.17
, we have

(5.17)

R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)\Delta c_{1}(\lambda)R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)^{-1}=\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}c_{1}(\lambda)=\Delta c_{1}(\lambda)+\sum_{\alpha>0}\alpha(\lambda)\sum_{s}\left((-1)^{|\alpha|}e_{-\alpha}^{(s)}\otimes e_{\alpha}^{(s)}-e_{\alpha}^{(s)}\otimes e_{-\alpha}^{(s)}\right).

We denote the modified quantum multiplication operator by

\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}(\lambda):=c_{1}(\lambda)+\sum_{\beta>0}(-1)^{(c_{1}(\mathsf{P}),\beta)+|\mathbf{v}|\cdot|\beta|}\lambda(\beta)\,\mathrm{Q}_{\beta}\,z^{\beta}.

This acts on critical cohomology for any
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
and any extended potential
\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}
.
We consider the pullback

(5.18)

\Delta\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}=\operatorname{Stab}_{+,\epsilon}^{-1}\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}\operatorname{Stab}_{+,\epsilon}

of
\mathrm{Q}
under the stable envelope map

\displaystyle H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime}))\otimes H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}))\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime}+\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime})).

Consider a decomposition

\Delta\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}=\sum_{\alpha}\Delta_{\alpha}\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}},

according to the weight of the Cartan action

[h\otimes 1,\Delta_{\alpha}\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}]=\alpha(h)\Delta_{\alpha}\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}.

By (
5.16
), Theorem
5.5
and Proposition
5.8
, we have
7
7

7

The modification
z^{\beta}\mapsto(-1)^{(c_{1}(\mathsf{P}),\beta)+|\mathbf{v}|\cdot|\beta|}z^{\beta}
is introduced to cancel the sign in (
5.7
).

(5.19)

\Delta_{0}\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}=\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}\otimes 1+1\otimes\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}+\text{a scalar operator}

By an analog of
[
MO
, Thm. 9.3.1]
, which uses only the symmetric property of quiver varieties,

\operatorname{Stab}_{+,\epsilon}\,(\tilde{z}^{\mathsf{v}}\otimes 1)\,\operatorname{Stab}_{-,\bar{\epsilon}}^{-1}
equals to the minuscule shift operator
\mathsf{S}(\sigma)
, where

\tilde{z}^{\mathsf{v}}
is the diagonal operator that acts on
H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})
by the scalar
(-1)^{|\mathbf{v}|}\cdot z^{\mathbf{v}}
. By a standard argument
[
MO
, Prop. 8.2.1, Lem. 8.3.1]
which holds for any smooth
X
with an action
\sigma\colon\mathbb{C}^{*}\to\operatorname{Aut}(X)
, shift operator
\mathsf{S}(\sigma)
commutes with modified quantum multiplications; therefore

[(\tilde{z}^{\mathsf{v}}\otimes 1)\,R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u),\Delta\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}]=0,\quad\mathrm{i.e.}\,\,\,R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)\,\Delta\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}\,R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)^{-1}=\sum_{\alpha}(-1)^{|\alpha|}z^{-\alpha}\Delta_{\alpha}\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}.

We also have

\displaystyle R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)\,\Delta\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}\,R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)^{-1}

\displaystyle=R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)\,\Delta c_{1}(\lambda)\,R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)^{-1}+\sum_{\beta>0}(-1)^{(c_{1}(\mathsf{P}),\beta)+|\mathbf{v}|\cdot|\beta|}\lambda(\beta)z^{\beta}\,R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)\,\Delta\mathrm{Q}_{\beta}\,R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}c_{1}(\lambda)+\sum_{\beta>0}(-1)^{(c_{1}(\mathsf{P}),\beta)+|\mathbf{v}|\cdot|\beta|}\lambda(\beta)z^{\beta}\,\Delta\mathrm{Q}_{\beta}

\displaystyle=\Delta\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}+(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}-\Delta)(c_{1}(\lambda)).

where in the second equality, we use (
5.17
), Lemma
5.3
, Remark
4.18
and (
5.18
).
This implies that

(5.20)

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}(1-(-1)^{|\alpha|}z^{-\alpha})\Delta_{\alpha}\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}=\Delta c_{1}(\lambda)-\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}c_{1}(\lambda)=\sum_{\alpha>0}\alpha(\lambda)\sum_{s}\left(e_{\alpha}^{(s)}\otimes e_{-\alpha}^{(s)}-(-1)^{|\alpha|}e_{-\alpha}^{(s)}\otimes e_{\alpha}^{(s)}\right),

where the last equality uses (
5.17
).
Equation (
5.20
) uniquely determines
\Delta_{\alpha}\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}
for
\alpha\neq 0
, given by

(5.21)

\Delta_{\alpha}\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}=\begin{cases}-\alpha(\lambda)\frac{z^{\alpha}}{(-1)^{|\alpha|}-z^{\alpha}}\sum_{s}e_{\alpha}^{(s)}\otimes e_{-\alpha}^{(s)},\quad\mathrm{if}\,\,\alpha>0\\
\alpha(\lambda)\frac{1}{(-1)^{|\alpha|}-z^{-\alpha}}\sum_{s}e_{\alpha}^{(s)}\otimes e_{-\alpha}^{(s)},\quad\mathrm{if}\,\,\alpha<0\end{cases}

We define

\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}_{\mathrm{remainder}}:=\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}-\left(c_{1}(\lambda)-\sum_{\alpha>0}\alpha(\lambda)\frac{z^{\alpha}}{(-1)^{|\alpha|}-z^{\alpha}}\sum_{s}e_{\alpha}^{(s)}e_{-\alpha}^{(s)}\right).

By (
5.16
), (
5.19
), (
5.21
),
we have

(5.22)

\Delta\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}_{\mathrm{remainder}}=\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}_{\mathrm{remainder}}\otimes 1+1\otimes\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}_{\mathrm{remainder}}+\text{a scalar operator}.

\Delta\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}_{\mathrm{remainder}}
preserves the weight space decomposition

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v}^{\prime\prime}}H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime}))\otimes H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}))\,,

so

\displaystyle\left[|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\otimes\operatorname{id}\,,\,\Delta\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}_{\mathrm{remainder}}\right]=0\,.

By Lemma
5.3
, Proposition
4.48
,
\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}_{\mathrm{remainder}}
is induced by a Steinberg correspondence, so by Remark
4.18
, we have

\displaystyle\left[R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}\,,\,\Delta\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}_{\mathrm{remainder}}\right]=0.

Combining the above two equations and (
5.22
), we get

\displaystyle\left[\operatorname{tr}_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}=0}_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime}}}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}\left((|\mathbf{0}\rangle\langle\mathbf{0}|\otimes\operatorname{id})R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}(u)\right)\,,\,\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}_{\mathrm{remainder}}\right]=0\,.

The computation in Lemma
4.11
shows that
\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}_{\mathrm{remainder}}
commutes with all classical multiplications, so
\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}_{\mathrm{remainder}}
itself is a classical multiplication. Since
\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}_{\mathrm{remainder}}
has cohomological degree
2
, it is of form
c_{1}(\mu)\cup\cdot+a\cdot
for some
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}^{Q_{0}}
and
a\in H^{2}_{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{pt})
. Since
\Delta_{\alpha}\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}_{\mathrm{remainder}}=0
for nonzero
\alpha
, we read from the coproduct formula (
4.20
) that
\mu
must be zero. Thus
\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}_{\mathrm{remainder}}
is a scalar multiplication.
∎

Corollary 5.11
.

Suppose that the specialization map
\mathrm{sp}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))
is injective, then the modified quantum multiplication by divisor
\lambda
on
H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})
is given by

c_{1}(\lambda)\,\widetilde{\star}\,\cdot=c_{1}(\lambda)\cup\cdot-\sum_{\alpha>0}\alpha(\lambda)\frac{z^{\alpha}}{(-1)^{|\alpha|}-z^{\alpha}}\mathrm{Cas}_{\alpha}+\cdots\,,

where
\mathrm{Cas}_{\alpha}=\sum_{s}e_{\alpha}^{(s)}e_{-\alpha}^{(s)}
is the Casimir operator for
\mathfrak{g}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}
, and dots denote a scalar operator.

Proof.

Since
\mathrm{Q}_{\beta}
is induced by a correspondence,
c_{1}(\lambda)\,\widetilde{\star}\,\cdot
commutes with specialization
[
COZZ1
, Prop. 7.17]
. By Proposition
4.48
, there exists a Steinberg correspondence
\mathsf{C}_{\alpha}
that induces the operator
\mathrm{Cas}_{\alpha}
for
H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})
and for
H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))
simultaneously. In particular, the restriction of Casimir operator for
\mathfrak{g}_{Q,0}
to the image of specialization map gives the Casimir operator for
\mathfrak{g}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}
. Then the corollary follows from Theorem
5.10
.
∎

5.4.
Asymmetric cases

For a not necessarily symmetric quiver variety, one may associate certain symmetrization of it and use Theorem
5.10
and the
following lemma to deduce the formula of quantum multiplication by divisors.

Proposition 5.12
.

Let
X
be a quiver variety (
2.2
) with affinization
X\to X_{0}
,
p\colon Y=\mathrm{Tot}_{X}(E)\to X
be the total space of a
\mathsf{T}
-equivariant
vector bundle
E
on
X
. Let
[Z]\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X,\mathsf{w}\boxminus\mathsf{w})_{X\times_{X_{0}}X}
and denote

[Z]_{X}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
to be the convolution induced by
[Z]
, and

[Z]_{Y}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,p^{*}\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,p^{*}\mathsf{w})
to be the convolution induced by the image of
[Z]
under the pushforward by the zero section

X\times X\to Y\times Y
.

Then we have a commutative diagram

(5.23)

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{*}\,\,\,}
\scriptstyle{\cong\hskip 8.19447pt}
\scriptstyle{[Z]_{X}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,p^{*}\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[Z]_{Y}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{*}(e(E)\cdot(-))\,\,\,}
\scriptstyle{\cong\hskip 8.19447pt}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,p^{*}\mathsf{w}),}

where
e(E)\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
is the Euler class operator, see e.g.
[
COZZ1
, Eqn. (2.10)]
.

Proof.

We have the following commutative diagrams

\textstyle{Y\times Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\operatorname{pr}}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\operatorname{pr}}_{1}}
\textstyle{X\times Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{Y\times X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\times X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\operatorname{pr}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{pr}_{1}}
\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{X}

where all squares are Cartesian. Denote
0\times 0\colon X\times X\to Y\times Y
to be the zero section.

By base change, for any
\alpha\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
, we have

((0\times 0)_{*}[F])\otimes(\widetilde{\operatorname{pr}}_{2}^{*}\,p^{*}\alpha)=(0\times 0)_{*}([F]\otimes\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\alpha).

Therefore, we have

\widetilde{\operatorname{pr}}_{1*}\left((0\times 0)_{*}[F]\otimes(\widetilde{\operatorname{pr}}_{2}^{*}\,p^{*}\alpha)\right)=\widetilde{\operatorname{pr}}_{1*}(0\times 0)_{*}([F]\otimes\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\alpha)=0_{*}\operatorname{pr}_{1*}([F]\otimes\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\alpha).

By the definition of convolution, the above is exactly

[F]_{Y}\circ(p^{*}\alpha)=0_{*}([F]_{X}\circ\alpha)\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,p^{*}\mathsf{w}).

Applying the Gysin pullback
0^{*}
, we obtain

0^{*}([F]_{Y}\circ(p^{*}\alpha))=e_{K}(E)\cdot([F]_{X}\circ\alpha)

Since
0^{*}
is the inverse of the smooth pullback
p^{*}
, we obtain the claim:

[F]_{Y}\circ(p^{*}\alpha)=p^{*}(e_{K}(E)\cdot([F]_{X}\circ\alpha)).\qed

Theorem 5.13
.

Take a state space
H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})
with
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leqslant 0}^{Q_{0}}
, where
\mathsf{T}=\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}
. Suppose that the specialization map
\mathrm{sp}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}))
is injective, then the modified quantum multiplication by divisor
\lambda
on
H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})
is given by

c_{1}(\lambda)\,\widetilde{\star}\,\cdot=c_{1}(\lambda)\cup\cdot-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha>0\\
\alpha\cdot\mu=0\end{subarray}}\alpha(\lambda)\frac{z^{\alpha}}{(-1)^{|\alpha|}-z^{\alpha}}\mathrm{Cas}_{\alpha,\mu}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha>0\\
\alpha\cdot\mu\neq 0\end{subarray}}(-1)^{|\alpha|}\alpha(\lambda)\,z^{\alpha}\,\mathrm{Cas}_{\alpha,\mu}+\cdots\,,

where
\mathrm{Cas}_{\alpha,\mu}
is the shifted Casimir operator in
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
, and dots denote a scalar operator.

Proof.

It is enough to prove the zero potential case and the nonzero potential follows from an argument similar to Corollary
5.11
(with Proposition
4.48
replaced by Proposition
4.49
).
Let
\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}
be a symmetric framing vector such that
\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{\operatorname{out}}=\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}
. Denote
X=\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
,
Y=\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})
, then
Y
is the total space of the vector bundle
E=\bigoplus_{i\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{Hom}(\mathsf{V}_{i},\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in},i}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}})
on
X
. Let the torus
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
act on
Y
by fixing the base
X
and scaling the fibers of
E
with weight
1
.

By the definition of torus equivariant virtual classes, we have

(5.24)

[\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)]_{\mathsf{T}}^{\mathrm{vir}}=[\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)]_{\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}^{\mathrm{vir}},

(5.25)

\displaystyle[\overline{M}_{0,2}(Y,\beta)]_{\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}^{\mathrm{vir}}

\displaystyle=i_{*}([\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)]_{\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}^{\mathrm{vir}}\cdot e(-\pi_{*}\operatorname{ev}^{*}E))

\displaystyle=i_{*}[\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)]_{\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}^{\mathrm{vir}}\cdot(\hbar^{-\operatorname{rk}E-\beta\cdot\mu}+\mathrm{l.o.t.}),

where
i\colon\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\overline{M}_{0,2}(Y,\beta)^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}
is induced by the zero section
X\hookrightarrow Y
,

\pi\colon\mathcal{C}\to\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)
is the universal curve and
\operatorname{ev}\colon\mathcal{C}\to X
is the evaluation map.
Denote

[Z]_{X}:=\operatorname{ev}_{*}([\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)]_{\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}^{\mathrm{vir}}),\,\,\,[Z]_{Y}:=i_{*}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{*}([\overline{M}_{0,2}(X,\beta)]_{\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}^{\mathrm{vir}}),\,\,\,[Z]_{Y}^{\prime}:=\operatorname{ev}_{*}([\overline{M}_{0,2}(Y,\beta)]_{\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}^{\mathrm{vir}}).

By Proposition
5.12
, we have

\displaystyle[Z]_{X}

\displaystyle=\frac{(p^{*})^{-1}\circ[Z]_{Y}\circ p^{*}(-)}{e(E)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(\hbar^{\operatorname{rk}E}+\mathrm{l.o.t.})(\hbar^{-\operatorname{rk}E-\beta\cdot\mu}+\mathrm{l.o.t.})}\left((p^{*})^{-1}\circ[Z]_{Y}^{\prime}\circ p^{*}(-)\right)

\displaystyle=-(-1)^{(c_{1}(\mathsf{P}_{Y}),\beta)+|\mathbf{v}|\cdot|\beta|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha>0\\
\alpha|\beta\end{subarray}}\frac{\alpha(\lambda)\,(-1)^{|\beta|}}{\hbar^{-\beta\cdot\mu}}\left((p^{*})^{-1}\circ\mathrm{Cas}_{\alpha,0}\circ p^{*}(-)\right)+\mathrm{l.o.t.}+\text{scalar operator}.

Here the second equality uses (
5.25
), the third uses Theorem
5.10
. By (
5.24
),
[Z]_{X}
is independent of the choice of
\hbar
, so we can take the limit
\hbar\to\infty
. Proposition
4.49
implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{\hbar\to\infty}\frac{1}{(-\hbar)^{-\beta\cdot\mu}}\left((p^{*})^{-1}\circ\mathrm{Cas}_{\alpha,0}\circ p^{*}(-)\right)=\begin{cases}\mathrm{Cas}_{\alpha,\mu}\,,&\text{if $\beta\cdot\mu=0$},\\
\mathrm{Cas}_{\alpha,\mu}\,,&\text{if $\beta\cdot\mu\neq 0$, $\alpha=\beta$},\\
\,\,\,\,\,0\,,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

where
\mathrm{Cas}_{\alpha,\mu}
is the shifted Casimir operator (see Definition
4.50
).
The result then follows.
∎

We apply Theorem
5.13
to compute the formula of quantum multiplication by divisors for
\operatorname{Hilb}(\mathbb{C}^{3})
in Section
10.4
.
Corresponding
K
-theoretic analog will be pursued in a future work
[
COZZ3
]
.

6.
Bilinear form and anti-automorphism

In this section, under certain properness assumption on the critical loci, we discuss an bilinear form on the shifted Yangian
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
modules, and the induced anti-automorphism on the shifted Yangian (Theorem
6.8
). These are geometric analogues of the Shapovalov form and the Chevalley anti-involution. We keep using notations from §
4
.

6.1.
Properness of torus fixed loci

Definition 6.1
.

We say that an auxiliary datum
\mathfrak{c}=(\mathbf{d},\mathsf{T}_{0}\curvearrowright\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}})
is
compact
if
\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}(\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}
is a proper variety for arbitrary
\mathbf{v}
. In this case, we also call the auxiliary space
\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}}

compact
.
An auxiliary data set
\mathcal{C}
is
compact
if every auxiliary datum in
\mathcal{C}
is compact. Similarly, a state space
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}=H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})
is
compact
if
\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})}(\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
is a proper variety for arbitrary
\mathbf{v}
.

The following lemma gives a criterion for abundant compact auxiliary data as well as state spaces.

Lemma 6.2
.

Suppose that
\left(\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\mathsf{w})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})\right)^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}
is proper (equivalent to finite), then
\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})}(\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
is proper if the following genericity condition is satisfied:

for every pair of
\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}
weights
\lambda
on
\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in}}
and
\lambda^{\prime}
on
\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{out}}
, the difference
\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}\notin\mathrm{Char}(\mathsf{T}_{0})
.

Proof.

\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
is the disjoint union of quiver varieties

\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{\phi/\sim}\mathcal{M}(Q^{\prime},\mathbf{v}_{\phi},\underline{\mathbf{d}}_{\phi}),

for a certain quiver
Q^{\prime}
which has a natural
\mathrm{Char}(\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}})
-grading on its nodes, and
\phi\colon\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\to\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})
is a group homomorphism. Since the stability is cyclic, nonempty
\mathcal{M}(Q^{\prime},\mathbf{v}_{\phi},\underline{\mathbf{d}}_{\phi})
must satisfy that the support of
\mathbf{v}_{\phi}
lies in the subspace
\left\{\lambda+\mathrm{Char}(\mathsf{T}_{0})\>|\>\lambda\text{ is a weight of $\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in}}$}\right\}
. Then the genericity condition implies that the tail of any out-going arrow on a nonempty
\mathcal{M}(Q^{\prime},\mathbf{v}_{\phi},\underline{\mathbf{d}}_{\phi})
must have gauge dimension zero. It follows that
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\subseteq\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}
where
\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}
,
\mathbf{d}^{\prime}_{\operatorname{out}}=\mathbf{0}
. From this we deduce that

\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})}(\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\subseteq\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})}(\mathsf{w})^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}.

Since the Jordan-Hölder map
\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})}(\mathsf{w})\to\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})}(\mathsf{w})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})
is projective, it is enough to show that the latter has finite
\mathsf{T}_{0}
fixed points. Note that
\mathbb{C}\left[\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})}(\mathsf{w})\right]^{\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}=\mathbb{C}\left[\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\mathsf{w})\right]^{\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}
because there is no out-going arrow to form cycle. Thus
\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})}(\mathsf{w})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\mathsf{w})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})
which has finite
\mathsf{T}_{0}
fixed points by assumption.
∎

Remark 6.3
.

Let us assume that
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-weights on the unframed quiver are integral, and we can take
\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}
weights on
\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in}}
,
\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{out}}
to be fractional, then the genericity condition in Lemma
6.2
is
\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Char}(\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\setminus\mathrm{Char}(\mathsf{T}_{0})
. In particular,
\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Char}(\mathsf{T}_{0})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\setminus\mathrm{Char}(\mathsf{T}_{0})
is allowed.

Example 6.4
.

Let
\widetilde{Q}
be the tripled quiver of
Q
,
\widetilde{\mathsf{W}}
be the canonical cubic potential,
\mathsf{T}_{0}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
be the torus that acts on
\widetilde{Q}
as in Example
2.4
. Denote
\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}=\operatorname{tr}(\widetilde{\mathsf{W}})
. Then
\left(\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\widetilde{\mathsf{w}})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})\right)^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}
consists of a unique point
0
corresponding to the trivial representation. In fact, by direct computation, we have
\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\widetilde{\mathsf{w}})=\{M\in\mathrm{Rep}(\Pi_{\overline{Q}},\mathbf{v}),\mathcal{E}\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathrm{Rep}(\Pi_{\overline{Q}},\mathbf{v})}(M)\}
, where
\Pi_{\overline{Q}}
is the preprojective algebra of
\overline{Q}
. Under the natural map

\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\widetilde{\mathsf{w}})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})\longrightarrow\left(\mathrm{Rep}(\Pi_{\overline{Q}},\mathbf{v})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})\right)\times\left(\mathfrak{gl}(\mathbf{v})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})\right)

the
\mathsf{T}_{0}
fixed locus of the left-hand-side maps to the
\mathsf{T}_{0}
fixed locus of the right-hand-side, which is
\{0\}\times\{0\}
. This implies that the preimage of
\left(\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\widetilde{\mathsf{w}})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})\right)^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}
under the quotient map is contained in the locus where
M
is a nilpotent
\Pi_{\overline{Q}}
-representation, and
\mathcal{E}
is an nilpotent endomorphism. Semisimplification of
\{M\text{ nilpotent},\mathcal{E}\text{ nilpotent}\}
as
\widetilde{Q}
-representation is the trivial one; thus
\left(\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\widetilde{\mathsf{w}})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})\right)^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}=\{0\}
.

6.2.
Duality and adjoint operators

For an algebraic variety
X
with a regular function
f\colon X\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
, we have Verdier duality

\displaystyle H^{i}(X,\varphi_{f}\omega_{X})^{\vee}\cong H^{-i}_{c}(X,\varphi_{f}\mathbb{C}_{X}).

If
X
is smooth and
\mathrm{Crit}(f)
is proper, then the RHS of the above isomorphism is isomorphic to
H^{-2d-i}(X,\varphi_{f}\omega_{X})
for
d=\dim X
, and the duality is induced by the nondegenerate pairing:

(6.1)

\displaystyle(\cdot,\cdot)_{X}:H(X,f)\otimes H(X,f)\cong H(X\times X,f\boxminus f)\xrightarrow{\Delta^{!}}H^{\mathrm{BM}}(X)_{\mathrm{Crit}(f)}\to\mathbb{C}\>.

Here the first isomorphism uses
H^{*}(X,f)\cong H^{*}(X,-f)
and Thom-Sebastiani isomorphism, the second map is Gysin pullback to diagonal, and the third map is pushforward to the point.

Consider a pair of smooth varieties
X
and
Y
with functions
f_{X}
and
f_{Y}
, and assume that both
\mathrm{Crit}(f_{X})
and
\mathrm{Crit}(f_{Y})
are proper. A class
\alpha\in H(X\times Y,f_{X}\boxminus f_{Y})
induces a convolution map
[
COZZ1
, (3.3)]
:

\alpha\colon H(Y,f_{Y})\to H(X,f_{X}).

Using the isomorphism
H(X\times Y,f_{X}\boxminus f_{Y})\cong H(Y\times X,f_{Y}\boxminus f_{X})
, the transpose of
\alpha
induces a convolution map
\alpha^{\mathrm{t}}\colon H(X,f_{X})\to H(Y,f_{Y})
.
\alpha
and
\alpha^{\mathrm{t}}
are adjoint to each other in the sense that

\displaystyle(\alpha(u),v)_{X}=(u,\alpha^{\mathrm{t}}(v))_{Y},\text{ for }u\in H(Y,f_{Y}),\>v\in H(X,f_{X}).

Here
(\cdot,\cdot)_{X}
and
(\cdot,\cdot)_{Y}
are the pairing (
6.1
) on
X
and on
Y
respectively.

When
X
has a torus
\mathsf{T}
action, we can replace the properness by equivariant properness, that is, if
X
is smooth and
\mathrm{Crit}(f)^{\mathsf{T}}
is proper, then we have nondegenerate pairing:

(6.2)

\displaystyle(\cdot,\cdot)\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,f)_{\mathrm{loc}}\otimes H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,f)_{\mathrm{loc}}\cong H^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X,f\boxminus f)_{\mathrm{loc}}\xrightarrow{\Delta^{!}}H^{\mathrm{BM}}(X)_{\mathrm{Crit}(f),\mathrm{loc}}\to\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t})=\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{pt}).

Here “loc” means
-\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}]}\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t})
. The discussions on transpose correspondence and adjoint operators naturally generalizes to the equivariant setting.

For our purpose, we introduce the following sign rule for pairing (
6.2
) on cohomologies
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
.

Definition 6.5
.

Consider the quiver variety
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
with
\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}
action and invariant potential
\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}
.
Suppose that
\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
is proper, we define the
bilinear form

on
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathbf{v})=H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})
:

(6.3)

(\cdot,\cdot)\colon\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathbf{v})\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathbf{v})\to\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt})

to be

\displaystyle(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}\mathsf{P}}\times\eqref{pairing on eq crit coh}\text{ for $X=\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})$, $\mathsf{T}=\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}$, $f=\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}$}.

Here
\mathsf{P}
is the virtual vector bundle (
3.33
) on
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
.

For a homomorphism
M\colon\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathbf{v})\to\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})
, we denote its
\mathbb{Z}/2
-graded (super) adjoint operator

(6.4)

M^{\tau}\colon\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})\to\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathbf{v})

with respect to the above pairing by

\displaystyle(Mx,y)=(-1)^{|M|\cdot|\mathbf{v}|}(x,M^{\tau}y),\;\text{where }x\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathbf{v}),\;y\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime}).

Note that
M^{\tau\tau}=(-1)^{|M|}M
.

For the tensor product of cohomologies, we invoke the super vector space sign rule:

\displaystyle(a\otimes b,c\otimes d)_{\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}=(-1)^{|b|\cdot|c|}(a,c)_{\mathcal{H}}\cdot(b,d)_{\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}\>.

Proposition 6.6

(Anti-automorphism for
\mathcal{D}\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
)
.

For any
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}^{Q_{0}}
, the following assignment for the generators
e_{i}(z),f_{i}(z),h_{i}(z)
in Definition
3.6
:

(6.5)

\displaystyle e_{i}(z)\mapsto(-1)^{|i|}f_{i}(z),\quad f_{i}(z)\mapsto e_{i}(z),\quad h_{i}(z)\mapsto h_{i}(z)

induces an anti-automorphism

(6.6)

\vartheta\colon\mathcal{D}\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})\to\mathcal{D}\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W}),

that is,
\vartheta
is a
\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]
-module automorphism such that

\vartheta(XY)=(-1)^{|X|\cdot|Y|}\vartheta(Y)\vartheta(X),\,\,\,\mathrm{where}\,\,\,X,Y\in\mathcal{D}\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W}).

Moreover, for any compact state space
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
with
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mu
, the bilinear form
(\cdot,\cdot)
on
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
is
\mathcal{D}\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-invariant, that is,

\displaystyle(a\cdot x,y)=(-1)^{|a|\cdot|x|}(x,\vartheta(a)\cdot y),\text{ for $a\in\mathcal{D}\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})$, $x,y\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}$}\>.

Proof.

The map
e_{i}(z)\mapsto(-1)^{|i|}f_{i}(z)
is an anti-isomorphism
\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}^{\mathrm{nil}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}^{\mathrm{nil},\mathrm{op}}
, and
f_{i}(z)\mapsto e_{i}(z)
is an anti-isomorphism from the opposite direction. Moreover, (
6.5
) respects the defining relations in Definition
3.6
; therefore it induces an anti-automorphism (
6.6
). Finally, the
\mathcal{D}\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-invariance follows by direct computations.
∎

6.3.
Anti-automorphisms for Reshetikhin type shifted Yangians

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}}
be an auxiliary space, and
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
be a state space with
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}^{Q_{0}}
. Assume that both
\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
are compact, then for any
m\in\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}_{\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}})[u]
, with respect to (
6.4
), we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}(m)^{\tau}=\mathsf{E}(m^{\tau}).

Proof.

According to the definition of root
R
-matrix and
[
COZZ1
, Lem. 3.29]
,

R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)_{F,F^{\prime}}=(-1)^{\mathrm{sgn}(F)}R(u)_{F,F^{\prime}}=(-1)^{\mathrm{sgn}(F)}(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}\mathsf{P}|_{F}^{-}}\left([\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}]^{\mathrm{t}}\circ[\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}]\right)_{F,F^{\prime}}(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}\mathsf{P}|_{F^{\prime}}^{+}},

where
(-1)^{\mathrm{sgn}(F)}=(-1)^{|\mathbf{v}|\cdot|\mathbf{v}^{\prime}|}
on the component
\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathbf{v})\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})
, which comes from the super vector space sign rule on the tensor product. It follows that

\displaystyle R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)^{\tau}_{F^{\prime},F}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\mathrm{sgn}(F^{\prime})}(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}\mathsf{P}|_{F^{\prime}}^{\text{fixed}}}(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}\mathsf{P}|_{F^{\prime}}^{+}}\left([\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}]^{\mathrm{t}}\circ[\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}]\right)_{F^{\prime},F}(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}\mathsf{P}|_{F}^{-}}(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}\mathsf{P}|_{F}^{\text{fixed}}}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\mathrm{sgn}(F^{\prime})}(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}\mathsf{P}|_{F^{\prime}}^{-}}\left([\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}]^{\mathrm{t}}\circ[\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}]\right)_{F^{\prime},F}(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}\mathsf{P}|_{F}^{+}}

\displaystyle=R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)_{F^{\prime},F}\>.

Therefore

\displaystyle\operatorname{Str}\left(m\cdot R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)\right)^{\tau}=\operatorname{Str}\left(m^{\tau}\cdot R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)^{\tau}\right)=\operatorname{Str}\left(m^{\tau}\cdot R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)\right),

and the lemma follows.
∎

Theorem 6.8

(Anti-automorphism for
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
)
.

Let
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leqslant 0}^{Q_{0}}
. Assume that there exists a set of compact and admissible auxiliary data
\mathcal{C}
. If
\mu\neq 0
, we assume moreover that there exists a compact state space with
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mu
. Then the map

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}(m)\mapsto\mathsf{E}(m^{\tau}),\;\text{for }m\in\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}_{\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}})[u],\;\mathfrak{c}\in\mathcal{C}

gives rise to an anti-automorphism

\varsigma\colon\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})\cong\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W}).

Moreover, the bilinear form
(\cdot,\cdot)
(
6.3
) on any compact state space
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
with
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mu
is
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-invariant.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.44
,
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})\cong\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})
. By Proposition
4.39
, the natural map

(6.7)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W},\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\prod_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{n}}\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}_{\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})[a_{0},a_{i_{1}},\ldots,a_{i_{n}}]}\left(\mathcal{H}_{0}[a_{0}]\otimes\mathcal{H}_{i_{1}}[a_{i_{1}}]\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{H}_{i_{n}}[a_{i_{n}}]\right)

is injective, where
\mathcal{H}_{0}
is a compact state space with shift
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mu
, and
\left\{\mathcal{H}_{i}:=\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}_{i}}_{\underline{\delta_{i}}}\right\}_{i\in Q_{0}}
is a collection of compact admissible auxiliary spaces (regarded as state spaces). The image of the map (
6.7
) is invariant under taking adjoint operator, as
\mathsf{E}(m)^{\tau}=\mathsf{E}(m^{\tau})
by Lemma
6.7
. Since taking adjoint operator is an algebra anti-automorphism, the map
\mathsf{E}(m)\mapsto\mathsf{E}(m^{\tau})
gives an anti-automorphism

\varsigma\colon\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})\cong\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W}).

Let
(\cdot,\cdot)
be the bilinear form on a compact state space
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
with
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mu
, then

\displaystyle(\mathsf{E}(m)\cdot x,y)=(-1)^{|m|\cdot|x|}(x,\mathsf{E}(m)^{\tau}\cdot y){\mathrel{\to}^{\scriptscriptstyle\text{Lemma \ref{lem adj op}}}}(-1)^{|m|\cdot|x|}(x,\mathsf{E}(m^{\tau})\cdot y),

in other words,
(\cdot,\cdot)
is
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-invariant.
∎

Remark 6.9
.

If
\left(\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\mathsf{w})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})\right)^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}
is finite for all
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}^{Q_{0}}
, then Lemma
6.2
implies that there always exists a set of compact and admissible auxiliary data
\mathcal{C}
, and there exists a compact state space with
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mu
for arbitrary
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leqslant 0}^{Q_{0}}
. In particular, assumptions in Theorem
6.8
are satisfied in this case.

7.
Vacuum cogeneration and irreducibility

In this section, we discuss two key properties of shifted Yangian
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
modules: vacuum cogeneration (Theorem
7.3
) and irreducibility (Proposition
7.6
). We keep using the same notations from §
4
.

7.1.
Vacuum cogeneration

Definition 7.1
.

For an algebra
A
defined over a field
\mathbb{K}
, we say that an
A
-module
M
is
cogenerated
by a vector
v\in M^{\vee}
if the map

(7.1)

\displaystyle A\cdot v\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{K}}(M,\mathbb{K})

given by precomposing with the action of
A
, is surjective. In particular, if
v
is the projection to the vacuum vector (identified with ground field
\mathbb{K}
) in
M=\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
, and
M
is cogenerated by
v
, we say that
M
is
cogenerated by vacuum
.

Remark 7.2
.

To show the surjectivity of (
7.1
), one is left to show for any
m\in M
, there exists
a\in A
such that
v(a\cdot m)\neq 0
.

Theorem 7.3
.

Let
\underline{\mathbf{d}}
be a framing such that
\mu:=\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leqslant 0}^{Q_{0}}
. If the following two conditions are satisfied:

(i)

the elements
\{\mathrm{ch}_{k}(\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in},i})\}_{i\in Q_{0},k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 1}}\subseteq H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt})
8
8

8

Because the framing bundle
\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in},i}
is trivial, it is pulled back from a point. So we treat their Chern characters as classes on a point.
can be generated from
\{\mathrm{ch}_{k}(\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in},i}-\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{out},i})\}_{i\in Q_{0},k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 1}}
over the base field
\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\operatorname{pt})
,

(ii)

one of the following specialization maps (as constructed in
[
COZZ1
, Ex. 7.19]
):

\mathsf{sp}_{\mathcal{M}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}})),\quad\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{M}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathfrak{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathfrak{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}}))

is injective after tensoring
\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt})
over the base ring
H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt})
,

then
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
is cogenerated by vacuum as a
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}^{\leqslant}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-module over the base field
\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt})
.

Proof.

Let us fix a gauge dimension
\mathbf{v}
, and consider the following auxiliary datum:
(\mathbf{v},\mathsf{T}_{0}\curvearrowright\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v}),\mathsf{w})
such that
\mathsf{T}_{0}
on out-going framing at node
i
with a nontrivial weight
t_{i}
, and the potential is given by the unframed potential
\mathsf{w}
. Take
G^{\prime}=\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})
and let it act on the (both in-coming and out-going) framing vector spaces
\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}}
by fundamental representation, and pick a maximal torus of
G^{\prime}
to be
T^{\prime}\cong\prod_{i\in Q_{o}}(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{\mathbf{v}_{i}}
. Consider the stable envelope followed by restriction to
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{0},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\bcancel{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{v})}\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}\boxplus\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}\right)\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})\times\bcancel{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{0},\underline{\mathbf{d}})},\mathsf{w}\boxplus\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}\right)

where the canceled spaces are points. In taking the stable envelope, we use the positive chamber for the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
framing torus that scales the framings of the auxiliary space with weight
-1
and fixes the framings of the state space. Let

\operatorname{id}\otimes 1\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\left(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}\right)\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}\right)

be the extension by scalar map, where
1\in H_{G^{\prime}}(\operatorname{pt})
is the unit. Consider the operator

(7.2)

\begin{gathered}e^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times G^{\prime}}\left(N^{-}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{0},\underline{\mathbf{d}})}\right)^{-1}\cdot\operatorname{Stab}\circ(\operatorname{id}\otimes 1)\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\left(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}\right)\\
\longrightarrow H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}),\mathsf{w}\right)\otimes_{H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times G^{\prime}}(\operatorname{pt})}\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times G^{\prime}}(\operatorname{pt}),\end{gathered}

which is a matrix element of the operator
F(u)^{-1}
in the Gauss decomposition (
4.6
) up to a sign. Composing (
7.2
) with any
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\operatorname{pt}\right)
-linear functional

H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}),\mathsf{w}\right)\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\operatorname{pt}\right),

and taking a coefficient in the spectral parameter
u\in H_{G^{\prime}}(\operatorname{pt})
as
u\to\infty
in some direction (all directions are equivalent by the Weyl group symmetry), the result gives a map

H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\left(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}\right)\to\operatorname{Frac}H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\left(\operatorname{pt}\right),

which lives in the algebra
\mathsf{Y}^{-}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
. Write

N^{-}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{0},\underline{\mathbf{d}})}=\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{Hom}(\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in},i},\mathsf{V}_{i}^{\prime}),

where
\mathsf{V}_{i}^{\prime}
is the tautological vector bundle on
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})
, then by the condition (i), in terms of spectral parameter
u\in H_{G^{\prime}}(\operatorname{pt})
, coefficients of
e^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times G^{\prime}}\left(N^{-}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{0},\underline{\mathbf{d}})}\right)
are given by elements in the algebra
\mathsf{Y}^{0}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
.

We conclude that the composition

(7.3)

\displaystyle f\circ\operatorname{Stab}\circ(\operatorname{id}\otimes 1)\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\left(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}\right)\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\operatorname{pt}\right)

for a linear functional
f\in\operatorname{Hom}_{H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\operatorname{pt}\right)}\left(H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}),\mathsf{w}\right),H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\operatorname{pt}\right)\right)
, followed by taking a coefficient in the spectral parameter
u\in H_{G^{\prime}}(\operatorname{pt})
as
u\to\infty
in some direction, gives a map

\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathbf{v})=H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\left(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}\right)\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\left(\operatorname{pt}\right)\cong\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathbf{0}),

which lives in the algebra
\mathsf{Y}^{\leqslant}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
. To prove the theorem, it is enough to show that
u
-coefficients of (
7.3
) cogenerates
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathbf{v})
for some
f
.

Consider the following choice of
f
. Let
j\colon\mathring{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})
be the natural inclusion of the open locus
\mathring{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})
consisting of those framed quiver representations such that in-coming framings are isomorphisms. Note that there is a
G^{\prime}
-equivariant isomorphism

\mathring{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})\cong R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})\times\prod_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathfrak{gl}(\mathbf{v}_{i}),

where
G^{\prime}
acts on
R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})
as gauge group and acts on
\prod_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathfrak{gl}(\mathbf{v}_{i})
as adjoint representation. In particular,

H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\mathring{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})\right)\cong H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times G^{\prime}}(\operatorname{pt}).

Consider specialization map
[
COZZ1
, Ex. 7.19]
:

\mathsf{sp}_{\mathbf{v}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\mathring{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}),\mathsf{w}\right)\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\mathring{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})\right)\cong H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times G^{\prime}}(\operatorname{pt}).

We define the linear functional

\displaystyle f:=\mathsf{sp}_{\mathbf{v}}\circ j^{*}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}),\mathsf{w}\right)\to H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times G^{\prime}}(\operatorname{pt}).

We claim that
u
-coefficients of (
7.3
) cogenerates
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathbf{v})
for the above
f
.

By comparison, we find that

\displaystyle f\circ\operatorname{Stab}\circ(\operatorname{id}\otimes 1)=\mathsf{sp}_{\mathbf{v}}\circ i^{*}\circ e^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}t_{i}\cdot\mathfrak{gl}(\mathbf{v}_{i})\right)\cdot\mathbf{\Psi}_{H},

where
\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\left(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}\right)\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\left(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}}\right)
is the interpolation map
[
COZZ1
, Def. 9.1]
, and
i\colon\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
is the closed embedding induced by extension by trivial framing.

Since specialization commutes with Gysin pullback, multiplication by Euler classes, and stable envelope, we have

\displaystyle f\circ\operatorname{Stab}\circ(\operatorname{id}\otimes 1)=i^{*}\circ e^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}t_{i}\cdot\mathfrak{gl}(\mathbf{v}_{i})\right)\cdot\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{M}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}=i^{*}\circ e^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}t_{i}\cdot\mathfrak{gl}(\mathbf{v}_{i})\right)\cdot\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}\circ\mathsf{sp}_{\mathcal{M}}.

Note that
i^{*}
on the RHS of the above equation is actually an isomorphism:

i^{*}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\left(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\right)\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\left(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})\right)\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\operatorname{pt}\right).

According to
[
COZZ1
, Lem. 9.2]
,
\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}
is injective. Then condition (ii) implies that

\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}\circ\mathsf{sp}_{\mathcal{M}}=\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{M}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}

is injective after localization. So it suffices to show that for any nonzero
x\in H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\operatorname{pt}\right)
, there exists a nonzero
u
-coefficient of the expansion of
e^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}t_{i}\cdot\mathfrak{gl}(\mathbf{v}_{i})\right)\cdot x
in
u\to\infty
. This is equivalent to proving that

e^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}t_{i}\cdot\mathfrak{gl}(\mathbf{v}_{i})\right)\cdot x\neq 0,

which follows from the facts that
t_{i}
is nontrivial and
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times G^{\prime}}\left(\operatorname{pt}\right)
is an integral domain. We are done.
∎

Corollary 7.4
.

Let
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{d})=\bigsqcup_{\mathbf{v}}\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
be the Nakajima variety associated to a doubled quiver
\overline{Q}
, and let
\mathsf{S}
be a torus that acts on
\mathrm{Rep}_{Q}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
by flavour symmetry (i.e. commutes with
G=\prod_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v}_{i})
action), then
\mathsf{T}_{0}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathsf{S}
act on
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{d})
using the identification
T^{*}\mathrm{Rep}_{Q}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})=\mathrm{Rep}_{Q}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\oplus\hbar\>\mathrm{Rep}_{Q}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\vee}
. Assume that
\mathsf{S}
-fixed locus satisfies
\mathrm{Rep}_{Q}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{S}}\subset\mathrm{Rep}_{Q}(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{d})
where the latter is regarded as subspace of the former via extension by zero. Take a torus
\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}
that acts on the framing vector space, then
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{d}))
is cogenerated by vacuum as a
\mathsf{Y}_{0}^{\leqslant}(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{\mathsf{W}})
-module over the base field
\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt})
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})=\mathcal{M}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
be the symmetric quiver variety associated to the tripled quiver
\widetilde{Q}
, and let
\mathsf{T}_{0}
act on
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
by scaling the added loops with weight
\hbar^{-1}
. Denote the standard cubic potential by
\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}
.
By dimensional reduction, we have an isomorphism of
\mathsf{Y}_{0}^{\leqslant}(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{\mathsf{W}})
-modules:

H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{d}))\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{d}),\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}).

So it is enough to prove the vacuum cogeneration for the latter. We claim that

(i)

the elements
\{\mathrm{ch}_{k}(\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in},i})\}_{i\in Q_{0},k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 1}}\subseteq H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt})
can be generated from
\{\mathrm{ch}_{k}(\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in},i}-\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{out},i})\}_{i\in Q_{0},k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 1}}
over the base field
\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\operatorname{pt})
,

(ii)

the specialization maps
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{M}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathfrak{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathfrak{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}}))
is injective.

Claim (i) follows from the fact that
\mathrm{ch}(\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{out},i})=e^{\hbar}\mathrm{ch}(\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in},i})
. By
[
COZZ1
, Rem. 7.16]
, we have commutative diagram

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(Z(\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\widetilde{\mathsf{w}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})),}

where
Z(\widetilde{\mathsf{w}})
is the zero locus of
\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}
in
\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
. Note that the zero locus of the moment map
\mu\colon\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\to[\mathfrak{g}/G]
is a closed substack of
Z(\widetilde{\mathsf{w}})
, and the composition

\delta:=\operatorname{can}\circ\>(Z(\mu)\hookrightarrow Z(\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}))_{*}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(Z(\mu))\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\widetilde{\mathsf{w}})

is an isomorphism by dimensional reduction. Therefore, for
j\colon Z(\mu)\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
, we have

\mathsf{sp}=j_{*}\circ\delta^{-1}.

We claim that the pushforward map
j_{*}
is injective. Consider an additional torus
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a}
that scales the framing vector space by weight
1
and fixes other quiver data. This
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a}
action on
\mathrm{Rep}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
can be equivalently obtained by first mapping
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a}
diagonally into the center
\prod_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathbb{C}^{*}
of gauge group
\prod_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v}_{i})
and then taking the inverse. Then it follows that
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a}}(Z(\mu))
and
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))
are free
H_{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a}}(\operatorname{pt})
-modules, and we have

H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(Z(\mu))\otimes H_{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a}}(\operatorname{pt})\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a}}(Z(\mu)),\;H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))\otimes H_{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a}}(\operatorname{pt})\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})).

Then our choice of
\mathsf{S}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a}
action satisfies the genericity condition in
[
Dav3
, Thm. 10.2]
, which implies that

j_{*}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a}}(Z(\mu))\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))

is injective. Since
\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a}
equivariant pushforward is obtained from the
\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}
by extension by scalar,
j_{*}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(Z(\mu))\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))
must be injective as well. This proves Claim (ii). Then the corollary follows from the above two claims and Theorem
7.3
.
∎

Corollary 7.5
.

Under the same assumptions as Corollary
7.4
, and let
\phi=\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{m})
be a
(\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}})
-invariant potential on
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{d})
, the following statements are equivalent:

(1)

the specialization map
\mathsf{sp}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{d}),\phi)\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{d}))
in
[
COZZ1
, Ex.  7.19]
is injective after tensoring with
\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt})
over the base ring
H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\operatorname{pt})
,

(2)

H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{d}),\phi)
is cogenerated by vacuum as a
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{\mathsf{W}})
-module over the base field
\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt})
.

(3)

H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{d}),\phi)
is cogenerated by vacuum as a
\mathsf{Y}_{0}^{\leqslant}(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{\mathsf{W}})
-module over the base field
\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt})
.

Proof.

(3)\Longrightarrow(2)
is tautological.

(2)\Longrightarrow(1)
. Suppose
\ker(\mathsf{sp})
is nontrivial after localization, then

\ker(\mathsf{sp})_{\mathrm{loc}}\subseteq H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{d}),\phi)_{\mathrm{loc}}

is a
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{\mathsf{W}})
-submodule, since
\mathsf{sp}
commutes with stable envelopes and
R
-matrices. By construction, we have

\mathsf{sp}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{d}),\phi)\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{d})).

Denote
\mathbb{K}=\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt})
,
|0\rangle\in H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{d}),\phi)=H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt})
to be a generator. Then the map
