\mathsf{Y}_{0}(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{\mathsf{W}})\cdot\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{K}}(\mathbb{K}\cdot|0\rangle,\mathbb{K})\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{K}}(H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{d}),\phi)_{\mathrm{loc}},\mathbb{K})

factors through the proper subspace
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{K}}(\operatorname{im}(\mathsf{sp})_{\mathrm{loc}},\mathbb{K})
, which contradicts with (2). Thus
\ker(\mathsf{sp})_{\mathrm{loc}}
is trivial.

(1)\Longrightarrow(3)
.
\mathsf{sp}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{d}),\phi)\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{d}))
commutes with stable envelopes and is therefore a
\mathsf{Y}_{0}^{\leqslant}(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{\mathsf{W}})
-module map. By Corollary
7.4
,
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{d}))
is cogenerated by vacuum as a
\mathsf{Y}_{0}^{\leqslant}(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{\mathsf{W}})
-module over the base field
\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt})
, so is its submodule
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{d}),\phi)
.
∎

7.2.
Irreducibility

Proposition 7.6
.

Let
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leqslant 0}^{Q_{0}}
,
\underline{\mathbf{d}}=(\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}})
with
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mu
. Assume that there exists a set of compact and admissible auxiliary data
\mathcal{C}
. If the state space
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
is compact and cogenerated by vacuum over the base field
\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt})
, then
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
is an irreducible
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-module over the base field
\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt})
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
is compact, it has a nondegenerate bilinear form
(\cdot,\cdot)
(
6.3
), which is
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-invariant by Theorem
6.8
. By duality, cogeneration by vacuum implies that
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
is also generated by the vacuum vector
|\mathbf{0}\rangle
over the base field
\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt})
. Cogeneration by vacuum implies that for arbitrary
x\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
there exists
a\in\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})\otimes_{H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt})}\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt})
such that
a\cdot x=|\mathbf{0}\rangle
, so
x
generates
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
. Therefore
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
is an irreducible
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-module over the base field
\operatorname{Frac}H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt})
.
∎

8.
Example: trivial quiver and modules of shifted Yangians of
\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1}

In this section, we consider the simplest example: the trivial quiver with zero potential:

Q\colon\quad\hbox to17.12pt{\vbox to17.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 8.5584pt\lower-8.5584pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#FFFFFF} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#FFFFFF} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 11.57 0 C 11.57 6.39 6.39 11.57 0 11.57 C -6.39 11.57 -11.57 6.39 -11.57 0 C -11.57 -6.39 -6.39 -11.57 0 -11.57 C 6.39 -11.57 11.57 -6.39 11.57 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.00117pt}{-2.15277pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -4.15 -2.98)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\par
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\;,\quad\mathsf{W}=0\,.

Let
\mathsf{T}_{0}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
be the torus that acts on
Q
trivially (
\mathsf{T}_{0}
action on the framing vector spaces will be specified later). Fix a cyclic stability
\theta<0
when we consider associated quiver varieties.

The main theorem of this section is an
explicit
description of the shifted Yangian
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,0)
for
Q
(
4.5
).

Theorem 8.1
.

Let
Q
be the trivial quiver. For arbitrary
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leqslant 0}
, there is a natural algebra isomorphism

\displaystyle\varrho_{\mu}\colon Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)\cong\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,0)\>,

where
Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})
is the

\mu
-shifted Yangian of
\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1}
(Definition
8.2
).

In this section, we also perform
explicit
computations of cohomological stable envelopes and root
R
-matrices in §
8.1
and
§
8.2
. These computations supersede the results in
[
IMZ
, §7.2]
, but the method there is different from ours. We deduce the explicit formula for quantum multiplication by divisors on the framed quiver varieties (Proposition
8.11
). This in particular recovers the well-known result on
\operatorname{Gr}(n,r)
.

There are also quivers with potentials associated to
\mathfrak{gl}_{m|n}
, see Example
3.10
and Appendix §
A.2
. In Appendix §
B
, we compute some
R
-matrices for the quivers with potentials associated to
\mathfrak{gl}_{2|1}
.

8.1.
The fundamental
R
-matrices

Consider the framed quiver

2
A
B

and the corresponding symmetric quiver variety
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{2})
. Take a torus
\mathsf{T}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t_{1}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t_{2}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
such that
(s_{1},s_{2},a)\in\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t_{1}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t_{2}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
acts on the quiver data by

\displaystyle(A_{1},A_{2},B_{1},B_{2})\mapsto(a^{-1}A_{1},A_{2},s_{1}aB_{1},s_{2}B_{2}).

Let
\mathsf{A}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
, then
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{2})^{\mathsf{A}}=\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1})
. Note that

\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1})=\mathcal{M}(0,\mathbf{1})\sqcup\mathcal{M}(1,\mathbf{1}),\quad\mathcal{M}(0,\mathbf{1})=\operatorname{pt},\quad\mathcal{M}(1,\mathbf{1})\cong\mathbb{C}.

We take the set of fundamental classes of
\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1})
(
n=0,1
) as a basis of
H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1}))
, and denote them

\displaystyle b_{0}=[\mathcal{M}(0,\mathbf{1})],\quad b_{1}=[\mathcal{M}(1,\mathbf{1})].

8.1.1.
Computation of stable envelopes

Let us consider stable envelopes for
\mathcal{M}(1,\mathbf{2})
in both “
+
” and “
-
” chambers. Let the equivariant variable of the gauge group
\mathbb{C}^{*}
be
x
, i.e.
H_{\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\operatorname{pt})=\mathbb{C}[x]
. Using
[
COZZ1
, Cor. 4.25]
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{+}(b_{0}\otimes b_{1})=x-u,\quad\operatorname{Stab}_{+}(b_{1}\otimes b_{0})=t_{2}-x,\quad\operatorname{Stab}_{-}(b_{0}\otimes b_{1})=u+t_{1}-x,\quad\operatorname{Stab}_{-}(b_{1}\otimes b_{0})=x.

The matrix forms of stable envelopes in the basis
\{b_{0}\otimes b_{1},b_{1}\otimes b_{0}\}
are

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{+}=\begin{pmatrix}-u&t_{2}\\
0&t_{2}-u\end{pmatrix}\;,\qquad\operatorname{Stab}_{-}=\begin{pmatrix}u+t_{1}&0\\
t_{1}&u\end{pmatrix}\;.

8.1.2.
The R-matrices and YBE

The
R
-matrix for
\mathcal{M}(1,\mathbf{2})
is given by

\displaystyle R(u)\big|_{\mathcal{M}(1,\mathbf{2})}=\operatorname{Stab}_{-}^{-1}\operatorname{Stab}_{+}=\frac{1}{u+t_{1}}\begin{pmatrix}-u&t_{2}\\
t_{1}&t_{2}-t_{1}-u\end{pmatrix}\;.

The
R
-matrix for
\mathcal{M}(0,\mathbf{2})
and for
\mathcal{M}(2,\mathbf{2})
are

\displaystyle R(u)\big|_{\mathcal{M}(0,\mathbf{2})}=1,\quad R(u)\big|_{\mathcal{M}(2,\mathbf{2})}=\frac{t_{2}-u}{u+t_{1}}.

Taking the normalizer (
3.34
) and the sign twist
\Sigma
in Theorem
3.21
into account, the
R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)
matrix is given by

(8.1)

\displaystyle R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u,t_{1},t_{2})=\frac{1}{u+t_{1}}\begin{pmatrix}u+t_{1}&&&\\
&u&t_{2}&\\
&t_{1}&u+t_{1}-t_{2}&\\
&&&u-t_{2}\end{pmatrix}\;.

The Yang-Baxter-equation

\displaystyle R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{12}(u,t_{1},t_{2})R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{13}(u+v,t_{1},t_{3})R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{23}(v,t_{2},t_{3})=R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{23}(v,t_{2},t_{3})R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{13}(u+v,t_{1},t_{3})R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{12}(u,t_{1},t_{2})

holds in
\operatorname{End}(\mathbb{C}^{1|1}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{1|1}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{1|1})(u,v,t_{1},t_{2},t_{3})
, where
\mathbb{C}^{1|1}=\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{b_{0},b_{1}\}
with
b_{0}
even and
b_{1}
odd.

When
t_{1}=t_{2}=\hbar
, (
8.1
) can be written in a compact form:

(8.2)

\displaystyle R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u,\hbar)=\frac{u+\hbar P}{u+\hbar},\;\text{ where }\;P(b_{i}\otimes b_{j})=(-1)^{ij}b_{j}\otimes b_{i}\;\text{ is the super permutation operator}.

8.2.
Lax matrix associated to a Clifford module

Consider the framing dimension
(\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}})=(2,1)
, depicted as follows

1
1
A_{1}
B_{1}
A_{2}

and the corresponding quiver variety
\mathcal{M}((2,1))
. Take a torus
\mathsf{T}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
such that
(s,a)\in\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
acts on the quiver data by

\displaystyle(A_{1},A_{2},B)\mapsto(a^{-1}A_{1},A_{2},saB_{1}).

Let
\mathsf{A}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
, then
\mathcal{M}((2,1))^{\mathsf{A}}=\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1})\times\mathcal{M}((1,0))
. As in the previous subsection, we use
b_{n}
(resp.
c_{n}
) to denote the fundamental class of
\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1})
(resp.
\mathcal{M}(n,(1,0))
) for
n=0,1
, then
H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}((2,1))^{\mathsf{A}})
is a free
H_{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{pt})
-module with a basis
\{b_{i}\otimes c_{j}\>:\>0\leqslant i,j\leqslant 1\}
.

Let the equivariant variable of the gauge group
\mathbb{C}^{*}
be
x
. Using
[
COZZ1
, Cor. 4.25]
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{+}(b_{0}\otimes c_{1})=x-u,\quad\operatorname{Stab}_{+}(b_{1}\otimes c_{0})=1,\quad\operatorname{Stab}_{-}(b_{0}\otimes c_{1})=u+\hbar-x,\quad\operatorname{Stab}_{-}(b_{1}\otimes c_{0})=x,

with the matrix forms:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{+}=\begin{pmatrix}-u&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\;,\qquad\operatorname{Stab}_{-}=\begin{pmatrix}u+\hbar&0\\
\hbar&u\end{pmatrix}\;.

Let
L(u):=R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)
for
\mathcal{M}((2,1))
, then

\displaystyle L(u,\hbar)=\frac{1}{u+\hbar}\begin{pmatrix}u+\hbar&&&\\
&u&1&\\
&-\hbar&1&\\
&&&1\end{pmatrix}\;.

Let
\mathsf{Cl}
be the
\mathbb{C}
-algebra with two odd generators
\psi
and
\psi^{*}
, subject to relation
\psi\psi^{*}+\psi^{*}\psi=1
. Then
\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{c_{0},c_{1}\}
can be endowed with a
\mathsf{Cl}
-module structure by setting

\displaystyle\psi^{*}c_{0}=c_{1},\quad\psi c_{1}=c_{0}.

Let
\mathcal{F}
denote this module. Then
L(u,\hbar)
can be represented as an element in
\operatorname{End}(\mathbb{C}^{1|1})\otimes\mathsf{Cl}
:

\displaystyle L(u,\hbar)=\frac{1}{u+\hbar}\begin{pmatrix}u+\hbar\psi\psi^{*}&\psi^{*}\\
-\hbar\psi&1\end{pmatrix}\;,

and it satisfies the RLL equation:

\displaystyle R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{12}(u-v,\hbar)L_{1}(u,\hbar)L_{2}(v,\hbar)=L_{2}(v,\hbar)L_{1}(u,\hbar)R^{\mathrm{sup}}_{12}(u-v,\hbar)

when acting on
\mathbb{C}^{1|1}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{1|1}\otimes\mathcal{F}
.

8.3.
Shifted Yangian of
\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1}

Let

(8.3)

R(z)=z\operatorname{id}+\hbar P\in\operatorname{End}(\mathbb{C}^{1|1})^{\otimes 2}[z,\hbar].

Here
P(a\otimes b)=(-1)^{|a|\cdot|b|}b\otimes a
is the super permutation operator.

Definition 8.2
.

Fix
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leqslant 0}
. The
\mu
-
shifted Yangian

Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})
is a
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
algebra generated by

\left\{e_{r},f_{r},g_{1,r},g_{2,s}\right\}_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0},s\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant-\mu}},

subject to relations

(8.4)

\displaystyle R(u-v)\,T_{1}(u)\,T_{2}(v)=T_{2}(v)\,T_{1}(u)\,R(u-v),

with
T(z)\in Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})[\![z,z^{-1}]\!]\otimes\operatorname{End}(\mathbb{C}^{1|1})
defined via

\displaystyle T(z)=F(z)\,G(z)\,E(z),\,\,\,\text{where}\,\,\,E(z)=\operatorname{id}_{1|1}+e(z)\otimes E_{12},\,\,\,F(z)=\operatorname{id}_{1|1}+f(z)\otimes E_{21},\,\,\,G(z)=\sum_{i=1}^{2}g_{i}(z)\otimes E_{ii},

and

(8.5)

\displaystyle e(z)=\sum_{r\geqslant 0}e_{r}z^{-r-1},\quad f(z)=\sum_{r\geqslant 0}f_{r}z^{-r-1},\quad g_{1}(z)=1+\sum_{r\geqslant 0}g_{1,r}z^{-r-1},\quad g_{2}(z)=z^{\mu}+\sum_{s\geqslant-\mu}g_{2,s}z^{-s-1}\,.

Here (
8.4
) is an equation in
Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})[\![u,v,u^{-1},v^{-1}]\!]\otimes\operatorname{End}(\mathbb{C}^{1|1})^{\otimes 2}
.

Remark 8.3
.

The special case
\mu=0
gives the definition of unshifted Yangian
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})
in
[
G
]
. In the below, we also write
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})=Y_{0}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})
.

Let
\mathfrak{c}
be the auxiliary datum such that the framing dimension is one, potential is trivial, and
\mathsf{T}_{0}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
fixes the in-coming framing and scales the out-going framing with weight one. The auxiliary data set
\mathcal{C}=\{\mathfrak{c}\}
is admissible (Definition
4.1
); therefore

\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,0)=\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,0,\mathcal{C}).

By (
8.2
), the
R
-matrix that braids
\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}}
is the
R
-matrix in (
8.3
) up to a scalar constant. It follows that for any state space
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
with
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mu
, the
R
-matrix that braids
\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
, denoted
L(u)
, satisfies the equation

R(u-v)\,L_{1}(u)\,L_{2}(v)=L_{2}(v)\,L_{1}(u)\,R(u-v).

Since
L(u)
has the Gauss decomposition of the form (
3.35
), it induces a
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
-algebra homomorphism:

\displaystyle Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)\to\prod_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\operatorname{End}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}_{\mathbb{C}(\hbar)[\mathsf{a}^{\mathrm{fr}}]}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}),

given by coefficients of
L(u)
. By definition of
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,0,\mathcal{C})
, the above map induces a surjective
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
-algebra homomorphism

(8.6)

\displaystyle\varrho_{\mu}\colon Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)\twoheadrightarrow\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,0,\mathcal{C})=\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,0).

Lemma 8.4
.

The map
\varrho_{0}\colon Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)\to\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,0)
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

It remains to show that
\varrho_{0}
is injective.
Viewing
\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}}
as a state space, its
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})
module structure is given by

\displaystyle T(z)\mapsto\frac{z+\hbar P}{z+\hbar}.

As a
\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1}
module,
\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}}
is the fundamental representation, so
\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1}\to\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb{C}(\hbar)}^{\mathbb{Z}/2}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{c}})
is injective. In particular,
\varrho_{0}
maps
\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1}
injectively into
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,0)
. We note that
\varrho_{0}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})\subseteq\mathfrak{g}_{Q,0}
.

As in
[
G
, (1.6)]
,
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})
is a filtered algebra by setting
\deg X_{r}=r
for
X=e,f,g_{1},g_{2}
. The map
\varrho_{0}
preserves the filtrations:
\varrho_{0}(F_{k}Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar))\subseteq F_{k}\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,0)
, so it induces a map between the associated graded algebras:
\bar{\varrho}_{0}\colon\mathrm{gr}\,Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)\to\mathrm{gr}\,\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,0)
. By the PBW theorem for
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})

[
G
, Cor. 2.1]
, there is a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{gr}\,Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)\cong\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1}[u]).

By Theorem
4.26
, we have a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{gr}\,\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,0)\cong\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{Q,0}[u]).

Moreover, the induced map
\bar{\varrho}_{0}\colon\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1}[u])\to\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{Q,0}[u])
is obtained by joining the polynomial variable
u
to the natural map
\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1}\to\mathfrak{g}_{Q,0}
followed by taking enveloping algebras. As we have shown,
\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1}\to\mathfrak{g}_{Q,0}
is injective, so
\bar{\varrho}_{0}\colon\mathrm{gr}\,Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)\to\mathrm{gr}\,\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,0)
is also injective. Hence
\varrho_{0}
is injective.
∎

Remark 8.5
.

By Theorem
3.21
, the generating series of
Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})
generators are mapped to operators acting on
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
as follows:

\displaystyle g_{1}(z)\mapsto c_{-1/z}((1-\hbar^{-1})\mathsf{V})\,,\quad

\displaystyle g_{2}(z)/g_{1}(z)\mapsto z^{\mu}c_{-1/z}(\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{out}}-\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in}})\,,

\displaystyle e(z)\mapsto\frac{[\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(n+1,n,\underline{\mathbf{d}})]}{z-c_{1}(\mathcal{L})}\,,\quad

\displaystyle f(z)\mapsto\frac{(-1)^{\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}+n}\,\hbar}{z-c_{1}(\mathcal{L})}[\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(n,n-1,\underline{\mathbf{d}})]^{\mathrm{t}}\,.

In the case of
\mu=0
, we have

(8.7)

\displaystyle-g_{1,0}\otimes g_{2,0}-g_{2,0}\otimes g_{1,0}+2g_{1,0}\otimes g_{1,0}+e_{0}\otimes f_{0}-f_{0}\otimes e_{0}\mapsto\hbar\,\boldsymbol{r},

where
\boldsymbol{r}
is the classical

R
-matrix (
4.12
).

8.4.
Proof of Theorem
8.1

Definition 8.6
.

Let
\mathfrak{Y}=\mathfrak{Y}^{0}\oplus\mathfrak{Y}^{1}
be the
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
Lie superalgebra with even part
\mathfrak{Y}^{0}=\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{C}(\hbar)}\{\mathfrak{a}_{r},\mathfrak{h}_{r}\}_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
and odd part
\mathfrak{Y}^{1}=\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{C}(\hbar)}\{\mathfrak{e}_{r},\mathfrak{f}_{r}\}_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
, and Lie brackets on
\mathfrak{Y}
are given by

(8.8)

\begin{gathered}\mathfrak{h}_{r}\text{ is central for all $r$},\\
[\mathfrak{e}_{n},\mathfrak{e}_{m}]=[\mathfrak{f}_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{m}]=[\mathfrak{a}_{n},\mathfrak{a}_{m}]=0,\\
[\mathfrak{e}_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{m}]=-\hbar\,\mathfrak{h}_{n+m},\\
[\mathfrak{a}_{n},\mathfrak{e}_{m}]=\mathfrak{e}_{n+m},\quad[\mathfrak{a}_{n},\mathfrak{f}_{m}]=-\mathfrak{f}_{n+m}.\end{gathered}

Let
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{Y})
be the enveloping algebra (over
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
) of
\mathfrak{Y}
. Define
\mathcal{U}_{0}(\mathfrak{Y})=\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{Y})
, and for
\mu<0
, define
\mathcal{U}_{\mu}(\mathfrak{Y})
to be the quotient of
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{Y})
by the two-sided ideal generated by
\mathfrak{h}_{i}
for all
i<-\mu-1
and
\mathfrak{h}_{-\mu-1}-1
.

Remark 8.7
.

\mathfrak{Y}
is isomorphic to
\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1}[u]
, we give the above explicit generators and Lie brackets and use notation
\mathfrak{Y}
instead of
\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1}[u]
because we will provide an explicit algebra isomorphism
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{Y})\cong Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
, and we would like to reserve the notation
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1}[u])
that is isomorphic to
\mathrm{gr}\,Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
.

We denote
X(z)=\sum_{n\geqslant 0}X_{n}z^{-n-1}
for
X=\mathfrak{e},\mathfrak{f},\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{h}
.

Lemma 8.8
.

For any
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leqslant 0}
, the map

(8.9)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}(z)\mapsto e(z),\quad\mathfrak{f}(z)\mapsto f(z),\quad\exp\left(\sum_{n\geqslant 0}\mathfrak{a}_{n}\psi_{n}(z)\right)\mapsto g_{1}(z),\quad\mathfrak{h}(z)\mapsto g_{1}(z)^{-1}g_{2}(z),

uniquely determines a surjective
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
algebra homomorphism
\varphi_{\mu}\colon\mathcal{U}_{\mu}(\mathfrak{Y})\twoheadrightarrow Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
. Here
\{\psi_{n}(z)\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant}}
is a sequence of power series that is uniquely determined by the equation

\displaystyle\exp\left(\sum_{n\geqslant 0}a^{n}\psi_{n}(z)\right)=\frac{z-a}{z+\hbar-a}

for a formal variable
a
.

Proof.

We first show that (
8.9
) generates a surjective
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
algebra homomorphism from
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{Y})
to
Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
. From the RTT relation (
8.4
) we deduce that

(8.10)

\begin{split}[g_{i}(u),g_{j}(v)]&=0\text{ for all }1\leqslant i,j\leqslant 2\,,\\
[e(u),e(v)]&=0=[f(u),f(v)]\,,\\
(u-v)[e(u),f(v)]&=\hbar\,(g_{1}(u)^{-1}g_{2}(u)-g_{1}(v)^{-1}g_{2}(v))\,,\\
(u-v)[g_{i}(u),e(v)]&=\hbar\,g_{i}(u)(e(u)-e(v))\,,\;\text{ for }\;1\leqslant i\leqslant 2\,,\\
(u-v)[g_{i}(u),f(v)]&=\hbar\,(f(v)-f(u))g_{i}(u)\,,\;\text{ for }\;1\leqslant i\leqslant 2\,.\end{split}

Then all relations except the last line in (
8.8
) are apparently respected by the map (
8.9
).

Notice that
\operatorname{ad}_{\mathfrak{a}_{n}}(\mathfrak{e}_{m})=\operatorname{ad}_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}^{n}(\mathfrak{e}_{m})
.
Let
\mathfrak{A}(z)=\exp\left(\sum_{n\geqslant 0}\mathfrak{a}_{n}\psi_{n}(z)\right)
, then we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}(z)\mathfrak{e}(w)\mathfrak{A}(z)^{-1}=\exp\left(\sum_{n\geqslant 0}\operatorname{ad}_{\mathfrak{a}_{n}}\psi_{n}(z)\right)\mathfrak{e}(w)=\exp\left(\sum_{n\geqslant 0}\operatorname{ad}_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}^{n}\psi_{n}(z)\right)\mathfrak{e}(w)=\frac{z-\operatorname{ad}_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}}{z+\hbar-\operatorname{ad}_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}}e(w).

Using the relation
\operatorname{ad}_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}\mathfrak{e}(w)=w\,\mathfrak{e}(w)-\mathfrak{e}_{0}
, we can rewrite the above equation as follows

\displaystyle(z-w)[\mathfrak{A}(z),\mathfrak{e}(w)]=-\hbar\,\mathfrak{A}(z)\mathfrak{e}(w)+[\mathfrak{e}_{0},\mathfrak{A}(z)].

Taking
w=z
, we get
[\mathfrak{e}_{0},\mathfrak{A}(z)]=\hbar\,\mathfrak{A}(z)\mathfrak{e}(z)
, plug it back to the above and we have

(8.11)

\displaystyle(z-w)[\mathfrak{A}(z),\mathfrak{e}(w)]=\hbar\,\mathfrak{A}(z)(\mathfrak{e}(z)-\mathfrak{e}(w))\,.

A similar argument gives

(8.12)

\displaystyle(z-w)[\mathfrak{A}(z),\mathfrak{f}(w)]=\hbar\,(\mathfrak{f}(w)-\mathfrak{f}(z))\,\mathfrak{A}(z)\,.

If we write
\mathfrak{A}(z)=1+\sum_{n\geqslant 0}\mathfrak{A}_{n}z^{-n-1}
, then for all
n\geqslant 0
,
\mathfrak{A}_{n}=-\hbar\,\mathfrak{a}_{n}+f_{n}(\{\mathfrak{a}_{i}\}_{i<n})
for some polynomial
f_{n}
(we set
\mathfrak{a}_{-1}:=-1
). So (
8.11
) and (
8.12
) are equivalent to the last two relations in (
8.8
) respectively. Compare with the last two equations in (
8.10
), and we see that (
8.9
) also respects the last two relations in (
8.8
), so (
8.9
) generates a
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
algebra homomorphism from
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{Y})
to
Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})
. It is obviously surjective. When
\mu<0
,
\{\mathfrak{h}_{i}\}_{i<-\mu-1}\cup\{\mathfrak{h}_{-\mu-1}-1\}
are in the kernel. This proves the lemma.
∎

Define
\widetilde{\varrho}_{\mu}
to be the composition
\mathcal{U}_{\mu}(\mathfrak{Y})\xrightarrow{\varphi_{\mu}}Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)\xrightarrow{{\varrho}_{\mu}}\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,0)
. It is surjective since both
{\varrho}_{\mu}
and
\varphi_{\mu}
are surjective. Theorem
8.1
follows from the next proposition.

Proposition 8.9
.

For all
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leqslant 0}
, the map
\widetilde{\varrho}_{\mu}\colon\mathcal{U}_{\mu}(\mathfrak{Y})\to\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,0)
is injective.

Proof.

\mu=0
case.
\mathfrak{Y}
is graded by setting
\deg X_{n}=n
for
X=\mathfrak{e},\mathfrak{f},\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{h}
. Let
F_{\bullet}\,\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{Y})
be the filtration induced by the grading on
\mathfrak{Y}
, then the map (
8.9
) preserves the filtrations, i.e.
\varphi_{0}(F_{k}\,\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{Y}))\subseteq F_{k}\,Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
. So
\varphi_{0}
induces a natural map between associated graded algebras
\bar{\varphi}_{0}\colon\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{Y})\to\mathrm{gr}\,Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
. Note that
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{Y})
is canonically isomorphic to
\mathrm{gr}\,\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{Y})
since the filtration is induced by a grading.
\mathrm{gr}\,Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1}[u])
. Let
\bar{x}_{n}
be the image of
x_{n}
in
\mathrm{gr}\,Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})
for
x=e,f,g_{1},g_{2}
. Then
\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1}[u]
has a basis
\{\bar{e}_{n},\bar{f}_{n},\bar{g}_{1,n},\bar{g}_{2,n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
. It is straightforward to compute from (
8.9
) that
\bar{\varphi}_{0}(\mathfrak{e}_{n})=\bar{e}_{n}
,
\bar{\varphi}_{0}(\mathfrak{f}_{n})=\bar{f}_{n}
,
\bar{\varphi}_{0}(\mathfrak{a}_{n})=-\bar{g}_{1,n}/\hbar
, and
\bar{\varphi}_{0}(\mathfrak{h}_{n})=\bar{g}_{2,n}-\bar{g}_{1,n}
. In particular,
\bar{\varphi}_{0}
induces an isomorphism. Thus
\varphi_{0}
is injective. Then the injectivity of
\widetilde{\varrho}_{0}
follows from Lemma
8.4
.

\mu<0
case. Define a
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
algebra homomorphism
\mathfrak{S}_{z}^{\mu}\colon\mathcal{U}_{\mu}(\mathfrak{Y})\to\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{Y})(\!(z^{-1})\!)
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{z}^{\mu}(\mathfrak{e}_{n})=(-1)^{\mu}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-z)^{\mu-k}\binom{\mu}{k}\mathfrak{e}_{n+k},\quad\mathfrak{S}_{z}^{\mu}(\mathfrak{f}_{n})=(-1)^{\mu}\mathfrak{f}_{n},\quad\mathfrak{S}_{z}^{\mu}(\mathfrak{a}_{n})=\mathfrak{a}_{n},\quad\mathfrak{S}_{z}^{\mu}(\mathfrak{h}_{n})=\sum_{k=0}^{n+\mu+1}(-z)^{k}\binom{\mu}{k}\mathfrak{h}_{n+\mu-k},

where we set
\mathfrak{h}_{i}=0
for
i<-1
and
\mathfrak{h}_{-1}=0
on the right hand side of the last equation. The proof that
\mathfrak{S}_{z}^{\mu}
is an algebra homomorphism is straightforward and is omitted.

We claim that
\mathfrak{S}_{z}^{\mu}
is injective. Let
\mathfrak{E}_{\mu},\mathfrak{F}_{\mu},\mathfrak{A}_{\mu},\mathfrak{H}_{\mu}
be the subalgebras of
\mathcal{U}_{\mu}(\mathfrak{Y})
which are generated by
\{\mathfrak{e}_{r}\}_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
,
\{\mathfrak{f}_{r}\}_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
,
\{\mathfrak{a}_{r}\}_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
, and
\{\mathfrak{h}_{s}\}_{s\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant-\mu}}
respectively. The PBW theorem for
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{Y})
implies that
\mathcal{U}_{\mu}(\mathfrak{Y})
has a basis (over
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
) consisting of ordered super monomials in
\{\mathfrak{e}_{r},\mathfrak{f}_{r},\mathfrak{a}_{r},\mathfrak{h}_{s}\}_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0},s\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant-\mu}}
. In particular,
\mathcal{U}_{\mu}(\mathfrak{Y})\cong\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}\otimes\mathfrak{F}_{\mu}\otimes\mathfrak{A}_{\mu}\otimes\mathfrak{H}_{\mu}
where the tensor product is over
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
. Then it is enough to show that the restrictions:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{z,\mathfrak{E}}^{\mu}\colon\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}\to\mathfrak{E}_{0}[\![z^{-1}]\!],\quad\mathfrak{S}_{z,\mathfrak{F}}^{\mu}\colon\mathfrak{F}_{\mu}\to\mathfrak{F}_{0},\quad\mathfrak{S}_{z,\mathfrak{A}}^{\mu}\colon\mathfrak{A}_{\mu}\to\mathfrak{A}_{0},\quad\mathfrak{S}_{z,\mathfrak{H}}^{\mu}\colon\mathfrak{H}_{\mu}\to\mathfrak{H}_{0}[z],

are injective.
\mathfrak{S}_{z,\mathfrak{F}}^{\mu}
and
\mathfrak{S}_{z,\mathfrak{A}}^{\mu}\colon\mathfrak{A}_{\mu}\to\mathfrak{A}_{0}
are obviously isomorphisms. The composition
\mathfrak{H}_{\mu}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{S}_{z,\mathfrak{H}}^{\mu}}\mathfrak{H}_{0}[z]\xrightarrow{\mod z}\mathfrak{H}_{0}
is given by
\mathfrak{h}_{n}\mapsto\mathfrak{h}_{n+\mu}
, which induces an isomorphism; thus
\mathfrak{S}_{z,\mathfrak{H}}^{\mu}
is injective.
\mathfrak{S}_{z,\mathfrak{E}}^{\mu}
decomposes into

\mathfrak{S}_{z,\mathfrak{E}}^{\mu}=\underbrace{\{z^{-1}\}\circ\cdots\circ\{z^{-1}\}}_{-\mu\text{ copies}}\circ\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{z,\mathfrak{E}}^{\mu},

where
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{z,\mathfrak{E}}^{\mu}\colon\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}\to\mathfrak{E}_{0}[\![z^{-1}]\!]
is given by
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{z}^{\mu}(\mathfrak{e}_{n})=(-1)^{\mu}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-z)^{-k}\binom{\mu}{k}\mathfrak{e}_{n+k}
,
\{z^{-1}\}\colon\mathfrak{E}_{0}[\![z^{-1}]\!]\to\mathfrak{E}_{0}[\![z^{-1}]\!]
is the endomorphism generated by
\mathfrak{e}_{n}\mapsto z^{-1}\mathfrak{e}_{n}
. The composition
\mathfrak{E}_{\mu}\xrightarrow{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{z,\mathfrak{E}}^{\mu}}\mathfrak{E}_{0}[z]\xrightarrow{\mod z}\mathfrak{E}_{0}
is given by
\mathfrak{e}_{n}\mapsto(-1)^{\mu}\mathfrak{e}_{n}
, which induces an isomorphism; thus
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{z,\mathfrak{E}}^{\mu}
is injective. On the other hand,
\mathfrak{E}_{0}
is the free exterior algebra
\bigwedge^{\bullet}\left(\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{C}(\hbar)}\{\mathfrak{e}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}\right)
. Suppose that

\displaystyle f=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{r\geqslant 0}f_{m,r}z^{-m}\in\ker(\{z^{-1}\}),

where the second summation is finite for any fixed
m
, and
f_{m,r}\in\bigwedge^{r}\left(\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{C}(\hbar)}\{\mathfrak{e}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}\right)
. Then we have

\displaystyle 0=\{z^{-1}\}(f)=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{r\geqslant 0}f_{m,r}z^{-m-r},\;\text{ equivalently }\;\sum_{m+r=n}f_{m,r}=0\;\text{ for any }\;n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}.

\bigwedge^{r}\left(\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{C}(\hbar)}\{\mathfrak{e}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}\right)
are linearly independent for different
r
, so
f_{m,r}=0
for all
m
and
r
. Thus
\ker(\{z^{-1}\})=0
. This shows that
\mathfrak{S}_{z,\mathfrak{E}}^{\mu}
is injective and the claim follows.

Finally, straightforward comparison shows that
\mathfrak{S}_{z}^{\mu}
is compatible with the wrong-way shift homomorphism (Definition
4.30
):

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varrho}_{0}\circ\mathfrak{S}_{z}^{\mu}=\mathsf{S}^{0,\mu}_{z}\circ\widetilde{\varrho}_{\mu}.

The left hand side is injective by what we have shown, so
\widetilde{\varrho}_{\mu}
is injective.
∎

It follows from the proof that
\varphi_{\mu}\colon\mathcal{U}_{\mu}(\mathfrak{Y})\to Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
is an isomorphism.

Corollary 8.10
.

Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})
is a free
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
-module with a basis given by ordered super monomials in

\left\{e_{r},f_{r},g_{1,r},g_{2,s}\right\}_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0},s\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant-\mu}}.

Proof.

The relations (
8.10
) implies that ordered super monomials in
\left\{e_{r},f_{r},g_{1,r},g_{2,s}\right\}_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0},s\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant-\mu}}
generate
Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})
as a
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
-module. To show that these monomials are
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
-independent, it suffices to show that they are
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
linear independent after localization. Note that ordered super monomials in
\left\{e_{r},f_{r},g_{1,r},g_{2,s}\right\}_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0},s\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant-\mu}}
can be
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
linearly represented by ordered super monomials in
\left\{\rho_{\mu}(\mathfrak{e}_{r}),\rho_{\mu}(\mathfrak{f}_{r}),\rho_{\mu}(\mathfrak{a}_{r}),\rho_{\mu}(\mathfrak{h}_{s})\right\}_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0},s\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant-\mu}}
and vice versa, and the latter set of monomials is a basis of
\mathcal{U}_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})
by the PBW theorem for
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})
. This proves the linear independence.
∎

8.5.
Quantum multiplication by divisors

\underline{\mathbf{d}}=(r_{1},r_{2})
with
r_{1}\geqslant r_{2}
in the following framed quiver with zero potential:

\par
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\qquad\mathsf{W}=0.

Denote
X_{n,r_{1},r_{2}}=\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}})
. Apply Theorem
5.13
to
X_{n,r_{1},r_{2}}
and we get an explicit formula of modified quantum multiplication by divisor on
X_{n,r_{1},r_{2}}
.
Note that
X_{r_{1},r_{1},r_{2}}
is an affine space, so we shall focus on the
r_{1}>n
cases.

Proposition 8.11
.

Assume
r_{1}>n
. Let
\mathcal{O}(1)
be the determinant of tautological bundle
\mathsf{V}
on
X_{n,r_{1},r_{2}}
, then

\displaystyle c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(1))\,\widetilde{\star}\,\cdot=\begin{cases}c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(1))\cup\cdot+\hbar^{-1}\frac{z}{1+z}e_{0}f_{0},&\text{ if $r_{1}=r_{2}$ },\\
c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(1))\cup\cdot+\hbar^{-1}z\,e_{0}f_{0},&\text{ if $r_{1}>r_{2}$ },\end{cases}

where
z
is the parameter of curve classes,
e_{0},f_{0}
are part of the generators of
Y_{r_{2}-r_{1}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1|1})
in Definition
8.2
.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.45
and the formula for the classical
R
-matrix (
8.7
), the Casimir operators for
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,0)
are

\mathrm{Cas}_{1}=\hbar^{-1}e_{0}f_{0},\quad\mathrm{Cas}_{i}=0,\,\,\mathrm{if}\,\,i>1.

Then by Theorem
5.10
, we have

c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(1))\,\widetilde{\star}\,\cdot=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(1))\cup\cdot+\hbar^{-1}\frac{z}{1+z}e_{0}f_{0}+\mathrm{const}.

The constant is given by
-\hbar^{-1}\frac{z}{1+z}e_{0}f_{0}
acting on
1\in H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(X_{n,r_{1},r_{2}})
. According to Theorem
3.7
,
f_{0}(1)
is given by the pushforward of the fundamental class
[\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(n,n-1,\underline{\mathbf{d}})]^{\mathrm{t}}
to
X_{n-1,r_{1},r_{2}}
. It follows from (
3.9
) that
\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(n,n-1,\underline{\mathbf{d}})
is a
\mathbb{P}^{r_{1}-n}
-bundle over
X_{n-1,r_{1},r_{2}}
, so
f_{0}(1)=0
by our assumption that
r_{1}>n
. This proves the
r_{1}=r_{2}
case.

For the
r_{1}>r_{2}
case, it suffices to show that

\mathrm{Cas}_{1,r_{2}-r_{1}}=\hbar^{-1}e_{0}f_{0},

where
e_{0},f_{0}
are part of the generators of
Y_{r_{2}-r_{1}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
. We put bar on the generators of
Y_{r_{2}-r_{1}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
to distinguish from their counterparts of
Y_{0}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
. From the proof of Lemma
4.27
, we have equations of operators:

i^{*}e(u)\,p^{*}=H_{z}\,\bar{e}(u)\,H_{z}^{-1}=\frac{(z-c_{1}(\mathcal{L}))^{r_{1}-r_{2}}}{u-c_{1}(\mathcal{L})}\,[\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(n+1,n,\underline{\mathbf{d}})]=z^{r_{1}-r_{2}}\bar{e}(u)+\text{lower order in $z$},

i^{*}f(u)\,p^{*}=(-1)^{r_{1}-r_{2}}\bar{f}(u).

By expanding in the
u
-power, we obtain

i^{*}e_{r}\,p^{*}=z^{r_{1}-r_{2}}\,\bar{e}_{r}+\text{lower order in $z$},\quad i^{*}f_{r}\,p^{*}=(-1)^{r_{1}-r_{2}}\bar{f}_{r}.

This implies that

i^{*}\mathrm{Cas}_{1}\,p^{*}=\hbar^{-1}i^{*}e_{0}f_{0}\,p^{*}=(-z)^{r_{1}-r_{2}}\,\hbar^{-1}\,\bar{e}_{0}\bar{f}_{0}+\text{lower order in $z$},

therefore
\mathrm{Cas}_{1,r_{2}-r_{1}}=\lim_{z\to\infty}(-z)^{r_{2}-r_{1}}i^{*}\mathrm{Cas}_{1}\,p^{*}=\hbar^{-1}\bar{e}_{0}\bar{f}_{0}
by Definition
4.50
.
∎

9.
Example: Jordan quiver and modules of shifted Yangians of
\mathfrak{gl}_{2}

In this section, we consider the example of the Jordan quiver consisting of one node and one edge loop:

Q:\hbox to38.17pt{\vbox to59.23pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 29.61407pt\lower-29.61407pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#FFFFFF} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#FFFFFF} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 11.57 0 C 11.57 6.39 6.39 11.57 0 11.57 C -6.39 11.57 -11.57 6.39 -11.57 0 C -11.57 -6.39 -6.39 -11.57 0 -11.57 C 6.39 -11.57 11.57 -6.39 11.57 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.00117pt}{-2.15277pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -4.15 -2.98)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\par{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}
{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{{{}}{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -8.37 -8.37 C -40.7 -40.7 -40.7 40.7 -8.82 8.82}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.7071}{-0.7071}{0.7071}{0.7071}{-6.37695pt}{6.37695pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(0.7071 -0.7071 0.7071 0.7071 -8.82 8.82)} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.32pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray=none,stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=round} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -1.66 2.21 C -1.52 1.38 0 0.14 0.42 0 C 0 -0.14 -1.52 -1.38 -1.66 -2.21}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

We regard
Q
as the tripled quiver (Example
2.4
) associated to the
A_{1}
quiver (with one node with no edge).
Let
\mathsf{T}_{0}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
be the torus that scales the loop with weight
-1
. Fix a cyclic stability
\theta<0
when we consider associated quiver varieties.

The main theorem of this section is an
explicit
description of the shifted Yangian
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,0)
for
Q
(
4.5
).

Theorem 9.1
.

Let
Q
be the Jordan quiver. For arbitrary
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leqslant 0}
, there is a natural algebra isomorphism

\displaystyle Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)\cong\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,0)\>,

where
Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
is the Frassek-Pestun-Tsymbaliuk

\mu
-shifted Yangian of
\mathfrak{gl}_{2}
(Definition
9.11
).

The
\mu=0
case of Theorem
9.1
follows from Theorem
4.45
and the fact that
\mathsf{Y}^{\mathrm{MO}}_{Q}\cong Y_{0}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})

[
MO
, Prop. 11.3.1]
. In §
9.6
, we give a uniform proof of Theorem
9.1
for all
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leqslant 0}
.

We also study several representations of shifted Yangian
Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
constructed from critical cohomology, including
higher spin, dual Verma and prefundamental modules.
We perform
explicit
computations of cohomological stable envelopes and root
R
-matrices in §
9.2
and
§
9.3
. We give a detailed study of the
fusion procedure
from geometric construction in §
9.8
(see Corollary
9.29
for the fusion formula).
We deduce the explicit formula for quantum multiplication by divisors on the framed quiver varieties (Proposition
9.31
).

9.1.
Bases of
\mathcal{H}_{N}
,
\mathcal{V}
, and
\mathcal{F}

The following are three basic cases of framings and potentials:

(1)

(Higher spin module)
\underline{\mathbf{d}}=\mathbf{1}=(1,1)
,
\mathsf{W}_{N}=B\Phi^{N}A
(with
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}
), and we require that
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
scales
B
with weight
N
and fixes
A
. Denote
\mathsf{w}_{N}=\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{W}_{N})
.

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\qquad\mathsf{W}=0.

We define

(9.2)

\mathcal{F}(n):=H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}})),\quad\mathcal{F}:=H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}}))=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}\mathcal{F}(n).

Note that there is a natural isomorphism
\mathcal{V}\cong\mathcal{F}[t]
since
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1})
is a vector bundle on
\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}})
, but we will see that their stable envelopes and
R
-matrices are not extensions by scalars.

To present stable envelopes and
R
-matrices, we need bases of
\mathcal{H}_{N}
,
\mathcal{V}
, and
\mathcal{F}
.

As
\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1})
is a vector bundle on
\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}})
with fibers given by the descent of
B\in\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^{n},\mathbb{C})
to the GIT quotient,
by dimensional reduction
[
Dav1
, Thm. A.1]
, there is an isomorphism

\displaystyle H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w}_{N})\cong H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(Z^{\prime}_{n}),

where
Z^{\prime}_{n}\subset\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}})
is the zero locus of the section
\Phi^{N}A\in\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{C},\mathbb{C}^{n})
. Note that

\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}})\cong\operatorname{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{C})\cong S^{n}\mathbb{C}\cong\mathbb{C}^{n},

and the equation
\Phi^{N}A=0
translates to requiring that
n
points on
\mathbb{C}
are supported on
\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[z]/(z^{N})
; therefore

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}_{n}=\begin{cases}\text{the zero of $\mathbb{C}^{n}$},&n\leqslant N,\\
\quad\quad\quad\emptyset,&n>N.\end{cases}

In particular,
H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(Z^{\prime}_{n})=\mathbb{C}[\hbar]\cdot[Z^{\prime}_{n}]
. Let
Z_{n}
be the preimage of
Z^{\prime}_{n}
in
\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1})
, then we have:

Lemma 9.2
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{N}(n)=H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w}_{N})\cong\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}[\hbar]\cdot[Z_{n}],&n\leqslant N,\\
\quad\quad 0,&n>N,\end{cases}

where the isomorphism is induced by the canonical map
H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(Z_{n})\to H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(Z(\mathsf{w}_{N}))\xrightarrow{\operatorname{can}}H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w}_{N})
.

For
\mathcal{V}
and
\mathcal{F}
, the natural choices of bases are given by fundamental classes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}(n)\cong\mathbb{C}[\hbar,t]\cdot[\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1})],\quad\mathcal{F}(n)\cong\mathbb{C}[\hbar]\cdot[\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}})].

Lemma 9.3
.

There is an injective specialization map

\displaystyle\mathsf{sp}\colon\mathcal{H}_{N}\to\mathcal{V}\big|_{t=N\hbar},\quad\mathsf{sp}([Z_{n}])=n!\hbar^{n}[\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1})]\quad(n\leqslant N).

Proof.

Note that
\mathcal{V}|_{t=N\hbar}
is
H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1}))
, where
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
scales
B
with weight
N
and
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
fixes
A
. By

[
COZZ1
, Ex. 7.19]
, the specialization map exits, and the explicit formula uses
e^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(N_{Z_{n}/\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1})})=n!\hbar^{n}
. In particular, it is injective.
∎

For later use, we introduce identifications of vector spaces

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}\cong\mathbb{C}[\hbar,t][\mathbf{x}],\quad\mathcal{F}\cong\mathbb{C}[\hbar][\mathbf{x}],

such that bases are identified by

(9.3)

\displaystyle[\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1})]\;\longleftrightarrow\;\frac{\mathbf{x}^{n}}{n!}\;\longleftrightarrow\;[\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}})].

Remark 9.4
.

The critical cohomologies
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n)
,
\mathcal{V}(n)
and
\mathcal{F}(n)
are pure, then by Remark
2.11
, Thom-Sebastiani Theorem applies to the product
\prod_{i=1}^{a}\mathcal{M}(n_{i},\mathbf{1})\times\prod_{j=1}^{b}\mathcal{M}(m_{j},\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}})
with potential
=\boxplus_{i=1}^{a^{\prime}}\mathsf{w}_{N_{i}}

(a^{\prime}\leqslant a)
without the need of localization.

9.2.
Computations of stable envelopes

Consider the quiver variety
\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{r})
depicted as below.

n
1
\cdots
\cdots
1
\Phi
A_{1}
B_{1}
B_{r}
A_{r}

Define an
\mathsf{A}\cong(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{r}
action by
\mathbf{r}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}z_{i}\cdot\mathbf{1}^{(i)}
. Let
\mathsf{T}=\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\prod_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t_{i}}
, where
(q,p_{1},\cdots,p_{r})\in\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\prod_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t_{i}}
acts on quiver data by

\displaystyle(\Phi,A_{1},B_{1},\ldots,A_{k},B_{k})\mapsto(q^{-1}\Phi,A_{1},p_{1}B_{1},\ldots,A_{r},p_{r}B_{r}).

We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{r})^{\mathsf{A}}=\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{n}=(n_{1},\cdots,n_{r})\\
n_{1}+\cdots+n_{r}=n\end{subarray}}F_{\vec{n}},\quad\text{where}\quad F_{\vec{n}}=\prod_{i=1}^{r}\mathcal{M}(n_{i},\mathbf{1}).

Let
\mathfrak{C}
be the chamber
\{z_{1}>\cdots>z_{r}\}
. Denote
H_{\mathrm{GL}_{n}}(\operatorname{pt})=\mathbb{C}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}]
.
Then
[
COZZ1
, Prop. 9.7]
implies:

Proposition 9.5
.

We have the following explicit formula for the stable envelope:

\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{r})^{\mathsf{A}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{r})),

(9.4)

\begin{split}&\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}([F_{\vec{n}}])=\mathbf{W}_{\mathfrak{C},\vec{n}}(\{x_{\alpha}\},\{z_{i}\},\{t_{i}\})\cdot[\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{r})],\\
\mathbf{W}_{\mathfrak{C},\vec{n}}(\{x_{\alpha}\},\{z_{i}\},\{t_{i}\})&=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Sh}\{S_{1},\ldots,S_{r}\}}\sigma\left[\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant r}\left(\prod_{\alpha\in S_{j}}(x_{\alpha}-z_{i})\right)\left(\prod_{\beta\in S_{i}}(z_{j}+t_{j}-x_{\beta})\right)\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in S_{j}\\
\beta\in S_{i}\end{subarray}}\frac{x_{\alpha}-x_{\beta}-\hbar}{x_{\alpha}-x_{\beta}}\right)\right],\end{split}

where
S_{1}\sqcup S_{2}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup S_{r}=\{1,2,\ldots,n\}
is a fixed decomposition with
|S_{i}|=n_{i}
, and
\mathrm{Sh}\{S_{1},\ldots,S_{r}\}
is the set of permutations
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
that preserves the order of elements in each
S_{i}
, that is,
\gamma<\nu\Leftrightarrow\sigma(\gamma)<\sigma(\nu)
for all pairs
\gamma,\nu\in S_{i}
. The matrix elements of
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}
is given by

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}([F_{\vec{n}}])\bigg|_{[F_{\vec{n}^{\prime}}]}=\mathbf{W}_{\mathfrak{C},\vec{n}}(\{x_{\alpha}\},\{z_{i}\},\{t_{i}\})\bigg|_{x_{\alpha}\mapsto z_{p_{\vec{n}^{\prime}}(\alpha)}+l_{\vec{n}^{\prime}}(\alpha)\hbar}[F_{\vec{n}^{\prime}}].

Here the notation
p_{\vec{n}^{\prime}}(\alpha)
and
l_{\vec{n}^{\prime}}(\alpha)
is determined by the conditions that
\alpha
belongs to the subset
S^{\prime}_{p(\alpha)}
and there are exactly
l(\alpha)
elements in
S^{\prime}_{p(\alpha)}
which are smaller than
\alpha
, where
S^{\prime}_{1}\sqcup S^{\prime}_{2}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup S^{\prime}_{r}=\{1,2,\ldots,n\}
is a fixed decomposition with
|S^{\prime}_{i}|=n^{\prime}_{i}
.

Remark 9.6
.

The
K
-theoretic version of formula (
9.4
) first appears in
[
Ta
, (60), (61)]
. The function

\mathbf{W}_{\mathfrak{C},\vec{n}}
is called the “weight function” in
[
YZJ
]
.

To get the stable envelope for the opposite chamber
-\mathfrak{C}=\{z_{1}<\cdots<z_{r}\}
, we change

\mathbf{W}_{\mathfrak{C},\vec{n}}(\{x_{\alpha}\},\{z_{i}\},\{t_{i}\})
in (
9.4
) to

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}_{-\mathfrak{C},\vec{n}}(\{x_{\alpha}\},\{z_{i}\},\{t_{i}\})=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Sh}\{S_{1},\ldots,S_{r}\}}\sigma\left[\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant r}\left(\prod_{\alpha\in S_{i}}(x_{\alpha}-z_{j})\right)\left(\prod_{\beta\in S_{j}}(z_{i}+t_{i}-x_{\beta})\right)\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in S_{i}\\
\beta\in S_{j}\end{subarray}}\frac{x_{\alpha}-x_{\beta}-\hbar}{x_{\alpha}-x_{\beta}}\right)\right].

9.2.1.
Transpose of stable envelope

The transpose
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{\tau}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{r}))_{\mathrm{loc}}\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{r})^{\mathsf{A}})_{\mathrm{loc}}
is the map induced by the transpose of stable envelope correspondence (
[
COZZ1
, §3.4.2]
), i.e.

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{\tau}([F_{\vec{n}^{\prime}}])\bigg|_{[F_{\vec{n}}]}

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{[F_{\vec{n}^{\prime}}]}1}{\int_{[F_{\vec{n}}]}1}\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}([F_{\vec{n}}])\bigg|_{[F_{\vec{n}^{\prime}}]}

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{i=1}^{r}\frac{n_{i}!\prod_{k=0}^{n_{i}-1}(t_{i}-k\hbar)}{n^{\prime}_{i}!\prod_{m=0}^{n^{\prime}_{i}-1}(t_{i}-m\hbar)}\right)\mathbf{W}_{\mathfrak{C},\vec{n}}(\{x_{\alpha}\},\{z_{i}\},\{t_{i}\})\bigg|_{x_{\alpha}\mapsto z_{p_{\vec{n}^{\prime}}(\alpha)}+l_{\vec{n}^{\prime}}(\alpha)\hbar}\cdot[F_{\vec{n}}].

9.2.2.
R
-matrix

The
R
-matrix
R_{-\mathfrak{C},\mathfrak{C}}\in\operatorname{End}(H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{r})^{\mathsf{A}})_{\mathrm{loc}})
is given by

\displaystyle R_{-\mathfrak{C},\mathfrak{C}}([F_{\vec{n}}])\bigg|_{F_{\vec{n}^{\prime}}}=\sum_{\vec{n}^{\prime\prime}}\frac{1}{e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F_{\vec{n}^{\prime\prime}}/\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{r})})}\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{\tau}([F_{\vec{n}^{\prime\prime}}])\bigg|_{[F_{\vec{n}^{\prime}}]}\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}([F_{\vec{n}}])\bigg|_{[F_{\vec{n}^{\prime\prime}}]}\cdot[F_{\vec{n}^{\prime}}].

9.2.3.
Including potential
\mathsf{w}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}B_{i}\Phi^{N_{i}}A_{i}

To preserve the potential, we set
t_{i}=N_{i}\hbar
with
\mathsf{T}^{\prime}=\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
.

Thom-Sebastiani isomorphism implies

\displaystyle H^{\mathsf{T}^{\prime}}(F_{\vec{n}},\mathsf{w})\cong\bigotimes_{i=1}^{r}H^{\mathsf{T}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{M}(n_{i},\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w}_{N_{i}})\cong\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}[\hbar,z_{1},\ldots,z_{r}]\cdot[Z_{n_{1}}]\otimes\cdots\otimes[Z_{n_{r}}],&\text{if }\>\forall\,i,n_{i}\leqslant N_{i},\\
\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad 0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

By
[
COZZ1
, Prop. 7.18]
and Lemma
9.3
, we have:

Proposition 9.7
.

The stable envelope

\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{r})^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{r}),\mathsf{w})

is the restriction of the zero potential case (
9.4
) to the image of specialization map. Namely,

(9.5)

\displaystyle\mathsf{sp}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}([Z_{n_{1}}]\otimes\cdots\otimes[Z_{n_{r}}])=\hbar^{n}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{r}n_{i}!\right)\mathbf{W}_{\mathfrak{C},\vec{n}}(\{x_{\alpha}\},\{z_{i}\},\{t_{i}\})\bigg|_{t_{i}\mapsto N_{i}\hbar}\cdot[\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{r})].

The matrix elements of
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}
are given by

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}([Z_{n_{1}}]\otimes\cdots\otimes[Z_{n_{r}}])\bigg|_{F_{\vec{n}^{\prime}}}=\left(\prod_{i=1}^{r}\frac{n_{i}!}{n^{\prime}_{i}!}\right)\mathbf{W}_{\mathfrak{C},\vec{n}}(\{x_{\alpha}\},\{z_{i}\},\{t_{i}\})\Bigg|_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{i}\mapsto N_{i}\hbar\\
x_{\alpha}\mapsto z_{p_{\vec{n}^{\prime}}(\alpha)}+l_{\vec{n}^{\prime}}(\alpha)\hbar\end{subarray}}\cdot[Z_{n^{\prime}_{1}}]\otimes\cdots\otimes[Z_{n^{\prime}_{r}}].

And the transpose is given by

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}^{\tau}_{\mathfrak{C}}([Z_{n^{\prime}_{1}}]\otimes\cdots\otimes[Z_{n^{\prime}_{r}}])\bigg|_{F_{\vec{n}}}=\left(\prod_{i=1}^{r}\frac{\prod_{k=0}^{n_{i}-1}(N_{i}-k)}{\prod_{m=0}^{n^{\prime}_{i}-1}(N_{i}-m)}\right)\mathbf{W}_{\mathfrak{C},\vec{n}}(\{x_{\alpha}\},\{z_{i}\},\{t_{i}\})\Bigg|_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{i}\mapsto N_{i}\hbar\\
x_{\alpha}\mapsto z_{p_{\vec{n}^{\prime}}(\alpha)}+l_{\vec{n}^{\prime}}(\alpha)\hbar\end{subarray}}\cdot[Z_{n_{1}}]\otimes\cdots\otimes[Z_{n_{r}}].

9.3.
Root
R
-matrices

We compute several root
R
-matrices explicitly.

9.3.1.
R
-matrix for
\mathcal{H}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{V}

Consider the following quiver with potential

Let
\mathsf{A}\cong\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
act on the framing by
u\mathbf{1}^{(1)}+\mathbf{1}^{(2)}
, and
\mathsf{T}=\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}
, where
(q,p)\in\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}
acts on quiver data by

\displaystyle(\Phi,A_{1},B_{1},A_{2},B_{2})\mapsto(q^{-1}\Phi,A_{1},qB_{1},A_{2},pB_{2}).

In the basis
[Z_{0}]\otimes[\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1})]
and
[Z_{1}]\otimes[\mathcal{M}(n-1,\mathbf{1})]
,
\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}
is given by the matrix

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\prod_{j=0}^{n-1}(j\hbar-u)&\,\,n\hbar\,(t-(n-1)\hbar)\prod_{j=0}^{n-2}(j\hbar-u)\\
0&(t-u)\prod_{j=0}^{n-2}((j-1)\hbar-u)\end{pmatrix},

\operatorname{Stab}_{u<0}
is given by the matrix

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}(u-(i-1)\hbar)&0\\
\prod_{i=0}^{n-2}(u-(i-1)\hbar)&\quad(u-(n-1)\hbar)\prod_{i=0}^{n-2}(u-(i-1)\hbar)\end{pmatrix}.

To properly normalize the diagonal elements of the
R
-matrix, we introduce the normalizers (
[
COZZ1
, Def. 3.5]
):

(9.6)

\displaystyle\epsilon|_{F}=(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}\mathcal{P}|_{F}^{+}}.

Here the vector bundle
\mathcal{P}
on
\mathcal{M}(2)
is the descent of
\operatorname{Hom}(V,D_{\operatorname{out}})
, where
D_{\operatorname{out}}
is the out-going framing vector space and
V
is the gauge vector space.
\mathcal{P}|_{F}^{+}
is the attracting subbundle of
\mathcal{P}
restricted on a fixed component
F
.

Then in the basis
[Z_{0}]\otimes[\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1})]
and
[Z_{1}]\otimes[\mathcal{M}(n-1,\mathbf{1})]
, we have

\displaystyle R_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{V}}(u)=\operatorname{Stab}_{u<0,\epsilon}^{-1}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0,\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}(-1)^{n}&0\\
0&(-1)^{n-1}\end{pmatrix}\operatorname{Stab}_{u<0}^{-1}\,\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{u+\hbar}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}u-(n-1)\hbar&\,\,n\hbar\left(t-(n-1)\hbar\right)\\
1&u+n\hbar-t\\
\end{array}\right).

Using the basis
[\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1})]=\frac{\mathbf{x}^{n}}{n!}
in (
9.3
), we can write
R_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{V}}(u)
in the following compact form

\displaystyle R_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{V}}(u)=\operatorname{id}+\frac{1}{u+\hbar}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-\mathbf{x}\mathbf{y}&\,\,\hbar\mathbf{x}(t-\mathbf{x}\mathbf{y})\\
\mathbf{y}/\hbar&\mathbf{x}\mathbf{y}-t\\
\end{array}\right),

where
\mathbf{y}=\hbar\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{x}}
acts on
\mathbb{C}[\hbar][\mathbf{x}]
as a differential operator.

-matrix for
\mathcal{H}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{N}

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{1.91891pt}{-30.67271pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 2.66 -42.44)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\par{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}
{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{{{}}{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -8.37 8.37 C -40.7 40.7 40.7 40.7 8.82 8.82}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{-0.7071}{-0.7071}{0.7071}{-0.7071}{6.37695pt}{6.37695pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(-0.7071 -0.7071 0.7071 -0.7071 8.82 8.82)} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.32pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray=none,stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=round} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -1.66 2.21 C -1.52 1.38 0 0.14 0.42 0 C 0 -0.14 -1.52 -1.38 -1.66 -2.21}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\qquad\mathsf{W}=B_{1}\Phi A_{1}+B_{2}\Phi^{N}A_{2}.

Let
\mathsf{A}\cong\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
act on the framing by
u\mathbf{1}^{(1)}+\mathbf{1}^{(2)}
, and
\mathsf{T}=\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
, where
q\in\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
acts on quiver data by

\displaystyle(\Phi,A_{1},B_{1},A_{2},B_{2})\mapsto(q^{-1}\Phi,A_{1},qB_{1},A_{2},q^{N}B_{2}).

Using the normalizer (
9.6
), we define

\displaystyle R_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}_{N}}(u):=\operatorname{Stab}_{u<0,\epsilon}^{-1}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0,\epsilon}\in\operatorname{End}(\mathcal{H}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{N})(u).

Proposition 9.8
.

R_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}_{N}}(u)
is the restriction of
R_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{V}}(u)_{t=N\hbar}
to
\mathcal{H}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{N}
that is identified with its image under the specialization
\operatorname{id}\otimes\>\mathsf{sp}\colon\mathcal{H}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{N}\to\mathcal{H}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{V}\big|_{t=N\hbar}
.

Proof.

Take an additional
\mathbb{C}^{*}
action on
\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{2})
which acts on
B_{2}
with weight
1
and fixes all other arrows, this
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action can be used to construct specialization map (as in
[
COZZ1
, Def. 7.14]
):

\mathsf{sp}\colon H^{\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{2}),B_{1}\Phi A_{1}+B_{2}\Phi^{N}A_{2})\to H^{\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{2}),B_{1}\Phi A_{1}).

Passing to a fixed locus, the induced specialization map is given by

\displaystyle\operatorname{id}\otimes\>\mathsf{sp}^{\prime}\colon H^{\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1}),B_{1}\Phi A_{1})\otimes H^{\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1}),B_{2}\Phi^{N}A_{2})\longrightarrow H^{\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1}),B_{1}\Phi A_{1})\otimes H^{\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1})),

where
\mathsf{sp}^{\prime}\colon H^{\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1}),B_{2}\Phi^{N}A_{2})\to H^{\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1}))
is the specialization map induced from the aforementioned
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action. By
[
COZZ1
, Ex. 7.19]
, we also have

\mathsf{sp}\colon H^{\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1}),B_{2}\Phi^{N}A_{2})\to H^{\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1})),

which is induced by the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action that scales every arrow with weight
1
. We claim that
\mathsf{sp}=\mathsf{sp}^{\prime}
. In fact, by

[
COZZ1
, Rmk. 7.16]
, we have
\mathsf{sp}\circ\operatorname{can}=\mathsf{sp}^{\prime}\circ\operatorname{can}=
pushforward map from
Z(B_{2}\Phi^{N}A_{2})
to
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1})
. Note that

\operatorname{can}\colon H^{\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(Z(B_{2}\Phi^{N}A_{2}))\to H^{\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1}),B_{2}\Phi^{N}A_{2})

is surjective because the composition

H^{\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(Z_{n})\to H^{\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(Z(B_{2}\Phi^{N}A_{2}))\xrightarrow{\operatorname{can}}H^{\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1}),B_{2}\Phi^{N}A_{2})

is surjective. This proves the claim. Then the result follows from
[
COZZ1
, Prop. 7.18]
.
∎

Write
\bar{\mathbf{x}}:=\hbar\mathbf{x}
, and let the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{gl}_{2}=\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{\mathbf{e}_{ij}\}_{i,j\in\{1,2\}}
act on
\mathcal{H}_{N}=\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\{1,\bar{\mathbf{x}},\ldots,\bar{\mathbf{x}}^{N-1},\bar{\mathbf{x}}^{N}\}
by

\displaystyle\mathbf{e}_{11}\mapsto 1-\bar{\mathbf{x}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\mathbf{x}}},\quad\mathbf{e}_{12}\mapsto\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\mathbf{x}}},\quad\mathbf{e}_{21}\mapsto\bar{\mathbf{x}}\left(N-\bar{\mathbf{x}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\mathbf{x}}}\right),\quad\mathbf{e}_{22}\mapsto 1-N+\bar{\mathbf{x}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\mathbf{x}}}.

Then
R_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}_{N}}(u)
takes the form:

(9.9)

\displaystyle R_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}_{N}}(u)=\frac{u\operatorname{id}+\hbar\>\Omega}{u+\hbar},\quad\,\,\Omega=\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{e}_{ij}\otimes\mathbf{e}_{ji}.

In particular,

(9.10)

R_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}_{1}}(u)=\frac{u\operatorname{id}+\hbar P}{u+\hbar},\quad\,\,P(\bar{\mathbf{x}}^{i}\otimes\bar{\mathbf{x}}^{j})=\bar{\mathbf{x}}^{j}\otimes\bar{\mathbf{x}}^{i}.

Remark 9.9
.

Another way to obtain
R_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}_{1}}(u)
is by dimensional reduction (
[
COZZ1
, Thm. 6.10]
) to the Nakajima quiver with one node and without edge. The corresponding
R
-matrix is computed in
[
MO
, §4.1.2]
.

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\qquad\mathsf{W}=B_{1}\Phi A_{1}.

Let
\mathsf{A}\cong\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
act on the framing by
u\underline{\mathbf{1}}+\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}}
.
In the basis
[Z_{0}]\otimes[\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}})]
and
[Z_{1}]\otimes[\mathcal{M}(n-1,\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}})]
,
\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}
is given by the matrix

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\prod_{j=0}^{n-1}(j\hbar-u)&n\hbar\prod_{j=0}^{n-2}(j\hbar-u)\\
0&\prod_{j=0}^{n-2}((j-1)\hbar-u)\end{pmatrix},

\operatorname{Stab}_{u<0}
is given by the matrix

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}(u-(i-1)\hbar)&0\\
\prod_{i=0}^{n-2}(u-(i-1)\hbar)&\quad(u-(n-1)\hbar)\prod_{i=0}^{n-2}(u-(i-1)\hbar)\end{pmatrix}.

Using the normalizer (
9.6
), we have

\displaystyle R_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{F}}(u)=\operatorname{Stab}_{u<0,\epsilon}^{-1}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0,\epsilon}=\frac{1}{u+\hbar}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}u-(n-1)\hbar&\,\,n\hbar\\
-1&\,\,1\\
\end{array}\right).

Remark 9.10
.

\mathsf{A}
-action on the affine quotient
\mathcal{M}_{0}(n,\underline{\mathbf{1}}^{\mathbf{1}})
is attracting. By
[
COZZ1
, Prop. 3.35]
,
\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0}^{-1}
is defined for integral cohomology classes. Thus
R_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{F}}(u)^{-1}\in\operatorname{End}(\mathcal{H}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{F})[u]
is a polynomial in
u
. Direct computations give

\displaystyle R_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{F}}(u)^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&\,\,-n\hbar\\
1&\,\,u-(n-1)\hbar\\
\end{array}\right).

Using the basis
[\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}})]=\frac{\mathbf{x}^{n}}{n!}
in (
9.3
), we can write
R_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{F}}(u)
in the following compact form

\displaystyle R_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{F}}(u)=\frac{1}{u+\hbar}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}u+\hbar-\mathbf{x}\mathbf{y}&\,\,\hbar\mathbf{x}\\
-\mathbf{y}/\hbar&\,\,1\\
\end{array}\right),

where
\mathbf{y}=\hbar\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{x}}
acts on
\mathbb{C}[\hbar][\mathbf{x}]
as a differential operator.

9.4.
Frassek-Pestun-Tsymbaliuk shifted Yangian

Let

(9.13)

R(z)=z\operatorname{id}+\hbar P\in\operatorname{End}(\mathbb{C}^{2})^{\otimes 2}[z,\hbar].

Here
P=\sum_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant 2}E_{ij}\otimes E_{ji}
is the permutation operator, where
E_{ij}
is the matrix with
1
at
(i,j)
place and zero elsewhere.

Definition 9.11

(
[
FPT
, §2.3]
)
.

Fix
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leqslant 0}
. The Frassek-Pestun-Tsymbaliuk
\mu
-
shifted Yangian

Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
is a
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
algebra generated by
\left\{e_{r},f_{r},g_{1,r},g_{2,s}\right\}_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0},s\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant-\mu}}
, subject to relations

(9.14)

\displaystyle R(u-v)\,T_{1}(u)\,T_{2}(v)=T_{2}(v)\,T_{1}(u)\,R(u-v),

with
T(z)\in Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})[\![z,z^{-1}]\!]\otimes\operatorname{End}(\mathbb{C}^{2})
defined via

\displaystyle T(z)=F(z)\,G(z)\,E(z),\,\,\,\text{where}\,\,\,E(z)=\operatorname{id}_{2}+e(z)\otimes E_{12},\,\,\,F(z)=\operatorname{id}_{2}+f(z)\otimes E_{21},\,\,\,G(z)=\sum_{i=1}^{2}g_{i}(z)\otimes E_{ii},

and

(9.15)

\displaystyle e(z)=\sum_{r\geqslant 0}e_{r}z^{-r-1},\quad f(z)=\sum_{r\geqslant 0}f_{r}z^{-r-1},\quad g_{1}(z)=1+\sum_{r\geqslant 0}g_{1,r}z^{-r-1},\quad g_{2}(z)=z^{\mu}+\sum_{s\geqslant-\mu}g_{2,s}z^{-s-1}\,.

Here (
9.14
) is an equation in
Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})[\![u,v,u^{-1},v^{-1}]\!]\otimes\operatorname{End}(\mathbb{C}^{2})^{\otimes 2}
.

The
\mu
-
shifted Yangian

Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
is the
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
-subalgebra of
Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
generated by
\left\{e_{r},f_{r}\right\}_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
and the coefficients of
h(z)=g_{1}(z)^{-1}g_{2}(z)
.
When
\mu=0
, we also write

Y(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})=Y_{0}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
,
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})=Y_{0}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
.

Next we explain how to describe modules of Frassek-Pestun-Tsymbaliuk shifted Yangian via stable envelopes.
Consider the following framed quiver with
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}\geqslant\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}\in\mathbb{N}
:

where
\mathbb{C}Q^{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}
is the path algebra of the Crawley-Boevey quiver
Q^{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}
defined in
beginning of §
3
. We assume that
\mathsf{W}
is
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}
-invariant, where
\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}
is a torus that acts on
\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}}
and
\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}}
(i.e. framing torus),

\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
scales

\Phi
with weight
-1
(its action on
A
,
B
is arbitrary as long as preserving
\mathsf{w}
). This implies that

\mathsf{W}\big|_{A=0,B=0}=0,

since for any
k>0
,
\operatorname{tr}(\Phi^{k})
is not
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
-invariant. Denote
\mathsf{w}=\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{W})
.

Consider the state space

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}:=H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}).

When
\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}
is the identity group, we write
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}=\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
.

\mathcal{H}_{1}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
by considering the following quiver with potential:
