Let
\mathsf{A}\cong\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
act on the framing by
u\underline{\mathbf{1}}+\underline{\mathbf{d}}
and
\mathsf{T}=\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}
. Applying the deformed dimensional reduction (
[
COZZ1
, Thm. C.1]
) to
\operatorname{tr}(B_{1}\Phi A_{1})\boxplus\mathsf{w}
along
B_{1}
, we get

\displaystyle H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(m,\underline{\mathbf{1}})\times\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\operatorname{tr}(B_{1}\Phi A_{1})\boxplus\mathsf{w})\cong H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}),\quad m\in\{0,1\}

and this implies that

\mathcal{H}_{1}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{C}[u]\cong H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{1}}+\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}}),\;\text{ for }\;\mathsf{w}^{\prime}=\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{W}^{\prime})

In the basis
[Z_{0}]
,
[Z_{1}]
, the stable envelope with normalizer (
9.6
) in the
u>0
chamber takes the matrix form:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0,\epsilon}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}(-1)^{n}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N^{-}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}(0,\mathbf{1})\times\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}})/\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}}+\underline{\mathbf{1}})})&\star_{1}\\
0&(-1)^{n-1}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N^{-}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}(1,\mathbf{1})\times\mathcal{M}(n-1,\underline{\mathbf{d}})/\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}}+\underline{\mathbf{1}})})\\
\end{array}\right),

with
\deg_{u}\star_{1}<\operatorname{rk}N^{-}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}(0,\mathbf{1})\times\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}})/\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}}+\underline{\mathbf{1}})}
.
In the ample partial order (
[
COZZ1
, Rmk. 3.8]
), we have

\mathcal{M}(1,\mathbf{1})\times\mathcal{M}(n-1,\underline{\mathbf{d}})<\mathcal{M}(0,\mathbf{1})\times\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}}),

so the

21
-component of
\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0,\epsilon}
is zero due to axiom (i) in
[
COZZ1
, Def. 3.4]
. Similarly

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{u<0,\epsilon}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N^{+}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}(0,\mathbf{1})\times\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}})/\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}}+\underline{\mathbf{1}})})&0\\
\star_{2}&(-1)^{\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N^{+}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}(1,\mathbf{1})\times\mathcal{M}(n-1,\underline{\mathbf{d}})/\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}}+\underline{\mathbf{1}})})\\
\end{array}\right),

with
\deg_{u}\star_{2}<\operatorname{rk}N^{+}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}(1,\mathbf{1})\times\mathcal{M}(n-1,\underline{\mathbf{d}})/\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}}+\underline{\mathbf{1}})}
.

Proposition 9.12
.

For arbitrary
\underline{\mathbf{d}}
and
\mathsf{W}
in (
9.16
), the map

(9.18)

\begin{split}e(u)\mapsto\frac{(-1)^{n}\star_{1}}{e^{\mathsf{T}}(N^{-}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}(0,\mathbf{1})\times\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}})/\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}}+\underline{\mathbf{1}})})},&\qquad f(u)\mapsto\frac{(-1)^{\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}-1}\star_{2}}{e^{\mathsf{T}}(N^{+}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}(1,\mathbf{1})\times\mathcal{M}(n-1,\underline{\mathbf{d}})/\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}}+\underline{\mathbf{1}})})},\\
g_{1}(u)\mapsto\frac{(-1)^{n}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N^{-}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}(0,\mathbf{1})\times\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}})/\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}}+\underline{\mathbf{1}})})}{e^{\mathsf{T}}(N^{+}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}(0,\mathbf{1})\times\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}})/\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}}+\underline{\mathbf{1}})})},&\qquad g_{2}(u)\mapsto\frac{(-1)^{n+\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}-1}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N^{-}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}(1,\mathbf{1})\times\mathcal{M}(n-1,\underline{\mathbf{d}})/\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}}+\underline{\mathbf{1}})})}{e^{\mathsf{T}}(N^{+}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{M}(1,\mathbf{1})\times\mathcal{M}(n-1,\underline{\mathbf{d}})/\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}}+\underline{\mathbf{1}})})},\end{split}

gives an action of
Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
on
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
with
\mu=\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}
. Here all rational functions are expanded in
u\to\infty
.

Proof.

e(u)
and
f(u)
have the correct form of power series in
u
since

\lim_{u\to\infty}e(u)=0=\lim_{u\to\infty}f(u).

The leading terms in the
u\to\infty
of
g_{1}(u)
and
g_{2}(u)
are
1
and
u^{\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}}
respectively. Thus the image of
e(u)
,
f(u)
, and
g_{i}(u)
are power series of the form (
9.15
). By a direct calculation, under the map (
9.18
), we have

T(u)=F(u)\,G(u)\,E(u)\mapsto R_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}}(u)=\left(\operatorname{Stab}_{u<0,\epsilon}\right)^{-1}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0,\epsilon}.

By the braid relation (
2.8
), the RTT relation holds:

\displaystyle R_{\mathcal{H}^{(1)}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{(2)}_{1}}(u-v)\,R_{\mathcal{H}^{(1)}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}}(u)\,R_{\mathcal{H}^{(2)}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}}(v)=R_{\mathcal{H}^{(2)}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}}(v)\,R_{\mathcal{H}^{(1)}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}}(u)\,R_{\mathcal{H}^{(1)}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{(2)}_{1}}(u-v).

By (
9.10
),
R_{\mathcal{H}^{(1)}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{(2)}_{1}}(z)
coincides with (
9.13
) (up to a constant).
Then the proposition follows from Definition
9.11
.
∎

Remark 9.13
.

For the Jordan quiver
Q
with zero potential,
\mathcal{H}_{1}
is admissible by Definition
3.13
, so the maps in (
9.18
) induce a surjective algebra map from
Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
to
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,0)
for the Jordan quiver
Q
, by Proposition
9.12
and Theorem
4.44
.

Example 9.14
.

In the examples of
\mathcal{V}
,
\mathcal{H}_{N}
and
\mathcal{F}
, we can explicitly pin down their shifted Yangian module structures by comparing the
R
-matrices (
9.3.1
), (
9.9
) and (
9.3.3
) with the known Lax matrices
[
FPT
]
. For simplicity, let us
restrict to the subalgebra
Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
, which is also denoted
as
Y(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
when
\mu=0
. Then we have

•

\tau_{\hbar}^{*}\mathcal{V}
is isomorphic to the universal dual Verma module with highest weight
t
induced by the evaluation map
Y(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})\to U(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})[\hbar]
;

•

\tau_{\hbar}^{*}\mathcal{H}_{N}
is isomorphic to the
N
-th symmetric power of the fundamental representation
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]^{\oplus 2}
of
Y(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
induced by the evaluation map
Y(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})\to U(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})[\hbar]
;

•

\tau_{\hbar}^{*}\mathcal{F}
is isomorphic to the predundamental module
L^{-}_{0}
of
Y_{-1}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
.

Here the automorphism is given by:

\tau_{a}\colon Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})\cong Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2}),\quad T(z)\mapsto T(z-a).

It induces a pullback map on Yangian modules:

(9.19)

\tau_{a}^{*}\colon Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})\mathrm{-Mod}\cong Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})\mathrm{-Mod}.

The subalgebra
Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
is
\tau_{a}
invariant, so
\tau_{a}^{*}
restricts to an automorphism
Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})\mathrm{-Mod}\cong Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})\mathrm{-Mod}
.

Remark 9.15
.

The images of
g_{1}(u)
and
g_{2}(u)
can be written as tautological classes on
\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}})
:

\displaystyle g_{1}(u)\mapsto c_{-1/u}\left((1-\hbar^{-1})\mathsf{V}\right),\quad g_{2}(u)\mapsto u^{\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}}\cdot c_{-1/u}\left((1-\hbar)\mathsf{V}+\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{out}}-\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in}}\right),

where
\mathsf{V}
is the descent of
\mathbb{C}^{n}
to the GIT quotient
\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}})
,
\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in}}
and
\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{out}}
are the pullback of the in-coming and out-going framing vector spaces to
\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}})
respectively.
\mathsf{V}
,
\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in}}
, and
\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{out}}
are
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-equivariant vector bundles on
\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}})
, and
c_{-1/u}(\cdots)
is the
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-equivariant Chern polynomial with variable
-1/u
. For example,

\displaystyle g_{1,0}\mapsto-\hbar\mathbf{v},\quad g_{2,-\mu}\mapsto c_{1}(\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in}})-c_{1}(\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{out}})+\hbar\mathbf{v}\,.

In the case of
\mu=0
, comparison with the Lie algebra generators in Proposition
4.23
gives:

g_{1,0}\mapsto-\hbar\mathsf{v}
,
g_{2,0}\mapsto\mathsf{d}+\hbar\mathsf{v}
, and

(9.20)

\displaystyle-g_{2,0}\otimes g_{1,0}-g_{1,0}\otimes g_{2,0}-2g_{1,0}\otimes g_{1,0}+e_{0}\otimes f_{0}+f_{0}\otimes e_{0}\mapsto\hbar\,\boldsymbol{r}\,.

Remark 9.16
.

Proposition
9.12
is obtained by
[
YZJ
]
in the special case when

\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}=\mathbf{d}
and
\mathsf{W}=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathbf{d}}B_{i}\Phi^{N_{i}}A_{i}
.
It is proven in
loc. cit.
that
Y(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
acts on
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{{\mathbf{d}}}
, and that the action makes
\mathcal{H}_{N}
the
N
-th symmetric power of the fundamental module through the evaluation map
Y(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})\to U(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})[\hbar]
. The method in
loc. cit.
is different from ours. They use dimensional reduction to the Borel-Moore homology of zero locus of
\{\Phi^{N_{i}}A_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\mathbf{d}}
, construct correspondences for the generators and check relations.

9.5.
Coproducts and stable envelopes

Frassek-Pestun-Tsymbaliuk shifted Yangians possess the following coproduct maps
[
FPT
, Prop. 2.316]
:

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mu^{\prime},\mu^{\prime\prime}}\colon Y_{\mu^{\prime}+\mu^{\prime\prime}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})\to Y_{\mu^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})\otimes Y_{\mu^{\prime\prime}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2}),\qquad T(z)\mapsto T(z)\otimes T(z).

The coproduct induces a tensor product on the module categories:

\displaystyle\left(Y_{\mu^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})\mathrm{-Mod}\right)\;\times\;\left(Y_{\mu^{\prime\prime}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})\mathrm{-Mod}\right)\;\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\otimes\hskip 8.19447pt}\;Y_{\mu^{\prime}+\mu^{\prime\prime}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})\mathrm{-Mod}.

On the geometry side, we have stable envelope:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{a>0,\epsilon}\colon\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}+\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}},

which is defined using the torus
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a}
action on the framing:
a\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}+\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}
with normalizer (
9.6
).
By §
4.2
, we have:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{a>0,\epsilon}^{-1}\,R_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}+\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}}}(u)\,\operatorname{Stab}_{a>0,\epsilon}=R_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}}}(u)\,R_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}}}(u-a).

Thus
\operatorname{Stab}_{a>0,\epsilon}
induces a
Y_{\mu^{\prime}+\mu^{\prime\prime}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
-module map (with
\mu^{\prime}=(0,\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}^{\prime}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}^{\prime})
,
\mu^{\prime\prime}=(0,\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}^{\prime\prime}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}^{\prime\prime})
):

\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}}\otimes\tau_{a}^{*}\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}+\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}},

where
\tau_{a}^{*}
is the Yangian module pullback map (
9.19
).

Similarly,
\operatorname{Stab}_{a<0,\epsilon}
induces a
Y_{\mu^{\prime}+\mu^{\prime\prime}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
-module map

\tau_{a}^{*}\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}}\to\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}+\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}}.

Proposition 9.17
.

The
R
-matrix

\displaystyle R_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}},\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}}}(a):=\left(\operatorname{Stab}_{a<0,\epsilon}\right)^{-1}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{a>0,\epsilon}\colon\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}}\otimes\tau_{a}^{*}\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\tau_{a}^{*}\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}}

is a
Y_{\mu^{\prime}+\mu^{\prime\prime}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
-module map.

Moreover, if
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}^{\prime}=0
then
R_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}},\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}}}(a)
is a polynomial in
a
; if
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}^{\prime\prime}=0
then
R_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}},\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}}}(a)^{-1}
is a polynomial in
a
.

Proof.

The statement on intertwining property follows from the above discussions. The statement on polynomiality follows from

[
COZZ1
, Prop. 3.35]
.
∎

Remark 9.18
.

Similarly as
[
MO
, Prop. 4.5.1]
, the above intertwiners are unitary:

\displaystyle R_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}},\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}}}(a)^{-1}=R_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}},\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}}}(-a).

Example 9.19
.

Write
\mathcal{F}^{(i)}=\mathbb{C}[\hbar][\mathbf{x}_{i}]
and
\mathbf{y}_{i}=\hbar\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{x}_{i}}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
, so we have
\mathcal{F}^{(1)}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{(2)}=\mathbb{C}[\hbar][\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2}]
. Then the
R
-matrix takes the following explicit form

(9.21)

R_{\mathcal{F}^{(1)},\mathcal{F}^{(2)}}(a)=\left(1-\mathbf{x}_{2}\mathbf{y}_{1}\right)^{a/\hbar}S_{12},

where
S_{12}(f(\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2}))=f(\mathbf{x}_{2},\mathbf{x}_{1})
.
In fact, by direct computations,
\left(1-\mathbf{x}_{2}\mathbf{y}_{1}\right)^{a/\hbar}S_{12}
solves the intertwining equation:

\displaystyle\left(1-\mathbf{x}_{2}\mathbf{y}_{1}\right)^{\frac{u-v}{\hbar}}\,S_{12}\,R_{\mathcal{F}^{(1)},\mathcal{H}_{1}}(u)\,R_{\mathcal{F}^{(2)},\mathcal{H}_{1}}(v)=R_{\mathcal{F}^{(2)},\mathcal{H}_{1}}(v)\,R_{\mathcal{F}^{(1)},\mathcal{H}_{1}}(u)\left(1-\mathbf{x}_{2}\mathbf{y}_{1}\right)^{\frac{u-v}{\hbar}}\,S_{12}.

An intertwiner that maps
1\in\mathbb{C}[\hbar][\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2}]
to itself is unique by
[
HZ
, Thm. 5.2 (i)]
, so (
9.21
) holds.

9.6.
Proof of Theorem
9.1

Theorem
9.1
follows from Remark
9.13
and the following Proposition
9.20
.

Proposition 9.20
.

For arbitrary
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leqslant 0}
, the natural map

\displaystyle\iota\colon Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})\to\prod_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{W},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\operatorname{End}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}})

is injective, where the product is for all
\underline{\mathbf{d}}
and
\mathsf{W}
in (
9.16
) such that
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mu
.

Proof.

We prove this by induction on
\mu
. When
\mu=0
, we can restrict to representations of the form

\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\cong\tau^{*}_{a_{1}}\mathcal{H}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\tau^{*}_{a_{n}}\mathcal{H}_{1},

then the induced map

\displaystyle Y_{0}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})\to\prod_{a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}}\operatorname{End}(\tau^{*}_{a_{1}}\mathcal{H}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\tau^{*}_{a_{n}}\mathcal{H}_{1})

is injective by
[
MO
, §11.3.2]
.

For
\mu<0
, assume that
\iota
is injective for
\mu^{\prime}=\mu+1
, we take representations of the form

\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}\cong\tau^{*}_{a_{1}}\mathcal{F}\otimes\tau^{*}_{a_{2}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}},

where
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}^{\prime}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}^{\prime}=\mu^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{F}
is prefundamental module (
9.2
). Here the tensor product is induced by the stable envelope as in the previous subsection. Then the action of
Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
on
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
factors through

\displaystyle Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})\xrightarrow{\Delta}Y_{(0,1)}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})\otimes Y_{\mu^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})\twoheadrightarrow D\otimes Y_{\mu^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})

where
D=\mathbb{C}[\hbar]\langle\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\rangle/([\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}]-\hbar)
is the Weyl algebra. By induction and the fact that
D
-action on
\mathcal{F}
is faithful, the induced map

\displaystyle D\otimes Y_{\mu^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})\to\prod_{a_{1},a_{2},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime},\mathsf{w}^{\prime}}\operatorname{End}(\tau^{*}_{a_{1}}\mathcal{F}\otimes\tau^{*}_{a_{2}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}})

is injective. Then it is enough to show that
Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})\to D\otimes Y_{\mu^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
is injective.

By the flatness over
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
, it suffices to prove the injectivity after passing to the classical limit
\hbar=0
. It is known that
Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})/(\hbar)
is a commutative algebra, which is the function ring of a pro-finite type affine variety
\mathcal{W}_{\mu}
.
\mathcal{W}_{\mu}
is the closed subscheme of the group ind-scheme
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\!(z^{-1})\!)
consisting of matrices of the form

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1+a(z)&\,\,b(z)\\
c(z)&\,\,*\\
\end{array}\right)\text{ with determinant }z^{-m}(1+d(z)),

where
a(z),b(z),c(z),d(z)\in z^{-1}\mathbb{C}[\![z^{-1}]\!]
are power series. The classical limit of
D
becomes polynomial ring of two variables, which is naturally identified with the subscheme
\mathcal{W}^{(1,0)}_{(0,1)}
in
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\!(z^{-1})\!)
consisting of matrices of the form

\displaystyle\frac{1}{z}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}z+xy&\,\,x\\
y&\,\,1\\
\end{array}\right),\quad(x,y)\in\mathbb{C}^{2}.

The
\hbar=0
limit of the map
Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})\to D\otimes Y_{\mu^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
is the map between function rings induced by the multiplication in
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\!(z^{-1})\!)
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}^{(1,0)}_{(0,1)}\times\mathcal{W}_{\mu^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{W}_{\mu}\>.

It is elementary to see that the above map is surjective. Since
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{W}_{\mu}]
is an integral domain, the induced map
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{W}_{\mu}]\to\mathbb{C}\left[\mathcal{W}^{(1,0)}_{(0,1)}\right]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{W}_{\mu^{\prime}}]
is injective.
∎

9.7.
Specialization maps as modules intertwiners

We generalize the construction of Proposition
9.8
,
which will be used in §
9.8
.
Take a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}})
that scales on
B
in the quiver
(
9.16
) with weight
1
and fixes all other arrows.
Let
\mathsf{w}=\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{W})
and
\mathsf{T}_{0}
be as in (
9.16
). This
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action fits into the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action in
[
COZZ1
, Setting 7.1]
,
and it can be used to construct specialization map (as in
[
COZZ1
, Def. 7.14]
):

(9.22)

\displaystyle\mathsf{sp}\colon\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}=H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}}))=\mathcal{H}^{0}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}.

Proposition 9.21
.

We have

R_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{0}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}}(u)\circ(\operatorname{id}\otimes\>\mathsf{sp})=(\operatorname{id}\otimes\>\mathsf{sp})\circ R_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}}(u).

In particular,
\mathsf{sp}
is a
Y_{\mu}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
-module map.

Proof.

Consider the quiver (
9.17
) and its moduli space
\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}}+\underline{\mathbf{1}})
with
\mathsf{T}=\mathsf{A}\times\mathsf{T}_{0}
, where

\mathsf{A}\cong\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
acts on the framing by

u\underline{\mathbf{1}}+\underline{\mathbf{d}}
.
Take another
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action which acts on
B
in the quiver (
9.17
) with weight
1
and fixes all other arrows.
As in
[
COZZ1
, Def. 7.14]
, there is a specialization map:

\displaystyle H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}}+\underline{\mathbf{1}}),B_{1}\Phi A_{1}+\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{m}))\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}}+\underline{\mathbf{1}}),B_{1}\Phi A_{1}).

Passing to
\mathsf{A}
-fixed loci, the induced specialization map is given by

\displaystyle\operatorname{id}\otimes\>\mathsf{sp}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1}),B_{1}\Phi A_{1})\otimes H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{m}))\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1}),B_{1}\Phi A_{1})\otimes H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}}))

where
\mathsf{sp}
is the map defined in (
9.22
). Then the result follows from

[
COZZ1
, Prop. 7.18]
.
∎

Remark 9.22
.

Same argument shows that

(9.23)

\displaystyle R_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}},\mathcal{H}^{0}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}}(u)\circ(\operatorname{id}\otimes\>\mathsf{sp})=(\operatorname{id}\otimes\>\mathsf{sp})\circ R_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\prime}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}},\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}}(u)

for arbitrary
\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{W}^{\prime}
in (
9.16
).

9.8.
Fusion procedure from geometry

It is known that an intertwiner between
\mathcal{H}_{N}
and
\mathcal{H}_{M}
can be obtained from the elementary
R
-matrix

R(u)=u\operatorname{id}+\hbar P,

via the
fusion procedure

[
KRS
]
. In this section, we derive the fusion procedure geometrically.

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\qquad\mathsf{W}_{*}=B\Phi A-\Xi BA,\quad\mathsf{w}_{*}=\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{W}_{*}),

where
\Xi\in\operatorname{End}(\mathbb{C}^{N})
is a fixed nilpotent matrix of rank
(N-1)
. Let
q\in\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
act on quiver data by

(9.24)

\displaystyle(\Phi,B,A)\mapsto(q^{-1}\Phi,q\cdot\sigma(q)B,A\sigma(q)^{-1}),

where
\sigma\colon\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\to\mathrm{GL}_{N}
is a homomorphism such that
\Xi
is eigenvector under the adjoint action of
\sigma(\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar})
with eigenvalue
1
. Given a basis
\mathbb{C}^{N}=\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{N}\}
such that
\Xi(e_{i})=e_{i+1}
, we can take
\sigma(q)=\operatorname{diag}(1,q,\ldots,q^{N-1})
.

By Proposition
9.12
, the state space

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}}}^{\mathsf{W}_{*}}:=H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w}_{*})=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w}_{*})

is equipped with a natural Yangian algebra
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
action coming from stable envelopes.
Similarly, the space (
9.1
):

\mathcal{H}_{N}=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w}_{N}),\,\,\,\mathrm{with}\,\,\,\mathsf{w}_{N}=\operatorname{tr}(B\Phi^{N}A)

is also a Yangian module.

Proposition 9.23
.

There is a
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
module isomorphism

(9.25)

\delta\colon\mathcal{H}_{N}\cong\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}}}^{\mathsf{W}_{*}},

such that

\displaystyle R_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}},\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}}}^{\mathsf{W}_{*}}}(u)\circ(\operatorname{id}\otimes\>\delta)=(\operatorname{id}\otimes\>\delta)\circ R_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}},\mathcal{H}_{N}}(u)

for arbitrary
\underline{\mathbf{d}}
and
\mathsf{W}
in (
9.16
).

We postpone the proof to the end of this section and use it for now.

Consider the same quiver variety
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N})
with the
canonical cubic potential

\mathsf{w}_{1}=\operatorname{tr}(B\Phi A)
.
By the dimensional reduction, we have an isomorphism

(9.26)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}}}^{\mathsf{W}_{1}}:=H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w}_{1})\cong H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N})),

where
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N})=\bigsqcup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{N}(m,\mathbf{N})
is the union of Nakajima varieties of
A_{1}
quiver
[
Nak1
]
.

Lemma 9.24
.

(
9.26
) is an isomorphism of
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
modules.

Proof.

By Proposition
9.12
,
\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}}}^{\mathsf{W}_{1}}
is a
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
module.
The
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
module structure on
H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N}))
is given by the stable envelope
\mathbf{Stab}_{u>0}
of
[
MO
]
for Nakajima varieties
9
9

9

\mathbf{Stab}_{u>0}=\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0,\epsilon^{\prime}}
where
\epsilon^{\prime}
is the normalizer
\epsilon^{\prime}\big|_{F}=(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}T^{1/2}|^{+}_{F/\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N}+\mathbf{1})}}
for the polarization
T^{1/2}=\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^{N+1},V)-\operatorname{End}(V)
.
.
Then the statement follows from commutative diagram:

\textstyle{H^{\mathbb{C}_{\hbar}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1})\times\mathcal{M}(m,\mathbf{N}),B_{1}\Phi A_{1}+\operatorname{tr}(B\Phi A))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{u>0,\epsilon}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathbb{C}_{\hbar}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(\mathcal{M}(n+m,\mathbf{1}+\mathbf{N}),B_{1}\Phi A_{1}+\operatorname{tr}(B\Phi A))}
\textstyle{H^{\mathbb{C}_{\hbar}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(\mathcal{N}(n,\mathbf{1})\times\mathcal{N}(m,\mathbf{N}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Stab}_{u>0}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H^{\mathbb{C}_{\hbar}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(\mathcal{N}(n+m,\mathbf{1}+\mathbf{N})),\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

where
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
acts on the framing by
u\underline{\mathbf{1}}+\mathbf{N}
, and vertical isomorphisms are dimensional reduction maps
(see
[
COZZ1
, Thm. 6.10]
, Example
2.4
and
[
COZZ1
, Rmk. 9.14]
).
∎

We relate
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
modules
\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}}}^{\mathsf{W}_{1}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{1}
.

Lemma 9.25
.

There is a
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
module isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}},\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathsf{W}_{1}}\cong\tau^{*}_{(N-1)\hbar}\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\tau^{*}_{\hbar}\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}\>.

Here “loc” means
-\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
, and tensor products are taken over the base field
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
.

Proof.

Consider the Nakajima variety associated to the following quiver

n
N
A
B

and let
(q,p)\in\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}
act on quiver data by

\displaystyle(B,A)\mapsto(q\cdot\sigma(p)B,A\sigma(p)^{-1}),

where
\sigma
is the cocharacter of
\mathrm{GL}_{N}
in (
9.24
). The stable envelope

\displaystyle\mathbf{Stab}_{t<0}\colon H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N})^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}})\to H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N}))

is a
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
-module map by the argument in Section
4.2
. Evaluation at
t=\hbar
amounts to taking equivariant cohomologies with respect to the diagonal subtorus
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{diag}}\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}
. Since
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N})^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}}=\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{1})^{\times N}
, with diagonal
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
weights
\{0,1,\ldots,N-1\}
on the one-dimensional framing vector spaces,
we get a
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
-module map:

\displaystyle\mathbf{Stab}_{t<0}\colon\tau^{*}_{(N-1)\hbar}\mathcal{H}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\tau^{*}_{\hbar}\mathcal{H}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1}\to H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N})).

It is elementary to see that the induced
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}
action on the diagonal
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
fixed locus
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N})^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar,\operatorname{diag}}}
is repelling. Then by
[
COZZ1
, Prop. 3.36]
, the above map is injective after localization.
By dimension counting:

\dim_{\mathbb{C}(\hbar)}\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}^{\otimes N}=2^{N}=\dim_{\mathbb{C}(\hbar)}H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N}))_{\mathrm{loc}},

the map

\displaystyle\mathbf{Stab}_{t<0}\colon\tau^{*}_{(N-1)\hbar}\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\tau^{*}_{\hbar}\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}\to H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N}))_{\mathrm{loc}}

is an isomorphism. Combing with dimensional reduction (
9.26
), we get the desired module isomorphism.
∎

Remark 9.26
.

Since stable envelopes are compatible with dimensional reductions (
[
COZZ1
, Thm. 6.10]
), the map

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{t<0,\epsilon}\colon H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N})^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}},\mathsf{w}_{1})\big|_{t=\hbar}\to H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w}_{1})\big|_{t=\hbar}

is an isomorphism after localization. The braid relation (
2.8
) implies:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{t<0,\epsilon}^{-1}\,R_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}},\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}_{1}}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}}}}(u)\,\operatorname{Stab}_{t<0,\epsilon}=R_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}},\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(1)}}(u-(N-1)\hbar)\cdots R_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}},\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(N-1)}}(u-\hbar)\,R_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}},\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(N)}}(u)

for arbitrary
\underline{\mathbf{d}}
and
\mathsf{W}
in (
9.16
).

Consider specialization maps (
9.22
) for
\mathsf{W}_{*}
and
\mathsf{W}_{1}
:

\displaystyle\mathsf{sp}_{*}\colon\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}}}^{\mathsf{W}_{*}}\to\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}}}^{0},\qquad\mathsf{sp}_{1}\colon\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}}}^{\mathsf{W}_{1}}\to\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}}}^{0}.

Lemma 9.27
.

The maps
\mathsf{sp}_{*}
and
\mathsf{sp}_{1}
are injective. Moreover, the image of
\mathsf{sp}_{*}
is contained in the image of
\mathsf{sp}_{1}
.

Proof.

\bullet
(Injectivity of
\mathsf{sp}_{*}
). By Proposition
9.23
and Example
9.14
,
\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}}}^{\mathsf{W}_{*}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
is an irreducible
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
module because it is already irreducible as an
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
module.
\mathsf{sp}_{*}
is a
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
module map by Proposition
9.21
, and the degree zero component
\mathsf{sp}_{*}\colon\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}}}^{\mathsf{W}_{*}}(0)\to\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}}}^{0}(0)
is the identity map; thus
\mathsf{sp}_{*}
must be injective after localization.
\mathcal{H}_{N}
is a free
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
module as we have seen in Section
9.1
, so
\mathsf{sp}_{*}
is injective.

\bullet
(Injectivity of
\mathsf{sp}_{1}
). By the dimensional reduction argument in the proof of Lemma
9.25
,
\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}}}^{\mathsf{W}_{1}}
is isomorphic to the BM homology of Nakajima variety
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N})
, and the latter is a free
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
module by
[
Nak3
, Thm. 7.3.5]
. Then the proof of injectivity of
\mathsf{sp}_{1}
boils down to the proof of injectivity after localization.

Since
\mathsf{w}_{1}
is linear in
B
, and
B
does not enter the stability condition, by dimensional reduction along
B
, we get

H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(Z(\Phi A))\cong H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w}_{*}),

where
Z(\Phi A)
is the zero loci of
\Phi A
in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N})
, and the isomorphism is induced by the canonical map

H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(Z(\Phi A))\to H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(Z(\mathsf{w}_{*}))\xrightarrow{\operatorname{can}}H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w}).

By
[
COZZ1
, Rmk. 7.16]
, it suffices to show that the pushforward map
H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(Z(\Phi A))\to H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N}))
is injective after localization. Equivalently, we need to show that the pushforward map
H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(Z^{\prime}(\Phi A))\to H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{N}}))
is injective after localization, where
\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{N}})
is the following quiver variety:

N
\Phi
A

and
Z^{\prime}(\Phi A)
is the zero of
\Phi A
in
\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{N}})
. It is easy to see that the fixed locus
Z^{\prime}(\Phi A)^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}
is discrete, and that every connected component of
\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{N}})^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}
is proper. Note that a point class
[x]
in any proper variety
X
is nonzero in its
Borel-Moore homology. Therefore, we only need to show that every connected component
F
of
\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{N}})^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}
contains at most one point in
Z^{\prime}(\Phi A)
, i.e. the equation
\Phi A=0
has at most one solution on
F
. Explicitly,
F
is isomorphic to the following quiver variety for some
(n_{1},\ldots,n_{N},n_{N+1},\ldots)
with cyclic stability:

n_{1}
1
n_{2}
1
\cdots
\cdots
n_{N}
1
\,n_{N+1}
\cdots
A_{1}
A_{2}
A_{N}
\Phi_{1}
\Phi_{2}
\Phi_{N-1}
\Phi_{N}
\Phi_{N+1}

we see that
\Phi A=0
has at most one solution on
F
.

\bullet
(The inclusion
\operatorname{im}(\mathsf{sp}_{*})\subseteq\operatorname{im}(\mathsf{sp}_{1})
). Since
\mathsf{w}_{*}
is linear in
B
, and
B
does not enter the stability condition, we can apply the dimensional reduction along
B
and the composition of the following maps is isomorphism

H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(Z(\Phi A-A\Xi))\to H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(Z(\mathsf{w}_{*}))\xrightarrow{\operatorname{can}}H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w}_{*}),

where
Z(\Phi A-A\Xi)
is the zero loci of
\Phi A-A\Xi
in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N})
. In particular,
H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(Z(\mathsf{w}_{*}))\xrightarrow{\operatorname{can}}H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w}_{*})
is surjective. Using an auxiliary
\mathbb{C}^{*}
that scales
B
with weight
1
,
\Phi
with weight
-1
, and fixes
A
, then this
\mathbb{C}^{*}
action fits into

[
COZZ1
, Setting 7.1]
and we have a specialization map
10
10

10

However, this auxiliary
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action does not satisfy
[
COZZ1
, Assumption 7.12]
, so we can not use it to define a specialization map
H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w}_{*})\to H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w}_{1})
between critical cohomologies.

\mathsf{sp}_{Z}\colon H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(Z(\mathsf{w}_{*}))\to H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(Z(\mathsf{w}_{1})).

By
[
COZZ1
, Rmk. 7.16]
,
\mathsf{sp}_{*}\circ\operatorname{can}=
pushforward from
Z(\mathsf{w}_{*})
to
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N})
, and
\mathsf{sp}_{1}\circ\operatorname{can}=
pushforward from
Z(\mathsf{w}_{1})
to
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N})
. Combining with
[
COZZ1
, Rmk. 7.9]
, the following diagram

\textstyle{H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(Z(\mathsf{w}_{*}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}_{Z}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(Z(\mathsf{w}_{1}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w}_{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}_{*}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N}))}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w}_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}_{1}}

commutes. Then the inclusion
\operatorname{im}(\mathsf{sp}_{*})\subseteq\operatorname{im}(\mathsf{sp}_{1})
follows from the surjectivity of canonical maps.
∎

By Lemma
9.27
, we have an induced injective map

\displaystyle\overline{\mathsf{sp}}\colon\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}}}^{\mathsf{W}_{*}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}}}^{\mathsf{W}_{1}}.

The equation (
9.23
) implies that

(9.27)

\displaystyle R_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}},\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}}}^{\mathsf{W}_{1}}}(u)\circ(\operatorname{id}\otimes\>\overline{\mathsf{sp}})=(\operatorname{id}\otimes\>\overline{\mathsf{sp}})\circ R_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}},\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}}}^{\mathsf{W}_{*}}}(u)

for arbitrary
\underline{\mathbf{d}}
and
\mathsf{W}
in (
9.16
). In particular,
\overline{\mathsf{sp}}
is a
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
module map.

To state the fusion formula, we define
\eta
to be the composition

(9.28)

\displaystyle\eta\colon\mathcal{H}_{N,\mathrm{loc}}\xrightarrow[\cong]{\eqref{equ on delt}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}_{*}}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}},\mathrm{loc}}\overset{\overline{\mathsf{sp}}}{\hookrightarrow}\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}_{1}}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}},\mathrm{loc}}\xrightarrow[\cong]{\text{Lem.\,}\ref{lem tensor N H_1}}\tau^{*}_{(N-1)\hbar}\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\tau^{*}_{\hbar}\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}\>.

Then
\eta
is a
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
module embedding.

Theorem 9.28
.

\eta
induces an isomorphism

\eta\colon\mathcal{H}_{N,\mathrm{loc}}\cong S^{N}(\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}})

to the
N
-th symmetric tensor
S^{N}(\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}})
of
\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}
. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle(\operatorname{id}\otimes\>\eta)\circ R_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}},\mathcal{H}_{N}}(u)=R_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}},\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(1)}}(u-(N-1)\hbar)\cdots R_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}},\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(N-1)}}(u-\hbar)\,R_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}},\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(N)}}(u)\circ(\operatorname{id}\otimes\>\eta)\,,

for arbitrary
\underline{\mathbf{d}}
and
\mathsf{W}
in (
9.16
).
Here
\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(i)}
denotes the
i
-th component of the RHS of (
9.28
).

Proof.

As
Y(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[h]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
modules,
\mathcal{H}_{N,\mathrm{loc}}
is abstractly isomorphic to
S^{N}(\mathbb{C}(\hbar)^{\oplus 2})
induced by the evaluation map
Y(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[h]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)\to U(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})\otimes\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
(Example
9.14
). So the image of
\eta
must be
S^{N}(\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}})
as it is the unique summand of
\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}^{\otimes N}
which is isomorphic to
S^{N}(\mathbb{C}(\hbar)^{\oplus 2})
as
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
module. The rest follows from what we have established.
∎

Taking
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}=\mathcal{H}_{M}
in the above theorem, we obtain:

Corollary 9.29
.

We have the
fusion formula
for the
R
-
matrix

R_{\mathcal{H}_{M},\mathcal{H}_{N}}(u)
:

\displaystyle(\eta\otimes\eta)\circ R_{\mathcal{H}_{M},\mathcal{H}_{N}}(u)=\left(\prod_{\alpha=1}^{\begin{subarray}{c}\longleftarrow\\
M\end{subarray}}\prod_{\beta=1}^{\begin{subarray}{c}\longrightarrow\\
N\end{subarray}}\frac{(u+(\beta-\alpha+M-N)\hbar)\operatorname{id}+\hbar P_{\alpha\beta}}{u+(\beta-\alpha+M-N+1)\hbar}\right)\circ(\eta\otimes\eta)

where
P_{\alpha\beta}\in\operatorname{End}\left(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\otimes M}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\otimes N}\right)
is the operator that permutes the
\alpha
-th component in
\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\otimes M}
and
\beta
-th component in
\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\otimes N}
, and the product is ordered such that index increases along the arrows.

Proof of Proposition
9.23
.

To facilitate presentations,
quiver data associated to
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1})
will be denoted by lowercase letters:
(\varphi,a,b)
; quiver data associated to
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N})
will still be denoted by
(\Phi,A,B)
.

Recall the notation
\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{k}}=(k,0)
, and let

Y^{\prime}=\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}}),\quad X^{\prime}=\mathrm{Tot}(E^{\prime})=\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1})

s^{\prime}=\varphi^{N}a\in\Gamma(Y^{\prime},E^{\prime\vee}),\quad\phi^{\prime}=0,\quad\mathsf{w}_{N}=\langle s^{\prime},b\rangle,

where
E^{\prime}
is the vector bundle that descends from
\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^{n},\mathbb{C})
with coordinate given by
b
, and
\langle-,-\rangle
is the natural pairing. Let

(9.29)

Y:=\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{N}}),\quad X=\mathrm{Tot}(E)=\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{N}),

s=\Phi A-A\Xi\in\Gamma(Y,E^{\vee}),\quad\phi=0,\quad\mathsf{w}_{*}=\langle s,B\rangle.

where
E
is the vector bundle that descends from
\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^{n},\mathbb{C}^{N})
with coordinate given by
B
. Note that cohomological dimensional reduction hold for
(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},s^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
and
(X,Y,s,\phi)
(see
[
COZZ1
, Def. 6.1]
).

We show that these two data are compatible in the sense of
[
COZZ1
, Def. 6.2]
.

Lemma 9.30
.

(1) The map

i\colon R(n,\mathbf{1})\to R(n,\mathbf{N}),\quad(\varphi,a,b)\mapsto(\Phi,A,B)

with
\Phi=\varphi
,
A=(a,\varphi a,\ldots,\varphi^{N-1}a)
,

B=\begin{pmatrix}b\varphi^{N-1}\\
\vdots\\
b\varphi\\
b\end{pmatrix}

is a
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar})
-equivariant closed immersion such that

i^{*}\mathsf{w}_{*}=\mathsf{w}_{N},\quad\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}_{N})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i}}{{\cong}}\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}_{*}).

This induces a closed embedding
i\colon X^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X
whose restriction to
Y^{\prime}
gives
i\colon Y^{\prime}\hookrightarrow Y
.

(2) The
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar})
-equivariant projection

\operatorname{pr}\colon\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^{n},\mathbb{C}^{N})\to\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^{n},\mathbb{C}),\quad B=\begin{pmatrix}b_{1}\\
\vdots\\
b_{N}\end{pmatrix}\mapsto b_{N}

induces a surjective vector bundle map

(9.30)

\operatorname{pr}\colon E|_{Y^{\prime}}\twoheadrightarrow E^{\prime}

such that conditions in
[
COZZ1
, Def. 6.2]
holds. Moreover, there is an isomorphism of vector bundles

\ker(\operatorname{pr})\cong N^{\vee}_{Y^{\prime}/Y}.

Proof.

(1) It is straightforward to check that the map
i
is
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar})
-equivariant and

i^{*}\operatorname{tr}(\Phi AB)=N\cdot\operatorname{tr}(b\varphi^{N}a),\quad i^{*}\operatorname{tr}(A\Xi B)=(N-1)\cdot\operatorname{tr}(b\varphi^{N}a),

therefore the first claim holds. To show the second, note that

\frac{\partial\mathsf{w}_{*}}{\partial A}=0,\frac{\partial\mathsf{w}_{*}}{\partial B}=0
gives

B\Phi=\Xi B,\quad\Phi A=A\Xi.

This implies that
A
is of the form
(a,\varphi a,\ldots,\varphi^{N-1}a)
and
B
is of form
\begin{pmatrix}b\varphi^{N-1}\\
\vdots\\
b\varphi\\
b\end{pmatrix}

such that

\varphi^{N}a=0,\quad b\varphi^{N}=0.

This coincides exactly with
\frac{\partial\mathsf{w}_{N}}{\partial a}=0,\frac{\partial\mathsf{w}_{N}}{\partial b}=0
.
It is easy to check remaining equations coincide.

(2) By a direct calculations, we have
\phi|_{Y^{\prime}}=\phi^{\prime}
,
s|_{Y^{\prime}}=\operatorname{pr}^{\vee}\circ s^{\prime}
.
As shown in (1), elements in the zero locus
Z(s)
are of form
(a,\varphi a,\cdots,\varphi^{N-1}a)
,
so it sits inside
Y^{\prime}
.
There is an obvious isomorphism

\ker(\operatorname{pr})\cong N^{\vee}_{Y^{\prime}/Y}

by construction.
By
[
COZZ1
, Prop. 6.4]
, all conditions in
[
COZZ1
, Def. 6.2]
are satisfied.
∎

Combining Lemma
9.30
and
[
COZZ1
, Prop. 6.9]
, the map

\displaystyle\delta_{H}=(Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}X\hookrightarrow X)_{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}^{*}\colon H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w}_{N})\to H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w}_{*})

is an isomorphism, where
\operatorname{pr}
is the projection (
9.30
).

Moreover, for arbitrary
\underline{\mathbf{d}}
and
\mathsf{W}
in (
9.16
), we can repeat the above construction and obtain a map

\displaystyle\delta_{H,\underline{\mathbf{d}}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}}+\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w}+\mathsf{w}_{N})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}}+\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w}+\mathsf{w}_{*}),

which is induced by a compatible dimensional reduction pair. Here
\mathsf{T}
is a torus that contains
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
and that the framings have
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
weights
u\underline{\mathbf{d}}+\underline{\mathbf{1}}
and
u\underline{\mathbf{d}}+\underline{\mathbf{N}}
respectively.
\delta_{H,\underline{\mathbf{d}}}
is an isomorphism by
[
COZZ1
, Prop. 6.9]
. Let

\delta=(-1)^{(N-1)n}\cdot\delta_{H},\quad\delta_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}=(-1)^{(N-1)n}\cdot\delta_{H,\underline{\mathbf{d}}},

then by
[
COZZ1
, Thm. 6.10]
, the diagram

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})\otimes H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w}_{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{u\gtrless 0,\epsilon}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}}+\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w}+\mathsf{w}_{*})}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})\otimes H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w}_{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{u\gtrless 0,\epsilon}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}\otimes\delta}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}}+\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w}+\mathsf{w}_{N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}}

commutes, with normalizer
\epsilon
given by (
9.6
). It follows that

\displaystyle R_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}},\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}}}^{\mathsf{W}_{*}}}(u)\circ(\operatorname{id}\otimes\>\delta)=(\operatorname{id}\otimes\>\delta)\circ R_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}},\mathcal{H}_{N}}(u).

Taking
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}}}=\mathcal{H}_{1}
implies that
\delta
is a
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
module isomorphism. This finishes the proof of Proposition
9.23
.
∎

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{22.03268pt}{-29.76613pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 30.49 -41.19)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\par{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}
{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{{{}}{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -8.37 8.37 C -40.7 40.7 40.7 40.7 8.82 8.82}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{-0.7071}{-0.7071}{0.7071}{-0.7071}{6.37695pt}{6.37695pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(-0.7071 -0.7071 0.7071 -0.7071 8.82 8.82)} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.32pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray=none,stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=round} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -1.66 2.21 C -1.52 1.38 0 0.14 0.42 0 C 0 -0.14 -1.52 -1.38 -1.66 -2.21}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\qquad\mathsf{W}=0.

Denote
X_{n,r_{1},r_{2}}=\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}})
. Apply Theorem
5.13
to
X_{n,r_{1},r_{2}}
and we get an explicit formula of modified quantum multiplication by divisor on
X_{n,r_{1},r_{2}}
.
Note that
X_{n,1,r_{2}}
are affine spaces, so we shall focus on the
r_{1}>1
cases.

Proposition 9.31
.

Assume
r_{1}>1
. Let
\mathcal{O}(1)
be the determinant of tautological bundle
\mathsf{V}
on
X_{n,r_{1},r_{2}}
, then

\displaystyle c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(1))\,\widetilde{\star}\,\cdot=\begin{cases}c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(1))\cup\cdot-\hbar^{-1}\frac{z}{1-z}e_{0}f_{0},&\text{ if $r_{1}=r_{2}$ },\\
c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(1))\cup\cdot-\hbar^{-1}z\,e_{0}f_{0},&\text{ if $r_{1}>r_{2}$ },\end{cases}

where
e_{0},f_{0}
are part of the generators of
Y_{r_{2}-r_{1}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
in Definition
9.11
.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.45
and the formula for the classical
R
-matrix (
9.20
), the Casimir operators for
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,0)
are

\mathrm{Cas}_{1}=\hbar^{-1}e_{0}f_{0},\quad\mathrm{Cas}_{i}=0,\,\,\mathrm{if}\,\,i>1.

Then by Theorem
5.10
, we have

c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(1))\,\widetilde{\star}\,\cdot=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(1))\cup\cdot-\hbar^{-1}\frac{z}{1-z}e_{0}f_{0}+\mathrm{const}.

The constant is given by
\hbar^{-1}\frac{z}{1-z}e_{0}f_{0}
acting on
1\in H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(X_{n,r_{1},r_{2}})
. According to Theorem
3.7
,
f_{0}(1)
is given by the pushforward of the fundamental class
[\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(n,n-1,\underline{\mathbf{d}})]^{\mathrm{t}}
to
X_{n-1,r_{1},r_{2}}
. It follows from (
3.9
) that
\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(n,n-1,\underline{\mathbf{d}})
is a
\mathbb{P}^{r_{1}-1}
-bundle over
X_{n-1,r_{1},r_{2}}
, so
f_{0}(1)=0
. This proves the
r_{1}=r_{2}
case.

For the
r_{1}>r_{2}
case, it suffices to show that

\mathrm{Cas}_{1,r_{2}-r_{1}}=\hbar^{-1}e_{0}f_{0},

where
e_{0},f_{0}
are part of the generators of
Y_{r_{2}-r_{1}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
. We put bar on the generators of
Y_{r_{2}-r_{1}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
to distinguish from their counterparts of
Y_{0}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
. From the proof of Lemma
4.27
, we have equations of operators:

(p^{*})^{-1}e(u)\,p^{*}=H_{z}\,\bar{e}(u)\,H_{z}^{-1}=\frac{(z-c_{1}(\mathcal{L}))^{r_{1}-r_{2}}}{u-c_{1}(\mathcal{L})}\,[\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(n+1,n,\underline{\mathbf{d}})]=z^{r_{1}-r_{2}}\bar{e}(u)+\text{lower order in $z$},

(p^{*})^{-1}f(u)\,p^{*}=(-1)^{r_{1}-r_{2}}\bar{f}(u).

By expanding in the
u
-power, we obtain

(p^{*})^{-1}e_{r}\,p^{*}=z^{r_{1}-r_{2}}\,\bar{e}_{r}+\text{lower order in $z$},\quad(p^{*})^{-1}f_{r}\,p^{*}=(-1)^{r_{1}-r_{2}}\bar{f}_{r}.

This implies that

(p^{*})^{-1}\mathrm{Cas}_{1}\,p^{*}=\hbar^{-1}(p^{*})^{-1}e_{0}f_{0}\,p^{*}=(-z)^{r_{1}-r_{2}}\,\hbar^{-1}\,\bar{e}_{0}\bar{f}_{0}+\text{lower order in $z$},

therefore
\mathrm{Cas}_{1,r_{2}-r_{1}}=\lim_{z\to\infty}(-z)^{r_{2}-r_{1}}(p^{*})^{-1}\mathrm{Cas}_{1}\,p^{*}=\hbar^{-1}\bar{e}_{0}\bar{f}_{0}
by Definition
4.50
.
∎

Remark 9.32
.

Take
r_{1}=r_{2}=r
, and let
\mathsf{w}=\operatorname{tr}(\Phi AB)
, by dimensional reduction, we have

H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(X_{n,r,r},\mathsf{w})\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(T^{*}\operatorname{Gr}(n,r)).

The modified quantum multiplication by
c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(1))
on
T^{*}\operatorname{Gr}(n,r)
is computed in
[
MO
, Prop. 11.2.2]
11
11

11

The generators
e_{0}
and
f_{0}
are identified with
\hbar e
and
-\hbar f
in
[
MO
, §11]
, respectively.
, given by

\displaystyle c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(1))\,\widetilde{\star}\,\cdot=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(1))\cup\cdot-\hbar^{-1}\frac{z}{1-z}e_{0}f_{0}+\mathrm{const},

for some nonzero constant. On the other hand, we have an injective specialization map

H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(X_{n,r,r},\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(X_{n,r,r}).

Therefore by Theorem
5.13
, the modified quantum multiplication by
c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(1))
on
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(X_{n,r,r},\mathsf{w})
is given by

c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(1))\cup\cdot-\hbar^{-1}\frac{z}{1-z}e_{0}f_{0}.

This example shows that the quantum multiplication by divisor defined using stable maps (
5.5
) is in general
not compatible
with dimensional reduction.
We expect a compatibility holds when the quantum multiplication is defined using twisted maps, see Remark
5.4
.

Let
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{q_{1}},\mathbb{C}^{*}_{q_{2}}
and
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{q_{3}}
be the tori that scale the loops
X,Y
and
Z
with weights
-1
respectively.
Set

\mathsf{S}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{q_{1}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{q_{2}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{q_{3}},

and

\mathsf{T}_{0}=\ker(\mathsf{S}\to\mathbb{C}^{*}),\quad(t_{1},t_{2},t_{3})\mapsto t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}.

Let
\hbar_{i}
be the equivariant parameter for
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{q_{i}}
, then

\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]=\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2},\hbar_{3}]/(\hbar_{1}+\hbar_{2}+\hbar_{3}).

We fix a cyclic stability
\theta<0
in this section when we consider associated quiver varieties.

We define
\ell
-shifted affine Yangian
Y_{\ell}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})

of
\mathfrak{gl}_{1}
(Definition
10.1
) using explicit generators with relations.
When
\ell\leqslant 0
, we construct a natural surjective map from it to the Reshetikhin type shifted Yangian
\mathsf{Y}_{\ell}(Q,\mathsf{W})
of the above
(Q,\mathsf{W})
, which we prove to be an isomorphism when
\ell=0

(Theorem
10.7
).
Building on this, we derive an explicit formula for quantum multiplication by divisors for Hilbert schemes of points on
\mathbb{C}^{3}
(Corollary
10.21
).

We also study various representations of shifted affine Yangians, including
Kirillov-Reshetikhin modules, Vacuum MacMahon modules, prefundamental modules
(Theorem
10.12
) and modules related to Gaiotto-Rapčák’s corner W-algebras (Proposition
10.18
).

10.1.
Shifted affine Yangians of
\mathfrak{gl}_{1}

Definition 10.1
.

For an integer
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
, we define the
\ell
-shifted affine Yangian of
\mathfrak{gl}_{1}
, denoted by
Y_{\ell}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
, to be the
\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})=\mathbb{C}(\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2})
algebra generated by
\left\{e_{j},f_{j},g_{j},c_{j}\right\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
subject to the following relations:

(Y0)

\displaystyle[g_{i},g_{j}]=0,\quad c_{j}\text{ is central},

(Y1)

\displaystyle[e_{i+3},e_{j}]-3[e_{i+2},e_{j+1}]+3[e_{i+1},e_{j+2}]-[e_{i},e_{j+3}]+\sigma_{2}([e_{i+1},e_{j}]-[e_{i},e_{j+1}])=\sigma_{3}\{e_{i},e_{j}\},

(Y2)

\displaystyle[f_{i+3},f_{j}]-3[f_{i+2},f_{j+1}]+3[f_{i+1},f_{j+2}]-[f_{i},f_{j+3}]+\sigma_{2}([f_{i+1},f_{j}]-[f_{i},f_{j+1}])=-\sigma_{3}\{f_{i},f_{j}\},

(Y3)

\displaystyle[e_{i},f_{j}]=\sigma_{3}h_{i+j},

(Y4)

\displaystyle[g_{i},e_{j}]=e_{i+j},

(Y5)

\displaystyle[g_{i},f_{j}]=-f_{i+j},

(Y6)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathfrak{S}_{3}}[e_{i_{1}},[e_{i_{2}},e_{i_{3}+1}]]=0,\quad\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathfrak{S}_{3}}[f_{i_{1}},[f_{i_{2}},f_{i_{3}+1}]]=0,

where
\sigma_{2}=\hbar_{1}\hbar_{2}+\hbar_{2}\hbar_{3}+\hbar_{3}\hbar_{1}
,
\sigma_{3}=\hbar_{1}\hbar_{2}\hbar_{3}
, and the RHS of (Y3) is given by

\displaystyle 1+\sum_{n\geqslant 0}h_{n-\ell}x^{-n-1}=\exp\left(\sum_{j\geqslant 1}\frac{c_{j-1}}{j\cdot x^{j}}+\sum_{k\geqslant 0}g_{k}\psi_{k}(x)\right),\quad h_{-\ell-1}=1,\quad\text{and }\;h_{j}=0\;\text{ for }\;j<-\ell-1,

where
(\psi_{k}(x))_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
is a sequence of functions such that
\exp\left(\sum_{k\geqslant 0}a^{k}\psi_{k}(x)\right)=\prod_{s=1}^{3}\frac{x+\hbar_{s}-a}{x-\hbar_{s}-a}
for a formal parameter
a
.

We set

\displaystyle e(u)=\sum_{j\geqslant 0}e_{j}u^{-j-1},\quad f(u)=\sum_{j\geqslant 0}f_{j}u^{-j-1}.

Remark 10.2
.

The relations (Y1)-(Y6) of
Y_{\ell}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
only depend on
\sigma_{2}
and
\sigma_{3}
which are invariant under the
\mathfrak{S}_{3}
permutation of
\{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2},\hbar_{3}\}
, so
Y_{\ell}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
has an
\mathfrak{S}_{3}
automorphism that permutes
\{\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2},\hbar_{3}\}
.

Remark 10.3
.

Let
Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})^{+}
be the
\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
-algebra generated by
\{e_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
subject to (Y1) and the first equation of (Y6).
By
[
Dav2
, Thm. 5.11]
, the assignment

e_{j}\mapsto c_{1}(\mathcal{L})^{j}\cap[\mathfrak{M}(1,\mathbf{0})]

induces an isomorphism:

Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})^{+}\cong\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]}\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0}),

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}
is the CoHA of
(Q,\mathsf{W})
with twisted product (
3.2
).

Lemma 10.4
.

For any
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}
, the assignment

\displaystyle e_{j}\mapsto\sum_{k=0}^{n}(-1)^{k}z^{n-k}\binom{n}{k}e_{j+k},\quad f_{j}\mapsto(-1)^{n}f_{j},\quad g_{j}\mapsto g_{j},\quad c_{j}\mapsto c_{j}-nz^{j+1}

generates a
\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
-algebra map
\bar{\mathsf{S}}_{\ell,\ell-n;z}\colon Y_{\ell}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})\to Y_{\ell-n}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})[z]
.

Proof.

We need to show that the assignment respects the relations (Y0)-(Y6).
Note that all relations except for (Y1) and the first equation in (Y6) can be checked directly. In principle, one can directly verify (Y1) and (Y6) as well, here we provide another strategy using CoHA. By Remark
10.3
, it is enough to show that

\displaystyle c_{1}(\mathcal{L})^{j}\cap[\mathfrak{M}(1,\mathbf{0})]\mapsto(z-c_{1}(\mathcal{L}))^{n}c_{1}(\mathcal{L})^{j}\cap[\mathfrak{M}(1,\mathbf{0})]

extends to a
\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]
-algebra homomorphism
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}[z]
. Let
\mathsf{V}
be the rank
r
tautological bundle on
\mathfrak{M}(r,\mathbf{0})
, then we claim that the homogeneous linear map
\phi=(\phi_{r}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}(r)\to\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}(r)[z])_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
with

\displaystyle\phi_{r}(\alpha)=z^{nr}c_{-1/z}(\mathsf{V})^{n}\cdot\alpha

generates a
\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]
-algebra homomorphism
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}[z]
. In fact, the CoHA multiplication is preserved because

\displaystyle z^{nr}c_{-1/z}(\mathsf{V})^{n}=z^{nr^{\prime}}c_{-1/z}(\mathsf{V}^{\prime})^{n}\cdot z^{nr^{\prime\prime}}c_{-1/z}(\mathsf{V}^{\prime\prime})^{n}\;\text{ for an SES }\;0\longrightarrow\mathsf{V}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathsf{V}\longrightarrow\mathsf{V}^{\prime\prime\prime}\longrightarrow 0.

Since
\phi_{1}(c_{1}(\mathcal{L})^{j}\cap[\mathfrak{M}(1,\mathbf{0})])=(z-c_{1}(\mathcal{L}))^{n}c_{1}(\mathcal{L})^{j}\cap[\mathfrak{M}(1,\mathbf{0})]
, the lemma follows.
∎

Remark 10.5
.

Our shifted affine Yangian
Y_{\ell}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
of
\mathfrak{gl}_{1}
differs from the one defined in
[
RSYZ2
, §4.1]
.

In the zero shifted case,
Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
is isomorphic to the algebra
\mathbf{SH}^{\mathbf{c}}
in
[
SV2
, §1.8]
, which is a
\mathbb{C}(\xi)
algebra generated by
\{D_{0,j},D_{1,k},D_{-1,k},\mathbf{c}_{k}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 1},k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
, and a complete set of relations given in
[
AS
]
. The generators are identified by

(10.2)

\displaystyle\xi\mapsto-\frac{\hbar_{3}}{\hbar_{1}},\quad D_{1,j}\mapsto\frac{-e_{j}}{\hbar_{3}\>\hbar_{1}^{j}},\quad D_{-1,j}\mapsto\frac{-f_{j}}{\sigma_{3}\>\hbar_{1}^{j}},\quad D_{0,j+1}\mapsto\frac{g_{j}}{\hbar_{1}^{j}},\quad\sum_{i\geqslant 0}(-1)^{i+1}\mathbf{c}_{i}\phi_{i}(s)\mapsto\sum_{j\geqslant 1}\frac{c_{j-1}}{j\>\hbar_{1}^{j}}s^{j},

where
\phi_{i}(s)
is defined in
[
SV2
, (1.68)]
, that is,
\exp\left(\sum_{i\geqslant 0}(-1)^{i+1}a^{i}\phi_{i}(s)\right)=\frac{1+\xi s+as}{1+as}
for all
a\in\mathbb{C}
.

Remark 10.6
.

By
[
SV2
, Thm. 7.9]
,
Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
admits a coproduct which is uniquely determined by

(10.3)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbf{\Delta}(e_{0})=\square(e_{0}),\quad\mathbf{\Delta}(f_{0})=\square(f_{0}),\quad\mathbf{\Delta}(g_{0})=\square(g_{0}),\\
&\mathbf{\Delta}(c_{j})=\square(c_{j}),\;j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0},\\
&\mathbf{\Delta}(g_{1})=\square(g_{1})+\sigma_{3}\sum_{n>0}nJ_{n}\otimes J_{-n},\end{split}

where
\square(x)=x\otimes 1+1\otimes x
,
J_{n}=\frac{\sigma_{3}^{-n}}{(n-1)!}\text{ad}^{n-1}_{-f_{1}}f_{0}
and
J_{-n}=-\frac{\sigma_{3}^{-n}}{(n-1)!}\text{ad}^{n-1}_{e_{1}}e_{0}
for
n>0
.

Apply the Gauss decomposition (
3.35
) to the tripled Jordan quiver
Q
, and we get

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\langle\mathbf{0}|R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)|\mathbf{0}\rangle&\langle\mathbf{0}|R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)|\delta_{i}\rangle\\
\langle\delta_{i}|R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)|\mathbf{0}\rangle&\langle\delta_{i}|R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)|\delta_{i}\rangle\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
\frac{-t}{\sigma_{3}}\mathsf{f}(u)&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{g}(u)&0\\
0&\mathsf{g}(u)\mathsf{h}(u)\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&\mathsf{e}(u)\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},

where
t
is the
\mathsf{T}_{0}
weight of the out-going framing of the auxiliary space
\mathcal{M}(\delta_{i})
, and

\mathsf{e}(u),\mathsf{f}(u),\mathsf{g}(u),\mathsf{h}(u)
are elements in
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
given by

\displaystyle\mathsf{e}(u)=\frac{(-1)^{n+1}}{u-c_{1}(\mathcal{L})}\,[\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(n+1,n,\underline{\mathbf{d}})],\quad\mathsf{f}(u)=\frac{(-1)^{n+\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}}}{u-c_{1}(\mathcal{L})}\,[\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(n,n-1,\underline{\mathbf{d}})]^{\mathrm{t}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{g}(u)=c_{-1/u}((1-t^{-1})\mathsf{V}),\quad\mathsf{h}(u)=u^{\mu}c_{-1/u}\left((q_{1}^{-1}+q_{2}^{-1}+q_{3}^{-1}-q_{1}-q_{2}-q_{3})\mathsf{V}+\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{out}}-\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in}}\right).

Here
\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(n+1,n,\underline{\mathbf{d}})\subset\mathcal{M}(n+1,\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}})
is the correspondence defined below the equation (
3.3
),
\mathcal{L}
is the tautological line bundle on
\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(n+1,n,\underline{\mathbf{d}})
,
\mathsf{V}
is the tautological vector bundle of rank
n
on
\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{d}})
.

Theorem 10.7
.

Let
Q
be the tripled Jordan quiver, equipped with the canonical cubic potential
\mathsf{W}=Z[X,Y]
. For arbitrary
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leqslant 0}
, the assignment

(10.4)

\displaystyle e(u)\mapsto\mathsf{e}(u),\quad f(u)\mapsto\mathsf{f}(u),\quad g_{j}\mapsto j!\>\mathrm{ch}_{j}(\mathsf{V}),\quad c_{j}\mapsto(j+1)!\>\mathrm{ch}_{j+1}(\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in}}-\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{out}}),

generates a surjective
\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
-algebra map

\displaystyle\varrho_{\mu}\colon Y_{\mu}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})\twoheadrightarrow\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W}).

Moreover, the family of maps
\varrho_{\mu}
satisfy the following properties:

(1)

the map
\varrho_{0}
is a bialgebra isomorphism,

(2)

for
\mu^{\prime}<\mu\leqslant 0
, the parametrized shift homomorphisms
\mathsf{S}_{\mu,\mu^{\prime};z}
§
4.5
and
\bar{\mathsf{S}}_{\mu,\mu^{\prime};z}
(defined in Lemma
10.4
) are compatible with
\varrho_{\mu}
and
\varrho_{\mu^{\prime}}
, that is,

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}_{\mu,\mu^{\prime};z}\circ\varrho_{\mu}=\varrho_{\mu^{\prime}}\circ\bar{\mathsf{S}}_{\mu,\mu^{\prime};z}\>.

Proof.

Step 1.
We show that (
10.4
) defines a
\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
-algebra map. Let
Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})^{+}
be the
\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
-algebra generated by
\{e_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
subject to (Y1) and the first equation of (Y6). We claim that

\displaystyle e_{j}\mapsto c_{1}(\mathcal{L})^{j}\cap[\mathfrak{M}(1,\mathbf{0})^{\mathrm{nil}}]\in\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}^{\mathrm{nil}}

gives a
\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
-algebra isomorphism

(10.5)

Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})^{+}\cong\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}^{\mathrm{nil}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]}\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0}).

By Remark
10.3
, the assignment

e_{j}\mapsto c_{1}(\mathcal{L})^{j}\cap[\mathfrak{M}(1,\mathbf{0})]

induces an isomorphism

Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})^{+}\cong\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]}\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0}).

Since
e_{j}\mapsto-\sigma_{3}e_{j}
is an automorphism of
Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})^{+}
, we only need to show that the natural map

\displaystyle H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(n,\mathbf{0}),\mathsf{w})_{\mathfrak{M}(n,\mathbf{0})^{\mathrm{nil}}}\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(n,\mathbf{0}),\mathsf{w})

is an isomorphism after tensoring with
\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
. Consider the semisimplification map

\mathsf{JH}\colon\mathfrak{M}(n,\mathbf{0})\to\mathcal{M}_{0}(n,\mathbf{0}),

where the target is the affine quotient space, then
\mathfrak{M}(n,\mathbf{0})^{\mathrm{nil}}=\mathsf{JH}^{-1}(0)
, where
0\in\mathcal{M}_{0}(n,\mathbf{0})
corresponds to the trivial quiver representation. Then it boils down to show that

(10.6)

\displaystyle H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}_{0}(n,\mathbf{0}),i_{*}i^{!}\mathsf{JH}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathfrak{M}(n,\mathbf{0})})\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}_{0}(n,\mathbf{0}),\mathsf{JH}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathfrak{M}(n,\mathbf{0})})

is an isomorphism after tensoring with
\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
. Note that the vanishing cycle sheaf
\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathfrak{M}(n,\mathbf{0})}
is supported on the critical locus
\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})
, and
\mathsf{JH}
maps
\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})
to its affine quotient
\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(n)
, which is isomorphic to
\mathbb{C}^{3n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n}
. Since
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-fixed locus of
\mathbb{C}^{3n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n}
consists of only one point
0
, applying
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-localization to
\mathsf{JH}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathfrak{M}(n,\mathbf{0})}
shows that (
10.6
) is an isomorphism after tensoring with
\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
. This proves the claimed isomorphism (
10.5
).

Combining the above claim with Theorem
3.7
, we see that (
10.4
) respects the relations (Y1) and the first equation of (Y6). Taking the opposite relations, we see that (
10.4
) also respects (Y2) and the second equation of (Y6). It is easy to see that (Y0), (Y4), and (Y5) are respected by (
10.4
). (Y3) follows from Theorem
3.7
. This proves that (
10.4
) defines a
\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
-algebra map
\varrho_{\mu}
.

Step 2.
We prove Property (2). Comparing the formulas for
\bar{\mathsf{S}}_{\mu,\mu^{\prime};z}
in Lemma
10.4
and for
\mathsf{S}_{\mu,\mu^{\prime};z}
in Remark
4.31
, it is straightforward to see that
\mathsf{S}_{\mu,\mu^{\prime};z}\circ\varrho_{\mu}=\varrho_{\mu^{\prime}}\circ\bar{\mathsf{S}}_{\mu,\mu^{\prime};z}
.

Step 3.
We prove Property (1). We will use the isomorphism (Theorem
4.45
):

\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})\cong\mathsf{Y}^{\mathrm{MO}}_{Q^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]}\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0}),

where
Q^{\prime}
is the Jordan quiver. This isomorphism is induced by dimensional reduction, which we may assume is along the
Z
-loop in quiver
Q
without loss of generality.
And operators
e(u),f(u),g_{j},c_{j}
are mapped to

(10.7)

\begin{gathered}e(u)\mapsto\frac{-\sigma_{3}}{u-c_{1}(\mathcal{L})}\,[\mathfrak{B}(n+1,n,r)],\quad f(u)\mapsto\frac{(-1)^{r}\sigma_{3}}{u-c_{1}(\mathcal{L})}\,[\mathfrak{B}(n,n-1,r)]^{\mathrm{t}},\\
g_{j}\mapsto j!\>\mathrm{ch}_{j}(\mathsf{V}),\quad c_{j}\mapsto(j+1)!\>\mathrm{ch}_{j+1}((1-q_{3})\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in}}).\end{gathered}

Here
\mathfrak{B}(n+1,n,r)\subset\mathcal{N}(n+1,r)\times\mathcal{N}(n,r)
is the following correspondence: if we identify
\mathcal{N}(n,r)
with moduli space of framed rank
r
torsion-free sheaves with second Chern number
-n
on
\mathbb{P}^{2}
, then
\mathfrak{B}(n+1,n,r)
consists of pairs of sheaves
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
such that
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
and
\mathrm{length}\>\mathcal{F}^{\prime}/\mathcal{F}=1
.
\mathcal{L}
is the line bundle on
\mathfrak{B}(n+1,n,r)
whose fiber at
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
is
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}/\mathcal{F}
,
\mathsf{V}
is the tautological vector bundle of rank
n
on
\mathcal{N}(n,r)
.

In view of the isomorphism
Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})\cong\mathbf{SH}^{\mathbf{c}}
(
10.2
), the induced representation (
10.7
) of
\mathbf{SH}^{\mathbf{c}}
on
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{N}(r))
agrees with the one given in
[
SV2
, Thm. 3.2]
. Here
\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}
acts on the framing
\mathbb{C}^{r}
with weights
a_{1},\ldots,a_{r}
. By
[
SV2
, Thm. 3.2]
, the induced map
\mathbf{SH}^{\mathbf{c}}\to\prod_{r}\operatorname{End}(H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{N}(r)))
is injective. It follows that
\varrho_{0}\colon Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})\to\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
is injective.

For the surjectivity of
\varrho_{0}
, we notice that
e_{0}
(resp.
f_{0}
) is the Baranovsky operator
\beta_{-1}(-\sigma_{3})
(resp.
\beta_{1}(-\sigma_{3})
)
[
MO
, §12.2]
, and that tautological classes
\{\mathrm{ch}_{j}(\mathsf{V}),\mathrm{ch}_{j}(\mathsf{D}_{\operatorname{in}})\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
are in the image of
\varrho_{0}
. Then
\varrho_{0}
is surjective by
[
MO
, Thm. 18.1.1]
. This implies that
\varrho_{0}
is a bijection.

Finally, by Lemma
10.8
below and
[
MO
, §12.4.9]
, the classical
R
-matrix
\mathbf{r}
of
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
is
12
12

12

The classical
R
-matrix
\mathbf{r}
of
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
is
-\hbar_{3}
times the classical
R
-matrix of
\mathsf{Y}_{Q^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{MO}}
due to different normalizations.

(10.8)

\mathbf{r}=\mathsf{v}\otimes\mathsf{d}+\mathsf{d}\otimes\mathsf{v}-\sigma_{3}\sum_{n\neq 0}\varrho_{0}(J_{n})\otimes\varrho_{0}(J_{-n}).

Then the coproduct formula (
4.20
) reads:

\displaystyle\Delta c_{1}(\mathsf{V})=\square\,c_{1}(\mathsf{V})+\sigma_{3}\sum_{n>0}n\varrho_{0}(J_{n})\otimes\varrho_{0}(J_{-n}),

that is,

\Delta(\varrho_{0}(g_{1}))=(\varrho_{0}\otimes\varrho_{0})\mathbf{\Delta}(g_{1}).

It is easy to see that for
x=e_{0},f_{0},g_{0},c_{j}
(
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}
), we have

\Delta(\varrho_{0}(x))=(\varrho_{0}\otimes\varrho_{0})\mathbf{\Delta}(x).

Thus
\varrho_{0}
is a bialgebra map.

Step 4.
We prove the surjectivity of
\varrho_{\mu}
. By Lemma
4.32
,
\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
is generated by
\bigcup_{a\in\mathbb{C}}\mathsf{S}_{0,\mu;z}(\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W}))\big|_{z=a}
. Since
\varrho_{0}
is an isomorphism, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}_{0,\mu;z}(\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W}))=\mathsf{S}_{0,\mu;z}(\varrho_{0}(Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})))=\varrho_{\mu}(\bar{\mathsf{S}}_{0,\mu;z}(Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})))\subseteq\varrho_{\mu}(Y_{\mu}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1}))[z],

and it follows that
\bigcup_{a\in\mathbb{C}}\mathsf{S}_{0,\mu;z}(\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W}))\big|_{z=a}\subseteq\varrho_{\mu}(Y_{\mu}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1}))
; therefore
\varrho_{\mu}
is surjective.
∎

Lemma 10.8
.

Let
J_{\pm n}
be the operators defined in Remark
10.6
, then
\varrho_{0}(J_{\pm n})=\beta_{\pm n}(1)
, where
\beta_{\pm n}(1)
are the Baranovsky operators defined in
[
MO
, §12.2]
.

Proof.

Apply
[
Dav2
, Prop. 5.2]
iteratively to
e_{j}\propto\alpha_{1}^{(j)}

13
13

13

Here
\alpha_{1}^{(j)}
is given in
[
Dav2
, §5]
and
\propto
means equal up to multiplying a scalar.
, and we see that the image of
J_{-n}=-\frac{\sigma_{3}^{-n}}{(n-1)!}\text{ad}^{n-1}_{e_{1}}e_{0}
under
\varrho_{0}
is in the BPS Lie algebra (isomorphic to the MO Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}_{Q^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{MO}}
by
[
BD
]
); therefore
\varrho_{0}(J_{\pm n})\propto\beta_{\pm n}(1)
because
\mathfrak{g}_{Q^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{MO}}
has a basis
\{\mathsf{v},\mathsf{d},\beta_{n}(1)\>|\>n\neq 0\}

[
MO
, §12.4.10]
.

We determine the coefficients as follows. Both
\varrho_{0}(J_{-n})
and
\beta_{-n}(1)
are primitive, that is,

\Delta\varrho_{0}(J_{\pm n})=\square\varrho_{0}(J_{\pm n}),\quad\Delta\beta_{-n}(1)=\square\beta_{-n}(1).

Then, to show that
\varrho_{0}(J_{-n})=\beta_{-n}(1)
, it is enough to show that the actions of
\varrho_{0}(J_{-n})
and
\beta_{-n}(1)
agree on
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\operatorname{Hilb}\mathbb{C}^{2})
.
\beta_{-n}(1)
acts on
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\operatorname{Hilb}\mathbb{C}^{2})
as Nakajima’s raising operator
\alpha_{-n}
, and
\varrho_{0}(J_{-n})
acts on
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\operatorname{Hilb}\mathbb{C}^{2})
as
\frac{1}{(n-1)!}\text{ad}^{n-1}_{-\alpha^{\prime}_{-1}}\alpha_{-1}
where
\alpha^{\prime}_{-1}
is the derivative of
\alpha_{-1}
defined by Lehn in
[
Leh
, Def. 3.8]
.

By
[
Leh
, Thm. 3.10]
, we have

\alpha_{-n}=\frac{1}{(n-1)!}\text{ad}^{n-1}_{-\alpha^{\prime}_{-1}}\alpha_{-1}.

This shows that
\varrho_{0}(J_{-n})=\beta_{-n}(1)
. Comparing the commutators
[J_{n},J_{-n}]=\frac{n}{\sigma_{3}}c_{0}
(by direct computation) and

[\beta_{n}(1),\beta_{-n}(1)]=\frac{-rn}{\hbar_{1}\hbar_{2}}=\frac{n}{\sigma_{3}}\varrho_{0}(c_{0}),

(by
[
MO
, (12.6)]
), we conclude that
\varrho_{0}(J_{n})=\beta_{n}(1)
.
∎

We conjecture that the isomorphism
\varrho_{0}
extends to any
\mu\leqslant 0
.

Conjecture 10.9
.

For arbitrary
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leqslant 0}
, the map
\varrho_{\mu}\colon Y_{\mu}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})\to\mathsf{Y}_{\mu}(Q,\mathsf{W})
is an isomorphism.

10.2.
Various representations from framed tripled Jordan quiver with potentials

Let us consider the following framings with potentials:

(1)

(Kirillov-Reshetikhin module)
\underline{\mathbf{d}}=\mathbf{1}=(1,1)
, the potential is
\mathsf{W}_{N}=\mathsf{W}+BZ^{N}A
(with
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}
), and we require that
\mathsf{T}_{0}
scales
B
by the character
q_{3}^{N}
and fixes
A
.

We define

\mathcal{F}(n):=H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}}),\mathsf{w}),\quad\mathcal{F}:=H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}}),\mathsf{w})=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}\mathcal{F}(n).

Remark 10.10
.

The mixed Hodge structures on derived global sections
\mathrm{R\Gamma}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w})
and
\mathrm{R\Gamma}(\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}}),\mathsf{w})
are pure. This can be seen as follows.
\mathrm{R\Gamma}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w})
is a direct summand of
\mathrm{R\Gamma}(\mathfrak{M}(n,\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w})
(using the nonabelian stable envelope, see
[
COZZ1
, §9.1]
). By a vector bundle pullback isomorphism followed by dimensional reduction,
\mathrm{R\Gamma}(\mathfrak{M}(n,\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w})
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{R\Gamma}(\mathfrak{X}(n,\mathbf{0}))
up to degree shift and Tate twist, where
\mathfrak{X}(n,\mathbf{0})
is the preprojective stack of the Jordan quiver with zero framing.
\mathrm{R\Gamma}(\mathfrak{X}(n,\mathbf{0}))
is pure by
[
Dav3
, Thm. A]
, and this shows the purity for
\mathrm{R\Gamma}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w})
. The purity for
\mathrm{R\Gamma}(\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}}),\mathsf{w})
follows from vector bundle pullback isomorphism

\mathrm{R\Gamma}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w})\cong\mathrm{R\Gamma}(\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}}),\mathsf{w}).

As a consequence of the purity, the equivariant cohomologies
\mathcal{V}(n)
and
\mathcal{F}(n)
are formal, and in particular they are free over
H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}}(\operatorname{pt})
and over
H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\operatorname{pt})
respectively.

Remark 10.11
.

If we introduce an additional
\mathbb{G}_{m}
action in the example (1) that scales
B
with weight
1
and fixes all the other quiver data, then it fits into the assumption for the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action in
[
COZZ1
, §7.2]
, and the construction in
loc. cit.
gives a specialization map

(10.9)

\mathsf{sp}\colon\mathcal{H}_{N}=H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w}_{N})\longrightarrow H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w})=\mathcal{V}|_{t=N\hbar_{3}}\>,

which is a
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-module map because it commutes with stable envelopes and therefore it commutes with
R
-matrices. We will see shortly that
\mathsf{sp}
is injective after localization.

Notations.
In below, we use the following notations.

•

The
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-localization is denoted as
(\cdots)_{\mathrm{loc}}=(\cdots)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]}\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
.

•

\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}[a]
denotes the cohomology
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a}}(\mathcal{M}(\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}^{\mathrm{fr}})
, which is obtained from the original state space
\mathcal{H}^{\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}}_{\underline{\mathbf{d}},\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
by including a torus
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a}
that scales the framing vector space with weight
1
.

Theorem 10.12
.

In the above examples, we have the following.

(a)

\mathcal{H}_{1}
is isomorphic to the Fock module in
[
MO
, §13.1]
, and
\mathcal{H}_{N,\mathrm{loc}}
is isomorphic to the
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-submodule of
\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}[(N-1)\hbar_{3}]\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}[\hbar_{3}]\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}
that is generated by vacuum
|\mathbf{0}\rangle\otimes\cdots\otimes|\mathbf{0}\rangle
.

(b)

\mathcal{H}_{N,\mathrm{loc}}
and
\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{loc}}|_{t=\lambda_{1}\hbar_{1}+\lambda_{2}\hbar_{2}}
are irreducible
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-modules for
(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\in\mathbb{C}^{2}\setminus\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, and
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{loc}}
is an irreducible
\mathsf{Y}_{-1}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-module.

(c)

The localized specialization map
\mathsf{sp}\colon\mathcal{H}_{N,\mathrm{loc}}\to\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{loc}}|_{t=N\hbar_{3}}
of (
10.9
)
is an injective
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-module map.

Proof.

(a) By dimensional reduction, we have
\mathcal{H}_{1}\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{1}))
, where
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{1})
is the Nakajima variety associated to the doubled Jordan quiver with framing dimension one. In view of the isomorphism

\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})\cong\mathsf{Y}_{Q^{\prime}}^{\text{MO}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]}\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0}),

\mathcal{H}_{1}\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{1}))
is a
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
module isomorphism, and the latter is the Fock module in
[
MO
, §13.1]
.

The statement concerning
\mathcal{H}_{N,\mathrm{loc}}
is proven using a similar idea as Theorem
9.28
. We define
\eta
to be the composition

(10.10)

\displaystyle\eta\colon\mathcal{H}_{N,\mathrm{loc}}\xrightarrow{\delta_{1}}\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}},\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathsf{W}_{*}}\xrightarrow{\delta_{2}^{-1}}H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N}),\phi)_{\mathrm{loc}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{sp}}H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N}))_{\mathrm{loc}}\xrightarrow{\mathbf{Stab}_{s<0}^{-1}}\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}[(N-1)\hbar_{3}]\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}[\hbar_{3}]\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}},

where notations in the middle steps are explained as follows.

Let
\mathcal{H}_{\underline{\mathbf{N}}}^{\mathsf{W}_{*}}=H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w}_{*})
(with
\mathsf{w}_{*}=\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{W}_{*})
) be the critical cohomology of the following quiver with potential
