{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}
{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{{{}}{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 10.26 -5.92 C 55.55 -32.07 -32.07 -55.55 -6.24 -10.81}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.5}{0.86603}{-0.86603}{0.5}{-4.50919pt}{-7.81017pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(0.5 0.86603 -0.86603 0.5 -6.24 -10.81)} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.32pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray=none,stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=round} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -1.66 2.21 C -1.52 1.38 0 0.14 0.42 0 C 0 -0.14 -1.52 -1.38 -1.66 -2.21}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\qquad\mathsf{W}_{*}=Z[X,Y]+ZAB-A\Xi B,

where
\Xi\in\operatorname{End}(\mathbb{C}^{N})
is the regular nilpotent matrix such that
\Xi(e_{i})=e_{i+1}
for a given bases
\mathbb{C}^{N}=\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{N}\}
. We set
(t_{1},t_{2},t_{3})\in\mathsf{T}_{0}\subset\mathbb{C}^{*}_{q_{1}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{q_{2}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{q_{3}}
action on quiver data to be

(10.11)

\displaystyle(X,Y,Z,B,A)\mapsto(t_{1}^{-1}X,t_{2}^{-1}Y,t_{3}^{-1}Z,t_{3}\cdot\sigma(t_{3})B,A\sigma(t_{3})^{-1}),

where
\sigma\colon\mathbb{C}^{*}_{q_{3}}\to\mathrm{GL}_{N}
is the homomorphism
\sigma(t_{3})=\operatorname{diag}(1,t_{3},\ldots,t_{3}^{N-1})
in the aforementioned basis.
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N})
is the Nakajima variety associated to the doubled Jordan quiver (with loops
X
and
Y
), with
\mathsf{T}_{0}
action induced by omitting
Z
in (
10.11
).
\phi
is the function on
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N})
given by

\displaystyle\phi=\operatorname{tr}(A\Xi B).

The maps involved in (
10.10
) are given as follows.

•

\delta_{1}
is the interpolation map
\delta_{H}
constructed in
[
COZZ1
, Prop. 6.7]
with respect to the following compatible dimensional reduction data. Recall the notation
\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{k}}=(k,0)
, and let

\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}=\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}}),\quad\mathcal{X}^{\prime}=\mathrm{Tot}(E^{\prime})=\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1}),

s^{\prime}=Z^{N}A\in\Gamma(\mathcal{Y}^{\prime},E^{\prime\vee}),\quad\varphi^{\prime}=\operatorname{tr}(Z[X,Y]),\quad\mathsf{w}_{N}=\varphi^{\prime}+\langle s^{\prime},B\rangle,

where
E^{\prime}
is the vector bundle that descends from
\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^{n},\mathbb{C})
with coordinate given by
B
, and
\langle-,-\rangle
is the natural pairing. Let

\mathcal{Y}:=\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{N}}),\quad\mathcal{X}=\mathrm{Tot}(E)=\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{N}),

s=ZA-A\Xi\in\Gamma(\mathcal{Y},E^{\vee}),\quad\varphi=\operatorname{tr}(Z[X,Y]),\quad\mathsf{w}_{*}=\varphi+\langle s,B\rangle,

where
E
is the vector bundle that descends from
\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^{n},\mathbb{C}^{N})
with coordinate given by
B
. The compatibility between the dimensional reduction data
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y},s,\varphi)
and
(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{Y}^{\prime},s^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})
is proven similarly to Lemma
9.30
and we shall not repeat it. By
[
COZZ1
, Prop. 6.9]
,
\delta_{1}
is an isomorphism.

•

\delta_{2}
is the deformed dimensional reduction along
Z
(which is an isomorphism by
[
COZZ1
, Thm. C.1]
).

•

\mathsf{sp}
is the specialization map in
[
COZZ1
, §7.2]
constructed using the additional
\mathbb{G}_{m}
action that scales
B
with weight
1
and fixes other quiver data.

•

The stable envelope map
\mathbf{Stab}_{s<0}
is constructed as follows. Let
(t_{1},t_{2},t_{3},p)\in\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{s}
act on
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N})
by

\displaystyle(X,Y,B,A)\mapsto(t_{1}^{-1}X,t_{2}^{-1}Y,t_{3}\cdot\sigma(p)B,A\sigma(p)^{-1}).

The stable envelope

\displaystyle\mathbf{Stab}_{s<0}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{s}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N})^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{s}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{s}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N}))

is a
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-module map by the argument in §
4.2
. Evaluation at
s=\hbar_{3}
amounts to taking equivariant cohomologies with respect to the subtorus

\mathsf{T}_{0}\xrightarrow{(\operatorname{id},q_{3})}\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{s},

which will be called the “diagonal”
\mathsf{T}_{0}
. Since
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N})^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{s}}=\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{1})^{\times N}
, with
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{q_{3}}
weights
\{0,1,\ldots,N-1\}
on the one-dimensional framing vector spaces,
we get a
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-module map:

\displaystyle\mathbf{Stab}_{s<0}\colon\mathcal{H}_{1}[(N-1)\hbar_{3}]\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1}[\hbar_{3}]\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1}\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N})).

It is elementary to see that the induced
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{s}
action on the diagonal
\mathsf{T}_{0}
fixed locus
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N})^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}
is repelling. Then by
[
COZZ1
, Prop. 3.36]
, the above map is injective after localization. Moreover, the map

\displaystyle\mathbf{Stab}_{s<0}\colon\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}[(N-1)\hbar_{3}]\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}[\hbar_{3}]\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N}))_{\mathrm{loc}}

is an isomorphism by a graded dimension counting argument.

We have seen that all intermediate maps but the specialization map
\mathsf{sp}
are isomorphisms in (
10.10
), and we claim that
\mathsf{sp}
is injective. To prove the claim, it is equivalent to show that the specialization map at the
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-fixed locus:

H(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N})^{\mathsf{T}_{0}},\phi)\xrightarrow{\mathsf{sp}}H(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N})^{\mathsf{T}_{0}})

is injective. Note that
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N})^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}
is the same as the
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-fixed locus of an
A_{\infty}
quiver, so the claim follows from Lemma
10.13
below.
The maps
\delta_{1}
,
\delta_{2}
, and
\mathsf{sp}
are
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-module maps because they commute with stable envelopes and therefore they commute with
R
-matrices.
\mathbf{Stab}_{s<0}
is a
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-module map as we explained above. We conclude that
\eta
is an injective
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-module map. We also note that
\eta
maps the vacuum
|\mathbf{0}\rangle\in\mathcal{H}_{N,\mathrm{loc}}
to vacuum

|\mathbf{0}\rangle\otimes\cdots\otimes|\mathbf{0}\rangle\in\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}[(N-1)\hbar_{3}]\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}[\hbar_{3}]\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}.

To show that
\mathcal{H}_{N,\mathrm{loc}}
is isomorphic to the
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-submodule of
\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}[(N-1)\hbar_{3}]\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}[\hbar_{3}]\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}
that is generated by vacuum
|\mathbf{0}\rangle\otimes\cdots\otimes|\mathbf{0}\rangle
, it is enough to show that
\mathcal{H}_{N,\mathrm{loc}}
is irreducible, which we prove below.

The subgroup
\{(x,x^{-1},1)\>|\>x\in\mathbb{C}^{*}\}\subset\mathsf{T}_{0}
satisfies the assumption for the
\mathsf{S}
torus in Corollary
7.4
, so
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N}))_{\mathrm{loc}}
is cogenerated by vacuum. As a submodule,
\mathcal{H}_{N,\mathrm{loc}}
is also cogenerated by vacuum.

Let us show that
\mathcal{H}_{N,\mathrm{loc}}
is compact. By
[
COZZ1
, Prop. 6.5]
, we have
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-equivariant isomorphism between schemes

\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1})}(\mathsf{w}_{N})\cong\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{N})}(\mathsf{w}_{*})\cong\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N})}(\phi).

\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N})}(\phi)
can be explicitly described as follows: it is the moduli space

M=\{(X,Y,A,B)\>|\>[X,Y]+AB=0\},

whose points represent stable representations of framed preprojective algebra
\Pi_{\overline{Q^{\prime}}}^{\mathrm{fr}}
of the doubled Jordan quiver
\overline{Q^{\prime}}
, together with a
\Pi_{\overline{Q^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{fr}}}
-module endomorphism
\mathcal{E}
of
M
such that
\mathcal{E}
acts on the framing vector space by
\Xi
. Note that the cyclic stability condition constrains such
\mathcal{E}
to be uniquely determined by
\Xi
.
\mathcal{E}
induces a
\Pi_{\overline{Q^{\prime}}}^{\mathrm{fr}}
-module filtration on
M
such that each associated graded piece has framing dimension one. Under the Jordan-Hölder map

\mathsf{JH}\colon\mathcal{N}(n,\mathbf{N})\to\mathcal{N}_{0}(n,\mathbf{N}),

\mathsf{JH}(\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{N}(n,\mathbf{N})}(\phi))
is contained in the union of the images of the direct sum map
\prod_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{N}_{0}(n_{i},\mathbf{1})\to\mathcal{N}_{0}(n,\mathbf{N})
,
where the union is taken for all decompositions
n=\sum_{i=1}^{N}n_{i}
. Since
\mathcal{N}_{0}(n_{i},\mathbf{1})^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}=\{0\}
, we see that
\mathsf{JH}(\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{N}(n,\mathbf{N})}(\phi)^{\mathsf{T}_{0}})=\{0\}
, and this implies that
\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{N}(n,\mathbf{N})}(\phi)^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}
is compact.

As
\mathcal{H}_{N,\mathrm{loc}}
is compact and cogenerated by vacuum, it is irreducible by Proposition
7.6
. This proves (a).

(b) We have shown that
\mathcal{H}_{N,\mathrm{loc}}
is irreducible in the proof of (a). We claim that
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{loc}}
is compact. In fact,
\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}})\cong\operatorname{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{C}^{3})
, and
\mathsf{T}_{0}\subset\mathbb{C}^{*}_{q_{1}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{q_{2}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{q_{3}}
acts on
\mathbb{C}^{3}
by weight
(q_{1},q_{2},q_{3})
. It is well known that
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{C}^{3})^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}
is a finite number of points and, in particular, compact.

The
(\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t})
-weights that appear in the normal bundle
N_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1})^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}}/\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1})}
are of the form

\{a_{1}\hbar_{1}+a_{2}\hbar_{2},\>t+b_{1}\hbar_{1}+b_{2}\hbar_{2}\>|\>a_{1},a_{2},b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\}.

So for
t=\lambda_{1}\hbar_{1}+\lambda_{2}\hbar_{2}
such that
(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\in\mathbb{C}^{2}\setminus\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, the equivariant localization implies that the embedding
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1})^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1})
induces an isomorphism

H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1})^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}},\mathsf{w})_{\mathrm{loc}}\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w})_{\mathrm{loc}}\big|_{t=\lambda_{1}\hbar_{1}+\lambda_{2}\hbar_{2}}.

We note that
\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{1})^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}}=\mathcal{M}(n,\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}})^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}
, and the latter is compact. Therefore,
\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{loc}}|_{t=\lambda_{1}\hbar_{1}+\lambda_{2}\hbar_{2}}
admits a
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-invariant nondegenerate bilinear form.

By Proposition
7.6
, to prove that
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{loc}}
and
\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{loc}}|_{t=\lambda_{1}\hbar_{1}+\lambda_{2}\hbar_{2}}
are irreducible, it remains to show that they are cogenerated by vacuum. By Theorem
7.3
, it is enough to check the conditions (i) and (ii) in
loc. cit.
. Condition (i) is obvious for both
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{loc}}
and
\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{loc}}|_{t=\lambda_{1}\hbar_{1}+\lambda_{2}\hbar_{2}}
. For condition (ii), we claim that specialization maps

\displaystyle\mathsf{sp}_{\mathbf{1}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w})\big|_{t=a_{1}\hbar_{1}+a_{2}\hbar_{2}}\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{1}))\big|_{t=a_{1}\hbar_{1}+a_{2}\hbar_{2}}\>,\quad\mathsf{sp}_{\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}}),\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}})),

are injective, for
all

(a_{1},a_{2})\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
. In fact, since
\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{1})
and
\mathfrak{M}(\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}})
are vector bundles over the stack of unframed representations
\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{0})
and the potential
\mathsf{w}
is the pullback one, we have natural isomorphisms

\begin{gathered}H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w})\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{0}),\mathsf{w})\otimes\mathbb{C}[t],\quad H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{1}))\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{0}))\otimes\mathbb{C}[t],\\
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}}),\mathsf{w})\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{0}),\mathsf{w}),\quad H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}}))\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{0})).\end{gathered}

We note that
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathbf{1}}
and
\mathsf{sp}_{\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}}}
are obtained by the specialization map

\mathsf{sp}_{\mathbf{0}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{0}),\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{0}))

on the base stack via the aforementioned isomorphisms.
By
[
COZZ1
, Rem. 7.16]
, we have

\mathsf{sp}_{\mathbf{0}}=j_{*}\circ\delta^{-1},

where
\delta\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(Z(\mu))\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{0}),\mathsf{w})
is the dimensional reduction isomorphism (
\mu(X,Y,Z)=[X,Y]
is the moment map morphism),
j\colon Z(\mu)\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{0})
is the natural embedding. The pushforward map
j_{*}
is injective by
[
Dav3
, Thm. 10.2]
, so
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathbf{0}}
,
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathbf{1}}
, and
\mathsf{sp}_{\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{1}}}
are injective. This proves (b).

(c) The specialization
\mathsf{sp}\colon\mathcal{H}_{N,\mathrm{loc}}\to\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{loc}}|_{t=N\hbar_{3}}
is a
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-module map because it commutes with stable envelope and therefore it commutes with
R
-matrices. Obviously,
\mathsf{sp}(|\mathbf{0}\rangle)=|\mathbf{0}\rangle
and, in particular,
\mathsf{sp}
is nonzero. The injectivity of
\mathsf{sp}
follows from the irreducibility of
\mathcal{H}_{N,\mathrm{loc}}
.
∎

Lemma 10.13
.

Let
\Gamma
be a quiver of
A_{r}
type, and
i
be an integer such that
1\leqslant i\leqslant r
. Consider the following framed representation of the double quiver

\overline{\Gamma}
:

n_{1}
n_{2}
\cdots
n_{i}
N
\cdots
n_{r}
A
B
X_{1}
Y_{1}
X_{2}
Y_{2}
X_{i-1}
Y_{i-1}
X_{i}
Y_{i}
X_{r-1}
Y_{r-1}

with
\mathsf{T}_{0}
acted on by

\displaystyle(t_{1},t_{2},t_{3})\in\mathsf{T}_{0}\>\colon\>(X_{\bullet},Y_{\bullet},B,A)\mapsto(t_{1}^{-1}X_{\bullet},t_{2}^{-1}Y_{\bullet},t_{3}\cdot\sigma(t_{3})B,A\sigma(t_{3})^{-1}),

where
\sigma(t_{3})=\operatorname{diag}(1,t_{3},\cdots,t_{3}^{N-1})\in\mathrm{GL}_{N}
in a given basis
\mathbb{C}^{N}=\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{N}\}
. The Nakajima variety of the above quiver is denoted
\mathcal{N}(N\delta_{i})=\bigsqcup_{\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},\ldots,n_{r})}\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{n},N\delta_{i})
. Define a potential
\phi
on
\mathcal{N}(N\delta_{i})
by
\phi(X_{\bullet},Y_{\bullet},B,A)=\operatorname{tr}(\Xi BA)
where
\Xi
is the nilpotent matrix such that
\Xi(e_{k})=e_{k+1}
in the aforementioned basis. Then the specialization map
\mathsf{sp}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{N}(N\delta_{i}),\phi)\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{N}(N\delta_{i}))
is injective after
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-localization.

Proof.

It is enough to show that
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{N}(N\delta_{i}),\phi)_{\mathrm{loc}}
is an irreducible
\mathsf{Y}_{\Gamma}^{\text{MO}}
-module. By
[
McB
, Cor. 6.2.3]
, the operators
\{e_{i}(u),f_{i}(u),h_{i}(u)\}_{i\in Q_{0}}
in the Gauss decomposition (
3.35
) generate a subalgebra of
\mathsf{Y}_{\Gamma}^{\text{MO}}
that is isomorphic to the Yangian algebra
Y_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{sl}_{r+1})
defined in
[
V
]
. Here parameters are identified by
2\hbar=\hbar_{3}
. By the construction of the critical convolution algebra action in
[
VV2
, §3]
, the
Y_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{sl}_{r+1})
-module structure on
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{N}(N\delta_{i}),\phi)_{\mathrm{loc}}
agrees with the one in
[
VV2
, Thm. 4.3(e)]
, which is irreducible (a Kirillov-Reshetikhin module) by
[
VV2
, Thm. 4.5]
. This implies the irreducibility of
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{N}(N\delta_{i}),\phi)_{\mathrm{loc}}
as a
\mathsf{Y}_{\Gamma}^{\text{MO}}
-module.
∎

Remark 10.14
.

Lemma
10.13
still holds if we replace
\Gamma
by an arbitrary Dynkin quiver and let
i\in\Gamma_{0}
be any node.

Remark 10.15
.

\mathcal{V}(n)_{\mathrm{loc}}
(and also
\mathcal{F}(n)_{\mathrm{loc}}
) has a basis indexed by 3d Young diagrams of
n
boxes. A
3d Young diagram
of
n
boxes is a set
\mathcal{D}
consisting of
n
triples
\{(i,j,k)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}^{3}\}
such that
(i,j,k)\in\mathcal{D}
implies
(i^{-},j,k),(i,j^{-},k),(i,j,k^{-})\in\mathcal{D}
, where
x^{-}=\max\{0,x-1\}
. The image of the specialization map

\mathsf{sp}\colon\mathcal{H}_{N}(n)_{\mathrm{loc}}\to\mathcal{V}(n)_{\mathrm{loc}}|_{t=N\hbar_{3}}

(which is injective by Theorem
10.12
) is spanned by those 3d Young diagrams
\mathcal{D}=\{(i,j,k)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}^{3}\}
such that
k<N
for all
(i,j,k)\in\mathcal{D}
.

Remark 10.16
.

We expect that the
K
-theoretic analog of the discussions in this section will provide a geometric incarnation of the constructions of MacMahon modules, resonance locus, and submodules for the quantum toroidal algebra
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
in
[
FJMM
]
.

10.3.
More examples: spiked instantons and related geometries

Let us consider the following framings with potentials.

(4)

(Free-field realization) We fix

\underline{\mathbf{d}}=\mathbf{L}+\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{N}=(L+M+N,L+M+N),

the potential is
\mathsf{W}_{\mathrm{cub}}
given below, and we require that
\mathsf{T}_{0}
scales
B_{i}
with weight
q_{i}
and fixes
A_{i}
, for all
i=1,2,3
. Let
\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}=(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{L+M+N}
that acts on the framing vector space
\mathbb{C}^{L+M+N}
diagonally,

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\quad\mathsf{W}_{\mathrm{cub}}=Z[X,Y]+XA_{1}B_{1}+YA_{2}B_{2}+ZA_{3}B_{3},\,\,\mathsf{w}_{\mathrm{cub}}=\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{W}_{\mathrm{cub}}).

We define

\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N}(n):=H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{L}+\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w}_{\mathrm{cub}}),\quad\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N}:=H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{L}+\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w}_{\mathrm{cub}})=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N}(n).

(5)

(A degenerate module)
\underline{\mathbf{d}}=\mathbf{3}=(3,3)
, the potential is
\mathsf{W}_{L,M,N}
given below, and we require that
\mathsf{T}_{0}
scales
(B_{1},B_{2},B_{3})
with weights
(q_{1}^{L},q_{2}^{M},q_{3}^{N})
and fixes
A_{i}
, for all
i=1,2,3
. Let
\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}=(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{3}
that acts on the framing vector space
\mathbb{C}^{3}
diagonally,

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\quad\mathsf{W}_{L,M,N}=Z[X,Y]+B_{1}X^{L}A_{1}+B_{2}Y^{M}A_{2}+B_{3}Z^{N}A_{3},\,\,\mathsf{w}_{L,M,N}=\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{W}_{L,M,N}).

We define

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{L,M,N}(n):=H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{3}),\mathsf{w}_{L,M,N}),\quad\mathcal{H}_{L,M,N}:=H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{3}),\mathsf{w}_{L,M,N})=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}\mathcal{H}_{L,M,N}(n).

By dimensional reduction, we have

\mathcal{S}_{0,0,N}\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N})),

where
\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N})
is the Nakajima variety of the doubled Jordan quiver with quiver data
(X,Y,B,A)
. The stable envelope

\mathbf{Stab}\colon\mathcal{H}_{1}[a_{1}]\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1}[a_{N}]\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{N}))

is a
\mathsf{Y}_{Q^{\prime}}^{\text{MO}}
-module isomorphism after
\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}
-localization. Here
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{N})
are equivariant parameters in
H_{\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt})=\mathbb{C}[a_{1},\ldots,a_{N}]
. According to
[
MO
, Prop. 19.2.2]
, the action of
\mathsf{Y}_{Q^{\prime}}^{\text{MO}}
on
\mathcal{H}_{1}[a_{1}]\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1}[a_{N}]
factors through the mode algebra
\mathfrak{U}(\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}))
of the W-algebra
\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
. It is also known that the map
\mathsf{Y}_{Q^{\prime}}^{\text{MO}}\to\mathfrak{U}(\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}))
has a dense image by
[
MO
, Prop. 19.2.3]
. We define a
\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
-algebra map
to be the composition

\Psi^{(3)}_{N}\colon Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})\xrightarrow[\cong]{\varrho_{0}}\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})\cong\mathsf{Y}_{Q^{\prime},\mathrm{loc}}^{\text{MO}}\to\mathfrak{U}(\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}))_{\mathrm{loc}},

and denote
Y_{0,0,N}
to be the quotient algebra

Y_{0,0,N}:=Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})/\ker(\Psi^{(3)}_{N}).

For a pair
(i,j)
of distinct elements in
\{1,2,3\}
, let
s_{ij}\in\operatorname{Aut}\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
be the automorphism that swaps
\hbar_{i}\leftrightarrow\hbar_{j}
and fixes the third one. By Remark
10.2
,
s_{ij}
extends to a
\mathbb{C}
-algebra automorphism
s_{ij}\colon Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})\cong Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
. For
i\in\{1,2\}
, we define a
\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
-skew linear map
to be the composition

\displaystyle\Psi^{(i)}_{N}\colon Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})\xrightarrow[\cong]{s_{i3}}Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})\xrightarrow{\Psi^{(i)}_{N}}\mathfrak{U}(\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}))_{\mathrm{loc}}\>,

and denote the quotient algebras

Y_{N,0,0}:=Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})/\ker(\Psi^{(1)}_{N}),\quad Y_{0,N,0}:=Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})/\ker(\Psi^{(2)}_{N}).

We note that the
Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
-module structure on
\mathcal{S}_{N,0,0}
is obtained from
\mathcal{S}_{0,0,N}
by pullback along the
\mathbb{C}
-algebra automorphism
s_{13}\colon Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})\cong Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
, so the
Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
-action on
\mathcal{S}_{N,0,0}
factors through
Y_{N,0,0}
. Similarly, the
Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
-action on
\mathcal{S}_{0,N,0}
factors through
Y_{0,N,0}
.

Definition 10.17
.

We define the
Y-algebra

Y_{L,M,N}
to be the quotient of
Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
by the kernel of the compositions

(10.12)

\displaystyle Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})\xrightarrow{(\Delta\otimes\operatorname{id})\circ\Delta}Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})\widehat{\otimes}Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})\widehat{\otimes}Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})\xrightarrow{\Psi^{(1)}_{L}\otimes\Psi^{(2)}_{M}\otimes\Psi^{(3)}_{N}}Y_{L,0,0}\widehat{\otimes}Y_{0,M,0}\widehat{\otimes}Y_{0,0,N}.

Proposition 10.18
.

The
Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
-actions on
\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N,\mathrm{loc}}
and on
\mathcal{H}_{L,M,N,\mathrm{loc}}
factor through
Y_{L,M,N}
. Moreover,
\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N,\mathrm{loc}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{L,M,N,\mathrm{loc}}
are irreducible
Y_{L,M,N}
modules after
\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}
-localizations.

Proof.

The stable envelope

\operatorname{Stab}\colon\mathcal{S}_{L,0,0,\mathrm{loc}}\otimes\mathcal{S}_{0,M,0,\mathrm{loc}}\otimes\mathcal{S}_{0,0,N,\mathrm{loc}}\to\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N,\mathrm{loc}}

is a
Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
-module map and is an isomorphism after
\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}
-localization. This implies that the
Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
-action on
\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N,\mathrm{loc}}
factors through the map (
10.12
), so the
Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
-actions on
\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N,\mathrm{loc}}
factors through
Y_{L,M,N}
.

\mathcal{H}_{0,0,N}
is the same as
\mathcal{H}_{N}
in §
10.2
, and
\mathcal{H}_{N,\mathrm{loc}}
is isomorphic to a
Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
-submodule of
\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}[(N-1)\hbar_{3}]\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}[\hbar_{3}]\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1,\mathrm{loc}}
by Theorem
10.12
. In particular,
Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
-action on
\mathcal{H}_{N,\mathrm{loc}}
factors through
Y_{0,0,N}
. Using the automorphism
s_{13}

(resp.
s_{23}
), we see that
Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
-action on
\mathcal{H}_{N,0,0}
(resp. on
\mathcal{H}_{0,N,0}
) factors through
Y_{0,0,N}
(resp.
Y_{0,N,0}
). The stable envelope

\operatorname{Stab}\colon\mathcal{H}_{L,0,0,\mathrm{loc}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{0,M,0,\mathrm{loc}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{0,0,N,\mathrm{loc}}\to\mathcal{H}_{L,M,N,\mathrm{loc}}

is a
Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
-module map and is an isomorphism after
\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}
-localization. Therefore, the
Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
-actions on
\mathcal{H}_{L,M,N,\mathrm{loc}}
factors through
Y_{L,M,N}
.

By Proposition
7.6
, to prove that
\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N,\mathrm{loc}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{L,M,N,\mathrm{loc}}
are irreducible, it is enough to show that they are compact and cogenerated by vacuum.

\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N}
is compact because

\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{L}+\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{N})^{\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}=\prod_{i=1}^{L+M+N}\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1}),

and the critical locus decomposes accordingly

\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{M}(n,\mathbf{L}+\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{N})}(\mathsf{w}_{L,M,N})^{\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}=\bigsqcup_{\sum n_{i}=n}\prod_{i=1}^{L+M+N}\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{M}(n_{i},\mathbf{1})}(\mathsf{w}_{1}),

and the latter has compact
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-fixed locus as we have seen in the proof of Theorem
10.12
(a). A similar argument shows that
\mathcal{H}_{L,M,N}
is compact.

In both
\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N}
and
\mathcal{H}_{L,M,N}
cases, consider the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
action that scales
B_{i}
with weight
1
for all
i=1,2,3
and fixes other quiver data. By
[
COZZ1
, §7.2]
, this
\mathbb{G}_{m}
action induces specializations

\displaystyle\mathsf{sp}_{\mathcal{S}}\colon\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N}\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{L}+\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w}),\quad\mathsf{sp}_{\mathcal{H}}\colon\mathcal{H}_{L,M,N}\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{3}),\mathsf{w}),

which are
Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
-module maps. After
(\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}})
-localization,
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathcal{S}}
is a tensor product of
(L+M+N)
copies of the specialization map
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w}_{1})_{\mathrm{loc}}\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1}),\mathsf{w})_{\mathrm{loc}}
, which is injective by Theorem
10.12
(c). Therefore,
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathcal{S}}
is injective after localization. Similarly,
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathcal{H}}
is injective after localization. Then, it is enough to show that
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{L}+\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w})
and
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{3}),\mathsf{w})
are cogenerated by vacuum. By Theorem
7.3
, it is enough to check the conditions (i) and (ii) in
loc. cit.
. Condition (i) is obvious for both
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{L}+\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w})
and
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{3}),\mathsf{w})
. For condition (ii), we claim that specialization maps

\begin{gathered}\mathsf{sp}_{\mathbf{L}+\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{N}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{L}+\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{L}+\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{N})),\\
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathbf{3}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{3}),\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{3})),\end{gathered}

are injective. In fact, since
\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{3})
and
\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{L}+\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{N})
are vector bundles over the stack of unframed representations
\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{0})
and the potential
\mathsf{w}
is the pullback one, we have natural isomorphisms

\begin{gathered}H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{L}+\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{N}),\mathsf{w})\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{0}),\mathsf{w})\otimes H_{\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt}),\quad H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{L}+\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{N}))\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{0}))\otimes H_{\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt}),\\
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{3}),\mathsf{w})\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{0}),\mathsf{w})\otimes H_{\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt}),\quad H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{3}))\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{0}))\otimes H_{\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{fr}}}(\operatorname{pt}).\end{gathered}

Moreover,
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathbf{L}+\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{N}}
and
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathbf{3}}
are obtained by the specialization map on the base stack
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathbf{0}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{0}),\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{0}))
via the aforementioned isomorphisms. As we have seen in the proof of Theorem
10.12
(c),
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathbf{0}}
is injective. This implies that
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathbf{L}+\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{N}}
and
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathbf{3}}
are injective; hence
\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N}
and
\mathcal{H}_{L,M,N}
are cogenerated by vacuum.
∎

Remark 10.19
.

Since the
\mathcal{W}_{N}
vertex algebra is a vertex subalgebra of tensor product of
N
copies of Heisenberg algebras, we have

\displaystyle Y_{0,0,N}\subset\widehat{\mathsf{H}}^{\widehat{\otimes}N}_{-\hbar_{3}/\sigma_{3}},

where
\widehat{\mathsf{H}}_{\lambda}
is a completion of the Heisenberg algebra
\mathsf{H}_{\lambda}
with level
\lambda
, which is a
\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
-algebra generated by
\{b_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
with relations
[b_{n},b_{m}]=\lambda n\delta_{n,-m}
. Then it follows from the definition of
Y_{L,M,N}
that

\displaystyle Y_{L,M,N}\subset\widehat{\mathsf{H}}^{\widehat{\otimes}L}_{-\hbar_{1}/\sigma_{3}}\widehat{\otimes}\widehat{\mathsf{H}}^{\widehat{\otimes}M}_{-\hbar_{2}/\sigma_{3}}\widehat{\otimes}\widehat{\mathsf{H}}^{\widehat{\otimes}N}_{-\hbar_{3}/\sigma_{3}}.

The computations in
[
RSYZ1
, §10]
show that the image of
Y_{L,M,N}
in
\widehat{\mathsf{H}}^{\widehat{\otimes}L}_{-\hbar_{1}/\sigma_{3}}\widehat{\otimes}\widehat{\mathsf{H}}^{\widehat{\otimes}M}_{-\hbar_{2}/\sigma_{3}}\widehat{\otimes}\widehat{\mathsf{H}}^{\widehat{\otimes}N}_{-\hbar_{3}/\sigma_{3}}
is contained in a subalgebra
\mathfrak{U}(\mathcal{W}_{L,M,N})
defined by the intersection of kernels of
L+M+N-1
screening operators
[
RSYZ1
, §9]
.
\mathcal{W}_{L,M,N}
is the vertex algebra which is the annihilator of the aforementioned screening operators. It is conjectured by Procházka and Rapčák
[
PR
]
that
\mathcal{W}_{L,M,N}
is isomorphic to Gaiotto-Rapčák’s corner vertex algebra
\mathcal{Y}_{L,M,N}
in
[
GR
]
.

10.4.
Quantum multiplication by divisors on
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{C}^{3})

Consider quiver with potential
(Q,\mathsf{W})
given by (
10.1
), and quiver varieties

X:=\mathcal{M}(n,(1,0)),\quad Y:=\mathcal{M}(n,(1,1)).

We trivially extend
\mathsf{w}
to a potential on
X
and
Y
.
For any
d\in\operatorname{Hom}(\mathrm{char}(\mathrm{GL}_{n}),\mathbb{Z})\cong\mathbb{Z}
, take

[Z]:=M_{d}(\gamma)\in H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(X^{2},\mathsf{w}\boxminus\mathsf{w})

to be (
5.1
).
There is a projection

p\colon Y\to X

and we denote
\hbar
to be the equivariant parameter of its fiber
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action.
Let

\mathsf{T}:=\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{e^{\hbar}}.

There is an isomorphism

p^{*}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(X,\mathsf{w})[\hbar]\cong H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,p^{*}\mathsf{w}).

Corollary 10.20
.

For an
\mathsf{T}
-equivariant line bundle
L
on
Y
, we have

c_{1}(L)\,\widetilde{\star}\,\cdot=c_{1}(L)\cup\cdot+\sigma_{3}\deg(L)\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}\frac{iz^{i}}{1-z^{i}}\,J_{-i}\,J_{i}\,+\mathrm{const},

where
\sigma_{3}=\hbar_{1}\hbar_{2}\hbar_{3}
,
J_{i}=\frac{\sigma_{3}^{-i}}{(i-1)!}\text{ad}^{i-1}_{-f_{1}}f_{0}
,
J_{-i}=-\frac{\sigma_{3}^{-i}}{(i-1)!}\text{ad}^{i-1}_{e_{1}}e_{0}
for
i>0
,
and
e_{i}
,
f_{i}
are part of the generators of Yangian
Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}_{1}})
(Definition
10.1
), which act on the critical cohomology via Theorem
10.7
.

Proof.

We denote
Y_{n}:=\mathcal{M}(n,(1,1))
. By
[
COZZ1
, Ex. 7.19]
, there is a specialization map

H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y_{n},p^{*}\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y_{n}).

Taking direct sum over
n
, we obtain
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-module map

\bigoplus_{n\geqslant 0}H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y_{n},p^{*}\mathsf{w})_{\mathrm{loc}}\to\bigoplus_{n\geqslant 0}H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y_{n})_{\mathrm{loc}},

such that the vacuum (i.e.
H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y_{0},p^{*}\mathsf{w})_{\mathrm{loc}}
) maps to vacuum (i.e.
H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y_{0})_{\mathrm{loc}}
).
By Theorem
10.12
(̇b), the LHS is an irreducible
\mathsf{Y}_{0}(Q,\mathsf{W})
-module.
So the specialization maps are injective.
By applying Corollary
5.11
to the symmetric quiver variety
Y
with a function
\mathsf{T}
-invariant function
\mathsf{w}\colon Y\to\mathbb{C}
, and comparing (
4.12
) and (
10.8
), we are done.
∎

We deduce the following formula of quantum multiplication by divisors for
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{C}^{3})
using Theorem
5.13
.

Corollary 10.21
.

For the above
(X,\mathsf{w})
and any
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-equivariant divisor class
L
on
X
, we have

(10.13)

c_{1}(L)\,\widetilde{\star}_{d}\,\cdot=\sigma_{3}\deg(L)\cdot d\cdot J_{-d}\,J_{d}+\mathrm{const}\,,\quad\forall\,\,d>0.

Here
\sigma_{3}=\hbar_{1}\hbar_{2}\hbar_{3}
,
J_{i}=\frac{\sigma_{3}^{-i}}{(i-1)!}\text{ad}^{i-1}_{-f_{1}}f_{0}
,
J_{-i}=-\frac{\sigma_{3}^{-i}}{(i-1)!}\text{ad}^{i-1}_{e_{1}}e_{0}
for
i>0
,
and
e_{i}
,
f_{i}
are part of the generators of shifted Yangian
Y_{-1}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}_{1}})
(
10.1
), which act on the critical cohomology via Theorem
10.7
.

Proof.

By Theorem
5.13
, it is enough to show
\mathrm{Cas}_{i,-1}=\sigma_{3}J_{-i}\,J_{i}
for all
i>0
. We denote
e(u),f(u)
,
e(u)^{\prime},f(u)^{\prime}
to be the elements in the Yangian
Y_{0}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}_{1}})
,
Y_{-1}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}_{1}})
respectively.

From the proof of Lemma
4.27
, we have equations of operators on critical cohomology:

(p^{*})^{-1}e(u)\,p^{*}=H_{\hbar}\,e(u)^{\prime}\,H_{\hbar}^{-1}=\frac{\hbar-c_{1}(\mathcal{L})}{u-c_{1}(\mathcal{L})}\,[\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(n+1,n,\underline{\mathbf{d}})]=(\hbar-u)e(u)^{\prime}+e_{0}^{\prime},

(p^{*})^{-1}f(u)\,p^{*}=-f(u)^{\prime}.

By expanding in the
u
-power, we obtain

(p^{*})^{-1}e_{k}\,p^{*}=\hbar e^{\prime}_{k}-e_{k+1}^{\prime},\quad(p^{*})^{-1}f_{k}\,p^{*}=-f_{k}^{\prime}.

This implies that

(10.14)

(p^{*})^{-1}\mathrm{Cas}_{i}\,p^{*}=\sigma_{3}(p^{*})^{-1}J_{-i}J_{i}\,p^{*}=\sigma_{3}(-1)^{i}\hbar^{i}J_{-i}^{\prime}J_{i}^{\prime}+\mathrm{l.o.t.},

where
J_{i}^{\prime}
differs from
J_{i}
by replacing
e_{k},f_{k}
by
e_{k}^{\prime},f_{k}^{\prime}
respectively, and
\mathrm{l.o.t.}
denotes the lower order terms of
\hbar
. Then
\mathrm{Cas}_{i,-1}=\sigma_{3}J_{-i}\,J_{i}
by Defintion
4.50
.
∎

Remark 10.22
.

In this case, we have
\widetilde{\star}_{d}=(-1)^{d}\star_{d}
.

Remark 10.23
.

By degree counting, we have

c_{1}(L)\,\widetilde{\star}_{d}\,\cdot\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}_{*}(X,\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}_{*+2-2d}(X,\mathsf{w}),

so the constant term in (
10.13
) vanishes when
d>1
.

Appendix A
Positive part of several Yangians

In this appendix, we consider three classes of examples of (shifted, affine) Yangians,
whose positive parts are described using explicit generators and relations.
We construct surjective algebra homomorphisms from them to the
spherical nilpotent CoHA of corresponding symmetric quivers with potentials (Propositions
A.3
,
A.7
,
A.10
). These are used in the constructions of surjective algebra homomorphisms from
the entire (shifted, affine) Yangians to the Drinfeld double Yangians (Examples
3.9
,
3.10
,
3.11
).

A.1.
Positive part of the Yangian of a symmetrizable Kac-Moody algebra

Definition A.1
.

Let the pair
(A,D)
be a
generalized Cartan matrix with symmetrizer
, that is,

•

A=(a_{ij})_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant n}\in\mathrm{Mat}_{n\times n}(\mathbb{Z})
such that:
a_{ii}=2
for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
;
a_{ij}\leqslant 0
for all
1\leqslant i\neq j\leqslant n
;
a_{ij}\neq 0\Leftrightarrow a_{ji}\neq 0
.

•

D=\operatorname{diag}(d_{1},\ldots,d_{n})
is a diagonal matrix with positive integer entries such that
DA
is symmetric.

From the pair
(A,D)
, following
[
GLS
]
, we construct a quiver
\widetilde{Q}
with potential
\mathsf{W}
. We start with a quiver
Q
with
Q_{0}=\{1,\ldots,n\}
, and for any
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n
, the number of arrows from
i
to
j
is

\displaystyle n_{ij}=|\gcd(a_{ij},a_{ji})|\>.

Let
\widetilde{Q}
be the
tripled quiver
of
Q
, then the set of edges in
\widetilde{Q}
decomposes as
\widetilde{Q}_{1}=Q_{1}\sqcup Q_{1}^{*}\sqcup\mathcal{E}
where
\mathcal{E}\cong Q_{0}
is the set of added edge loops. Let
\{X_{a}\}_{a\in Q_{1}}
,
\{X_{a^{*}}\}_{a^{*}\in Q^{*}_{1}}
,
\{\Phi_{i}\}_{i\in Q_{0}}
be the edges in
Q_{1}
,
Q_{1}^{*}
, and
\mathcal{E}
respectively.

When
i<j
, we define:

\displaystyle l_{ij}=\bigg|\frac{a_{ij}}{n_{ij}}\bigg|\>,\quad l_{ji}=\bigg|\frac{a_{ji}}{n_{ij}}\bigg|\>,

and the
potential
14
14

14

Our convention of potentials is the same as
[
YZ1
, Eqn. (1)]
which differs from
[
GLS
]
by the transpose of
A
, see
[
YZ1
, Rmk. 4.2]
.
:

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}:=\sum_{(a:i\to j)\in Q_{1}}\Phi_{i}^{l_{ij}}X_{a^{*}}X_{a}-\Phi_{j}^{l_{ji}}X_{a}X_{a^{*}},\quad\mathsf{w}:=\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{W}).

Note that the Jacobian algebra of
\mathsf{W}
is the generalized preprojective algebra of
[
GLS
]
.
In the finite dimensional simple Lie algebra case, the potential is also constructed by
[
CD
]
.

Let us label the set of arrows in
Q_{1}
from
i
to
j
by
\{a_{1},\ldots,a_{n_{ij}}\}
. Take
\mathsf{T}_{0}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
which scales
X_{a_{p}}
with weight
(n_{ij}/2+1-p)d_{i}l_{ij}
, scales
X_{a^{*}_{p}}
with weight
(p-n_{ij}/2)d_{i}l_{ij}
, and scales
\Phi_{i}
with weight
-d_{i}
. Then
\mathsf{W}
is
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
-invariant,
as
DA
is symmetric.

Definition A.2
.

The
Kac-Moody Lie algebra
associated to
(A,D)
, denoted
\mathfrak{g}_{A,D}
, is generated by
\{e_{i},f_{i}\>|\>i\in Q_{0}\}
with relations

\begin{gathered}\operatorname{ad}_{e_{i}}^{1-a_{ij}}(e_{j})=0=\operatorname{ad}_{f_{i}}^{1-a_{ij}}(f_{j}),\quad[e_{i},f_{j}]=0,\;i\neq j,\\
[[e_{i},f_{i}],e_{j}]=a_{ij}e_{j},\quad[[e_{i},f_{i}],f_{j}]=-a_{ij}f_{j},\;\forall\,\,i,j\in Q_{0}.\end{gathered}

The
positive part

Y^{+}(\mathfrak{g}_{A,D})
of the Yangian of
\mathfrak{g}_{A,D}

[
GTL
,
YZ2
]
is generated by
\{x^{+}_{i,r}\}_{i\in Q_{0}}^{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
, subject to relations

(A.1)

\begin{gathered}(u-v)[x^{+}_{i}(u),x^{+}_{j}(v)]-d_{i}a_{ij}\hbar/2\{x^{+}_{i}(u),x^{+}_{j}(v)\}=[x^{+}_{i,0},x^{+}_{j}(v)]-[x^{+}_{i}(u),x^{+}_{j,0}],\;\forall\,i,j\in Q_{0},\\
[x^{+}_{i}(u),x^{+}_{j}(v)]=0,\;\text{if }a_{ij}=0,\\
\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{1-a_{ij}}}[x^{+}_{i}(u_{\sigma(1)}),[x^{+}_{i}(u_{\sigma(2)}),[\cdots,[x^{+}_{i}(u_{\sigma(1-a_{kl})}),x^{+}_{j}(v)]]]]=0,\;\text{for }i\neq j.\end{gathered}

Here
x^{+}_{i}(z):=\sum_{r\geqslant 0}x^{+}_{i,r}z^{-r-1}
.

We establish a surjective algebra map from the positive part of Yangian to the spherical
nilpotent
CoHA of
(\widetilde{Q},\mathsf{W})
as defined in §
3.2
. A related result when the target is the spherical shuffle algebra was proven in
[
YZ2
, Thm. 5.1]
.

Proposition A.3
.

There is a surjective map of
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
-algebras:

Y^{+}(\mathfrak{g}_{A,D})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\widetilde{Q},\mathsf{W}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar),\quad x^{+}_{i}(z)\mapsto e_{i}(z).

Proof.

It is enough to show that
x^{+}_{i}(z)\mapsto e_{i}(z)
gives a
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
-algebra homomorphism, then the surjectivity follows from the definition that
\{e_{i}(z)\}_{i\in Q_{0}}
generates
\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\widetilde{Q},\mathsf{W}}
. Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\widetilde{Q},0}
be the spherical CoHA with zero potential, which is isomorphic to the spherical shuffle algebra. By
[
YZ2
, Thm. 5.1]
, there is a
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
-algebra homomorphism

Y^{+}(\mathfrak{g}_{A,D})\to\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\widetilde{Q},0},\quad x^{+}_{i}(z)\mapsto\tilde{e}_{i}(z),

where
\tilde{e}_{i}(z)=\sum_{r\geqslant 0}\tilde{e}_{i,r}z^{-r-1}
is the generating function of the CoHA elements

\tilde{e}_{i,r}:=c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i})^{r}\cap\left[\mathfrak{M}(\delta_{i},\mathbf{0})\right],\quad\forall\,r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0},

and
\mathcal{L}_{i}
is the universal line bundle on
\mathfrak{M}(\delta_{i},\mathbf{0})\cong[\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{C}^{*}]
. The specialization map

\mathsf{sp}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\widetilde{Q},\mathsf{W}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\widetilde{Q},0}

is a
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
-algebra homomorphism. In Lemma
A.4
below, we show that
\mathsf{sp}
is injective after
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
-localization. Let us regard
\tilde{e}_{i}(z)
as generators of the spherical CoHA with potential
\mathsf{W}
, denoted
\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\widetilde{Q},\mathsf{W}}
, then
x^{+}_{i}(z)\mapsto\tilde{e}_{i}(z)
gives a
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
-algebra homomorphism

Y^{+}(\mathfrak{g}_{A,D})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)\to\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\widetilde{Q},\mathsf{W}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar).

The pushforward along the closed embedding

\iota\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\widetilde{Q},\mathsf{W}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{\widetilde{Q},\mathsf{W}}

maps
e_{i}(z)
to
d_{i}\hbar\>\tilde{e}_{i}(z)
. Note that
x^{+}_{i}(z)\mapsto d_{i}\hbar\>x^{+}_{i}(z)
gives a
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
-algebra automorphism

Y^{+}(\mathfrak{g}_{A,D})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)\cong Y^{+}(\mathfrak{g}_{A,D})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar).

Then it is enough to check that
\iota
is injective after
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
-localization for those degrees that appear in the relations (
A.1
). This is checked in Lemma
A.5
below.
∎

Lemma A.4
.

The
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
-localized specialization map

\displaystyle\mathsf{sp}\colon H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}),\mathsf{w})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)\to H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}))\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)

is injective.

Proof.

Consider an auxiliary quiver
Q^{\sharp}
which is obtained from
\widetilde{Q}
by removing all the arrows in
Q_{1}^{*}
. Then
\mathfrak{M}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})
is a vector bundle
E
over
\mathfrak{M}(Q^{\sharp},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})
with natural projection
\pi
, and
\mathsf{w}
can be written as

\displaystyle\mathsf{w}=\langle e,s\rangle,\text{ $e$ is the fiber coordinate of $E$, }\quad s=(X_{a}\Phi_{i}^{l_{ij}}-\Phi_{j}^{l_{ji}}X_{a})_{(a:i\to j)\in Q_{1}}\in\Gamma(\mathfrak{M}(Q^{\sharp},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}),E^{\vee}).

Then we have dimensional reduction isomorphism

H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}\left(\mathfrak{M}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}),\mathsf{w}\right)\cong H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathfrak{Z}(s)),

where
\mathfrak{Z}(s)
is the zero locus of
s
in
\mathfrak{M}(Q^{\sharp},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})
. By
[
COZZ1
, Rem.  7.16]
, we have

\mathsf{sp}=\tilde{j}_{*}\circ\delta^{-1},

where
\delta\colon H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\pi^{-1}\mathfrak{Z}(s))\cong H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathfrak{M}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}),\mathsf{w})
is the dimensional reduction isomorphism, and
\tilde{j}\colon\pi^{-1}\mathfrak{Z}(s)\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{M}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})
is the closed embedding. Then, it is equivalent to show that
\tilde{j}_{*}
is injective after
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
-localization, or by smooth pullback isomorphism, equivalent to show that
j_{*}
is injective after
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
-localization, where
j\colon\mathfrak{Z}(s)\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{M}(Q^{\sharp},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})
is the closed embedding.

Consider Jordan-Hölder map

\mathsf{JH}\colon\mathfrak{M}\left(Q^{\sharp},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}\right)\to\mathcal{M}_{0}\left(Q^{\sharp},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}\right)=\mathrm{Rep}_{Q^{\sharp}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v}).

Since all the cycles in
Q^{\sharp}
are compositions of edge loops, we have

\mathcal{M}_{0}\left(Q^{\sharp},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}\right)\cong\mathfrak{gl}(\mathbf{v})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v}).

Then we have
\mathcal{M}_{0}\left(Q^{\sharp},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}\right)^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}=\{0\}
. Set
\mathfrak{M}(Q^{\sharp},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})^{\mathrm{nil}}:=\mathsf{JH}^{-1}(\{0\})
, then the pushforward map

H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}\left(\mathfrak{M}(Q^{\sharp},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})^{\mathrm{nil}}\right)\to H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}\left(\mathfrak{M}(Q^{\sharp},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})\right)

is obtained by

\displaystyle H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}\left(\mathfrak{M}(Q^{\sharp},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})^{\mathrm{nil}}\right)

\displaystyle\cong H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{0}(Q^{\sharp},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}),i_{*}i^{!}\mathsf{JH}_{*}\omega_{\mathfrak{M}(Q^{\sharp},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}\right)

\displaystyle\to H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{0}(Q^{\sharp},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}\right),\mathsf{JH}_{*}\omega_{\mathfrak{M}(Q^{\sharp},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})})\cong H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}\left(\mathfrak{M}(Q^{\sharp},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})\right),

where
i\colon\{0\}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{0}(Q^{\sharp},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})
is the natural map, which becomes an isomorphism after
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
-localization.

Similarly, if we set
\mathfrak{Z}(s)^{\mathrm{nil}}=\mathfrak{Z}(s)\cap\mathsf{JH}^{-1}(\{0\})
, then the pushforward map
H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathfrak{Z}(s)^{\mathrm{nil}})\to H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathfrak{Z}(s))
becomes an isomorphism after
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
-localization. Therfeore, it suffices to show that
j^{\mathrm{nil}}_{*}
is injective after
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
-localization, where
j^{\mathrm{nil}}\colon\mathfrak{Z}(s)^{\mathrm{nil}}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{M}(Q^{\sharp},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})^{\mathrm{nil}}
is the closed embedding.

We have

H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathfrak{Z}(s)^{\mathrm{nil}})\cong H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(Z(s)^{\mathrm{nil}}),\quad H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathfrak{M}(Q^{\sharp},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})^{\mathrm{nil}})\cong H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(\mathrm{Rep}_{Q^{\sharp}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})^{\mathrm{nil}}),

where
Z(s)^{\mathrm{nil}}
and
\mathrm{Rep}_{Q^{\sharp}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})^{\mathrm{nil}}
are the preimages of
\mathfrak{Z}(s)^{\mathrm{nil}}
and
\mathfrak{M}(Q^{\sharp},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})^{\mathrm{nil}}
in
\mathrm{Rep}_{Q^{\sharp}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})
respectively.

We claim that

(a)

H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(Z(s)^{\mathrm{nil}})
and
H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(\mathrm{Rep}_{Q^{\sharp}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})^{\mathrm{nil}})
are free modules of
H_{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(\operatorname{pt})
, and

(b)

the natural map
H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(Z(s)^{\mathrm{nil}})\to H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(\mathrm{Rep}_{Q^{\sharp}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})^{\mathrm{nil}})
is injective.

The Claim (b) implies that
j^{\mathrm{nil}}_{*}
is injective without
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
-localization, and this will imply the lemma.

Notice that
\mathrm{Rep}_{Q^{\sharp}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})^{\mathrm{nil}}=\mathrm{Rep}_{Q}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})\times\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{gl}(\mathbf{v})}
, where
\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{gl}(\mathbf{v})}
is the nilpotent cone of
\mathfrak{gl}(\mathbf{v})
. Note that
\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{gl}(\mathbf{v})}
is stratified by finitely many
\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})
-orbits

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{gl}(\mathbf{v})}=\bigsqcup_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}=(\lambda_{i})_{i\in Q_{0}}}\mathcal{N}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}},\quad\mathcal{N}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}=\prod_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathcal{N}_{\lambda_{i}},

where
\lambda_{i}
is a partition of
\mathbf{v}_{i}
and
\mathcal{N}_{\lambda_{i}}
is the set of
\mathbf{v}_{i}\times\mathbf{v}_{i}
nilpotent matrices of type
\lambda_{i}
.
Denote the natural projection

\operatorname{pr}\colon\mathrm{Rep}_{Q^{\sharp}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})^{\mathrm{nil}}\to\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{gl}(\mathbf{v})}.

To prove the Claim (a), using the excision sequence, it is enough to prove that

H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}\left(Z(s)^{\mathrm{nil}}\cap\operatorname{pr}^{-1}\mathcal{N}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right),\quad H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}\left(\operatorname{pr}^{-1}\mathcal{N}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right)

are free modules of
H_{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(\operatorname{pt})
and concentrated in even degree for all
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
. Note that

\operatorname{pr}^{-1}\mathcal{N}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\cong\mathrm{Rep}_{Q}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})\times\mathcal{N}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}

is an equivariant vector bundle on
\mathcal{N}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
, and
\mathcal{N}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\cong\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})/C_{\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(x)
, where
C_{\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(x)
is the centralizer of a point
x\in\mathcal{N}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
. Then we have

H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}\left(\operatorname{pr}^{-1}\mathcal{N}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right)\cong H_{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times C_{\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(x)}(\operatorname{pt}),

and the latter is a free module of
H_{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(\operatorname{pt})
and concentrated in even degree. Let
x=(\Phi_{i})_{i\in Q_{0}}\in\mathcal{N}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
, then
\operatorname{pr}^{-1}(x)\cap Z(s)^{\mathrm{nil}}
is the subspace of
\mathrm{Rep}_{Q}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})
consisting of those
(X_{a})_{a\in Q_{1}}
which solve the equations

X_{a}\Phi_{i}^{l_{ij}}-\Phi_{j}^{l_{ji}}X_{a}=0,\quad\forall\,\,(a:i\to j)\in Q_{1}.

Since the equation is linear in
X
,
\operatorname{pr}^{-1}(x)\cap Z(s)^{\mathrm{nil}}
is a linear subspace of
\mathrm{Rep}_{Q}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})
. Transporting along the
\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})
-action, we see that
\operatorname{pr}^{-1}(x)\cap Z(s)^{\mathrm{nil}}
is a vector subbundle of
\mathrm{Rep}_{Q}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})\times\mathcal{N}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\to\mathcal{N}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
. In particular, we have

H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}\left(Z(s)^{\mathrm{nil}}\cap\operatorname{pr}^{-1}\mathcal{N}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right)\cong H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(\mathcal{N}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}})\cong H_{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times C_{\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(x)}(\operatorname{pt}),

which is a free module of
H_{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(\operatorname{pt})
and concentrated in even degree. This proves the Claim (a).

Since
\mathrm{Rep}_{Q^{\sharp}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}\subset Z(s)^{\mathrm{nil}}
, the
I
-localized pushforward map

H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}\left(Z(s)^{\mathrm{nil}}\right)_{I}\to H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}\left(\mathrm{Rep}_{Q^{\sharp}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})^{\mathrm{nil}}\right)_{I}

is an isomorphism, where
I\subset H_{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(\operatorname{pt})
is the prime ideal defined as the kernel of the projection
H_{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(\operatorname{pt})\twoheadrightarrow H_{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\operatorname{pt})
. Then the Claim (b) follows from the injectivity after
I
-localization and the freeness (Claim (a)).
∎

Lemma A.5
.

The pushforward along the closed embedding

\iota\colon H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}),\mathsf{w})_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})^{\mathrm{nil}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)\to H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}),\mathsf{w})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)

is injective when
\mathbf{v}=n\delta_{i},n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 1}
or
\mathbf{v}=m\delta_{i}+\delta_{j},i\neq j,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 1}
.

Proof.

Consider the Jordan-Hölder map
\mathsf{JH}\colon\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})\to\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})
where the target is the affine quotient

\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})=\mathrm{Rep}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v}).

Then
\iota
is induced by applying the hypercohomology
H^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}(\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}),-)
to the natural map between complexes:

\displaystyle i_{*}i^{!}\mathsf{JH}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}\to\mathsf{JH}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})},

where
i\colon\{0\}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})
is the closed embedding given by the trivial quiver representation. Note that
\mathsf{JH}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}
is supported on

\mathsf{JH}(\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\mathsf{w}))\cong\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\mathsf{w})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v}).

Then it suffices to show that the
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
-fixed point of
\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\mathsf{w})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})
is
\{0\}
in the following two cases:

(i)

\mathbf{v}=n\delta_{i}
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 1}
,

(ii)

\mathbf{v}=m\delta_{i}+\delta_{j}
,
i\neq j
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 1}
.

In the case (i),
\mathsf{w}
is zero on
\mathrm{Rep}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})
, and we have

\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})\cong\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[\operatorname{tr}(\Phi_{i}),\operatorname{tr}(\Phi_{i}^{2}),\ldots,\operatorname{tr}(\Phi_{i}^{n})].

Since
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
scales
\Phi_{i}
with nontrivial weight, we have
\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}=\{0\}
.

In the case (ii), without loss of generality, we can assume
i<j
, and the
i>j
case can be proven in the same way. Consider the natural map

\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\mathsf{w})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})\to\mathfrak{gl}(\mathbf{v})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})

which is given by sending
(\Phi_{k},X_{a},X_{a^{*}})
to
(\Phi_{k})_{k\in Q_{0}}\in\mathfrak{gl}(\mathbf{v})
. The
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
fixed point of
\mathfrak{gl}(\mathbf{v})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})
is
\{0\}
, so for any representative
(\Phi_{k},X_{a},X_{a^{*}})
of a
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
fixed point in
\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\mathsf{w})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})
,
(\Phi_{k})_{k\in Q_{0}}
must be nilpotent. Nilpotency implies that
\Phi_{j}=0
as
\mathbf{v}_{j}=1
, and the functions
\operatorname{tr}(\Phi_{i}^{s})
vanishes on
\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\mathsf{w})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})
for all
s>0
. Then, by invariant theory,
\mathbb{C}\left[\left(\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\mathsf{w})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})\right)^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}\right]
is generated by
\{X_{b}\Phi_{i}^{r}X_{a^{*}}\}_{(a,b:i\to j)\in Q_{1},r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
. On the other hand,
\partial\mathsf{w}/\partial X_{a}=0
gives
\Phi_{i}^{l_{ij}}X_{a^{*}}=0
for all
(a:i\to j)\in Q_{1}
(here we have used
\Phi_{j}=0
). Then, only those
X_{b}\Phi_{i}^{r}X_{a^{*}}
with
r<l_{ij}
can be nonzero. However,
X_{b}\Phi_{i}^{r}X_{a^{*}}
has
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
weight
d_{i}(r-l_{ij})
which is nonzero if
r\neq l_{ij}
. Therefore,

\mathbb{C}\left[\left(\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\mathsf{w})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})\right)^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}\right]=\mathbb{C},

and we conclude that
\left(\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\mathsf{w})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})\right)^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}=\{0\}
.
∎

A.2.
Positive part of the affine Yangian of
\mathfrak{sl}_{n|m}

Let
m,n\in\mathbb{N}
with
\max\{mn,m,n\}\geqslant 3
. A
parity sequence
of type
(m,n)
is a tuple
\mathbf{s}=(s_{1},s_{2},\ldots,s_{m+n})
of
\pm 1

with
m
entries equal to
1
. Let
Q_{0}=\mathbb{Z}/(m+n)\mathbb{Z}
.

Given a parity sequence
\mathbf{s}
, we define matrices
A=(a_{i,j})_{i,j\in Q_{0}}
and
M=(m_{i,j})_{i,j\in Q_{0}}
by

(A.2)

\begin{gathered}a_{ij}=(s_{i}+s_{i+1})\delta_{ij}-s_{i}\delta_{i,j+1}-s_{j}\delta_{i+1,j},\\
m_{i,i+1}=-m_{i+1,i}=-s_{i+1},\\
m_{i,j}=0\text{ if }|i-j|\neq 1.\end{gathered}

Define a
symmetric quiver

Q=(Q_{0},Q_{1})
by setting
Q_{1}=Q_{1}^{+}\sqcup Q_{1}^{-}\sqcup\mathcal{E}
with

\displaystyle Q_{1}^{+}=\{X_{i}:i\to i+1\>|\>i\in Q_{0}\},\quad Q_{1}^{-}=\{Y_{i}:i+1\to i\>|\>i\in Q_{0}\},\quad\mathcal{E}=\{\Phi_{i}:i\to i\>|\>s_{i}+s_{i+1}\neq 0\}.

Note that the odd nodes are
Q_{0}^{\mathrm{f}}=\{i\in Q_{0}:s_{i}+s_{i+1}=0\}
. Define the
potential

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}=\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}\Phi_{i}(Y_{i}X_{i}-X_{i-1}Y_{i-1})+\sum_{j\in Q_{0}^{\mathrm{f}}}Y_{j}X_{j}X_{j-1}Y_{j-1},

and denote
\mathsf{w}=\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{W})
. Set
\mathsf{T}_{0}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\varepsilon}
and let it act on
Q
by

\displaystyle(q,t)\in\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\varepsilon}:(X_{i},Y_{i},\Phi_{j})\mapsto(q^{s_{i+1}}t^{-s_{i+1}}X_{i},q^{s_{i+1}}t^{s_{i+1}}Y_{i},q^{-2s_{j}}\Phi_{j}),\text{ for }i\in Q_{0},j\in Q_{0}^{\mathrm{f}}.

Then
\mathsf{W}
is
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-invariant.

Definition A.6

(
[
U
,
VV1
]
)
.

The positive part of the affine Yangian of
\mathfrak{sl}_{n|m}
, denoted
Y^{+}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_{n|m})
, is the
\mathbb{C}[\hbar,\varepsilon]
-algebra generated by
\{x^{+}_{i,r}\}_{i\in Q_{0},r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
subject to relations

(A.3)

\begin{gathered}(u-v+m_{ij}\varepsilon)[x^{+}_{i}(u),x^{+}_{j}(v)]-a_{ij}\hbar\{x^{+}_{i}(u),x^{+}_{j}(v)\}=[x^{+}_{i,0},x^{+}_{j}(v)]-[x^{+}_{i}(u),x^{+}_{j,0}],\;\forall\,i,j\in Q_{0},\\
[x^{+}_{i}(u),x^{+}_{j}(v)]=0,\;\text{if }a_{ij}=0,\\
\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{2}}[x^{+}_{i}(u_{\sigma(1)}),[x^{+}_{i}(u_{\sigma(2)}),x^{+}_{j}(v)]]=0,\;\text{for }i\in Q_{0}^{\mathrm{b}}\text{ and }|i-j|=1,\\
\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{2}}[x^{+}_{i}(u_{\sigma(1)}),[x^{+}_{i+1}(v),[x^{+}_{i}(u_{\sigma(2)}),x^{+}_{i-1}(w)]]]=0,\;\text{for }i\in Q_{0}^{\mathrm{f}}\text{ and }i\pm 1\in Q_{0}^{\mathrm{b}}.\end{gathered}

Here
[a,b]=ab-(-1)^{|a|\cdot|b|}ba
,
\{a,b\}=ab+(-1)^{|a|\cdot|b|}ba
, and
|x^{+}_{i,r}|=|i|

Proposition A.7
.

There is a surjective
\mathbb{C}(\hbar,\varepsilon)
-algebra map

\displaystyle Y^{+}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_{n|m})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar,\varepsilon]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar,\varepsilon)\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar,\varepsilon]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar,\varepsilon),\quad x^{+}_{i}\mapsto e_{i}(z).

Proof.

It is enough to show that
x^{+}_{i}(z)\mapsto e_{i}(z)
gives a
\mathbb{C}(\hbar)
-algebra homomorphism, then the surjectivity follows from the definition that
\{e_{i}(z)\}_{i\in Q_{0}}
generates
\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}
. Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}
be the spherical CoHA with potential
\mathsf{W}
.

By
[
VV1
, Thm. C.2]
, there is a
\mathbb{C}[\hbar,\varepsilon]
-algebra homomorphism

Y^{+}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_{n|m})\to\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}},\quad x^{+}_{i}(z)\mapsto\tilde{e}_{i}(z),

where
\tilde{e}_{i}(z)=\sum_{r\geqslant 0}\tilde{e}_{i,r}z^{-r-1}
is the generating function of the CoHA elements

\tilde{e}_{i,r}:=c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i})^{r}\cap\left[\mathfrak{M}(\delta_{i},\mathbf{0})\right],\quad\forall\,r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0},

and
\mathcal{L}_{i}
is the universal line bundle on
\mathfrak{M}(\delta_{i},\mathbf{0})\cong[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{g}_{i}}/\mathbb{C}^{*}]
. Here
\mathsf{g}_{i}=1
if
i
is even and
\mathsf{g}_{i}=0
if
i
is odd.

The pushforward along the closed embedding

\iota\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}

maps
e_{i}(z)
to
-2s_{i}\hbar\>\tilde{e}_{i}(z)
if
i
is even and to
\tilde{e}_{i}(z)
if
i
is odd.
Note that

x^{+}_{i}(z)\mapsto\begin{cases}-2s_{i}\hbar x^{+}_{i}(z),&i\in Q_{0}^{\mathrm{b}},\\
x^{+}_{i}(z),&i\in Q_{0}^{\mathrm{f}},\end{cases}

gives a
\mathbb{C}(\hbar,\varepsilon)
-algebra automorphism

Y^{+}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_{n|m})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar,\varepsilon]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar,\varepsilon)\cong Y^{+}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_{n|m})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar,\varepsilon]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar,\varepsilon).

Then it is enough to check that
\iota
is injective after
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-localization for those degrees that appear in the relations (
A.3
). This is checked in Lemma
A.8
below.
∎

Lemma A.8
.

The pushforward along the closed embedding

\iota\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}),\mathsf{w})_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})^{\mathrm{nil}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar,\varepsilon]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar,\varepsilon)\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}),\mathsf{w})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar,\varepsilon]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar,\varepsilon)

is injective when
\mathbf{v}=n\delta_{i},n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 1}
, or
\mathbf{v}=m\delta_{i}+\delta_{j},i\neq j,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 1}
, or
\mathbf{v}=\delta_{i-1}+2\delta_{i}+\delta_{i+1}
,
i
odd and
i\pm 1
even.

Proof.

Similar to the proof of Lemma
A.5
, we need to show that
\mathsf{T}_{0}
fixed point of
\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{Q}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\mathsf{w})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})
is
\{0\}
in the following three cases:

(i)

\mathbf{v}=n\delta_{i}
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 1}
,

(ii)

\mathbf{v}=m\delta_{i}+\delta_{j}
,
i\neq j
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 1}
,

(iii)

\mathbf{v}=\delta_{i-1}+2\delta_{i}+\delta_{i+1}
,
i
odd and
i\pm 1
even.

The first two cases are proven similarly as Lemma
A.5
and we shall not repeat. For case (iii), we have a natural map

\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{Q}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\mathsf{w})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})\to\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*},

which is given by sending
(X_{i},X_{i-1},Y_{i},Y_{i-1},\Phi_{i+1},\Phi_{i-1})
to
(\Phi_{i-1},\Phi_{i+1})\in\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
. The
\mathsf{T}_{0}
fixed point of
\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
is
\{0\}
, so for any representative
(X_{i},X_{i-1},Y_{i},Y_{i-1},\Phi_{i+1},\Phi_{i-1})
of a
\mathsf{T}_{0}
fixed point in
\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\mathsf{w})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})
, we have
\Phi_{i\pm 1}=0
. Then, by invariant theory,
R=\mathbb{C}\left[\left(\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{Q}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\mathsf{w})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})\right)^{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}}\right]
is generated by trace of cycles formed by
\{X_{i},X_{i-1},Y_{i},Y_{i-1}\}
. On the other hand,
\partial\mathsf{w}/\partial X_{i}=0
implies that
X_{i-1}Y_{i-1}Y_{i}=0
, so the nontrivial generators of
R
are
\operatorname{tr}(X_{i}Y_{i})^{m}
and
\operatorname{tr}(X_{i-1}Y_{i-1})^{m}
for
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 1}
. Both of the two types of generators have nonzero
\mathsf{T}_{0}
weights, so
R=\mathbb{C}
, and we conclude that
\left(\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{Q}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\mathsf{w})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})\right)^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}=\{0\}
.
∎

A.3.
Positive part of the Yangian of
D(2,1;\lambda)

Recall the
exceptional simple Lie superalgebra

\mathfrak{g=}D(2,1;\lambda)
, which has two presentations
D_{\lambda}
and
D^{\circ}_{\lambda}
with nonconjugate root systems. Explicitly,
\mathfrak{g}
is generated by
\{e_{i},f_{i}\}_{i=1,2,3}
, with parity given by
|e_{i}|=|f_{i}|=i+1\pmod{2}
if
\mathfrak{g}=D_{\lambda}
and
|e_{i}|=|f_{i}|=1
if
\mathfrak{g}=D^{\circ}_{\lambda}
, and subject to the relations

\displaystyle[e_{i},f_{j}]=0,\>i\neq j,\quad[[e_{i},f_{i}],e_{j}]=a_{ij}e_{j},\quad[[e_{i},f_{i}],f_{j}]=-a_{ij}f_{j},\;\forall\,\,i,j\in Q_{0},

and

\displaystyle\operatorname{ad}_{e_{i}}^{1+\delta(a_{ij}\neq 0)}(e_{j})=0=\operatorname{ad}_{f_{i}}^{1+\delta(a_{ij}\neq 0)}(f_{j}),\;\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=D_{\lambda},

\displaystyle a_{13}[[e_{1},e_{2}],e_{3}]=a_{12}[[e_{1},e_{3}],e_{2}],\;a_{13}[[f_{1},f_{2}],f_{3}]=a_{12}[[f_{1},f_{3}],f_{2}],\;\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=D^{\circ}_{\lambda}.

Here
\delta(-)=1
if
(-)
holds and
\delta(-)=0
otherwise, and the Cartan matrix
(a_{ij})
is given by

(A.4)

\displaystyle(a_{ij})=\begin{pmatrix}2&-1&0\\
1&0&\lambda\\
0&-1&2\end{pmatrix}\;\text{ if }\;\mathfrak{g}=D_{\lambda},\quad(a_{ij})=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1&1+\lambda\\
-1&0&-\lambda\\
1+\lambda&-\lambda&0\end{pmatrix}\;\text{ if }\;\mathfrak{g}=D^{\circ}_{\lambda}.

Define diagonal matrix
(d_{ij})
which is given by

\displaystyle(d_{ij})=\operatorname{diag}(1,-1,\lambda)\;\text{ if }\;\mathfrak{g}=D_{\lambda},\quad(d_{ij})=\mathrm{id}_{3\times 3}\;\text{ if }\;\mathfrak{g}=D^{\circ}_{\lambda}.

Definition A.9
.

The positive part of the Yangian of
D(2,1;\lambda)
, denoted
Y^{+}(\mathfrak{g})
for
\mathfrak{g}=D_{\lambda}
or
\mathfrak{g}=D^{\circ}_{\lambda}
, is the
\mathbb{C}[\hbar,\lambda]
-algebra generated by
\{x^{+}_{i,r}\}_{i\in\{1,2,3\},r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
subject to relations

(A.5)

\begin{gathered}(u-v)[x^{+}_{i}(u),x^{+}_{j}(v)]-d_{ii}a_{ij}\hbar/2\{x^{+}_{i}(u),x^{+}_{j}(v)\}=[x^{+}_{i,0},x^{+}_{j}(v)]-[x^{+}_{i}(u),x^{+}_{j,0}],\;\forall\,i,j,\\
[x^{+}_{i}(u),x^{+}_{j}(v)]=0,\;\text{if }a_{ij}=0,\\
\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{2}}[x^{+}_{i}(u_{\sigma(1)}),[x^{+}_{i}(u_{\sigma(2)}),x^{+}_{j}(v)]]=0,\;\text{if }\mathfrak{g}=D_{\lambda},\;a_{ij}\neq 0,\;\text{and}\;i\neq j,\\
a_{13}[[x^{+}_{1}(v_{1}),x^{+}_{2}(v_{2})],x^{+}_{3}(v_{3})]=a_{12}[[x^{+}_{1}(v_{1}),x^{+}_{3}(v_{3})],x^{+}_{2}(v_{2})],\;\text{if }\mathfrak{g}=D^{\circ}_{\lambda}.\end{gathered}

Here
x^{+}_{i}(z):=\sum_{r\geqslant 0}x^{+}_{i,r}z^{-r-1}
,
[a,b]=ab-(-1)^{|a|\cdot|b|}ba
,
\{a,b\}=ab+(-1)^{|a|\cdot|b|}ba
, and

\displaystyle|x^{+}_{i,r}|=\begin{cases}i+1\pmod{2},&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=D_{\lambda},\\
1,&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}=D_{\lambda}^{\circ}.\end{cases}

A.3.1.
Y^{+}(D_{\lambda})
and CoHA

Let
Q=(Q_{0},Q_{1})
be the
symmetric quiver
with
Q_{0}=\{1,2,3\}
and
Q_{1}=Q_{1}^{+}\sqcup Q_{1}^{-}\sqcup\mathcal{E}
, where

\displaystyle Q_{1}^{+}=\{X_{i}:i\to i+1\>|\>i=1,2\},\quad Q_{1}^{-}=\{Y_{i}:i+1\to i\>|\>i=1,2\},\quad\mathcal{E}=\{\Phi_{j}:j\to j\>|\>j=1,3\},

depicted as follows:

\Phi_{1}
\Phi_{3}
X_{1}
Y_{1}
X_{2}
Y_{2}

Let
\mathsf{T}_{0}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar_{1}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar_{2}}
act on
Q
by

\displaystyle(t_{1},t_{2})\in\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar_{1}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar_{2}}\colon(X_{1},Y_{1},X_{2},Y_{2},\Phi_{1},\Phi_{3})\mapsto(t_{1}X_{1},t_{1}Y_{1},t_{2}X_{2},t_{2}Y_{2},t_{1}^{-2}\Phi_{1},t_{2}^{-2}\Phi_{3}).

Take the
potential

\mathsf{W}
to be

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}=\Phi_{1}Y_{1}X_{1}+\Phi_{3}X_{2}Y_{2},

and denote
\mathsf{w}=\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{W})
.

Proposition A.10
.

There is a surjective
\mathbb{C}
-algebra map

\displaystyle Y^{+}(D_{\lambda})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar,\lambda]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar,\lambda)\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]}\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0}),\quad x^{+}_{i}(z)\mapsto e_{i}(z),\quad\hbar\mapsto 2\hbar_{1},\quad\lambda\mapsto\hbar_{2}/\hbar_{1}.

Proof.

We notice that the relations (
A.5
) for
\mathfrak{g}=D_{\lambda}
only involve at most two nodes in the quiver
Q
. The relation
[e_{1}(u),e_{3}(v)]=0
holds in
\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{Q,\mathsf{W}}
because the node
1
and
3
are disconnected. So to verify (
A.5
) on the CoHA side, it is enough to focus on the quiver representations which are supported on two neighboring nodes, that is, the following two subquivers with potentials restricted from
\mathsf{W}
:

\Phi_{1}
X_{1}
Y_{1}

\Phi_{3}
X_{2}
Y_{2}

The above two quivers with potentials are special cases of quivers with potentials in §
A.2
, and the rest of relations in (
A.5
) follow from Proposition
A.7
.
∎

A.3.2.
Y^{+}(D^{\circ}_{\lambda})
and CoHA

Let
Q^{\circ}=(Q^{\circ}_{0},Q^{\circ}_{1})
be the
symmetric quiver
with
Q^{\circ}_{0}=\mathbb{Z}/3\mathbb{Z}
and
Q^{\circ}_{1}=Q_{1}^{\circ+}\sqcup Q_{1}^{\circ-}
, where

\displaystyle Q_{1}^{\circ+}=\{X_{i}:i\to i+1\>|\>i\in Q_{0}\},\quad Q_{1}^{\circ-}=\{Y_{i}:i+1\to i\>|\>i\in Q_{0}\},

depicted as follows:

X_{1}
Y_{1}
X_{2}
Y_{2}
Y_{3}
X_{3}

Let
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar_{1}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar_{2}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar_{3}}
act on
Q^{\circ}
by

\displaystyle(t_{1},t_{2},t_{3})\in\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar_{1}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar_{2}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar_{3}}\colon(X_{i},Y_{i})\mapsto(t_{i}X_{i},t_{i}Y_{i}).

Take the
potential

\mathsf{W}^{\circ}
to be

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{\circ}=X_{3}X_{2}X_{1}+Y_{1}Y_{2}Y_{3},

which is fixed by the subtorus

\mathsf{T}_{0}=\{(t_{1},t_{2},t_{3})\in\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar_{1}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar_{2}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar_{3}}\>|\>t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}=1\},

and denote
\mathsf{w}^{\circ}=\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{W}^{\circ})
.
Denote the equivariant base ring to be

\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]:=H_{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\operatorname{pt})\cong\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2},\hbar_{3}]/(\hbar_{1}+\hbar_{2}+\hbar_{3}).

Proposition A.11
.

There is a surjective
\mathbb{C}
-algebra map

\displaystyle Y^{+}(D^{\circ}_{\lambda})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar,\lambda]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar,\lambda)\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{Q^{\circ},\mathsf{W}^{\circ}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]}\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0}),\quad x^{+}_{i}(z)\mapsto e_{i}(z),\quad\hbar\mapsto 2\hbar_{1},\quad\lambda\mapsto\hbar_{2}/\hbar_{1}.

Proof.

Similarly to the proof of Proposition
A.10
, all but the last relation in (
A.5
) reduce to the following quiver

X_{i}
Y_{i}

which is a special case of the quivers with potentials in Proposition
A.7
. It remains to show that the equation

(A.6)

\displaystyle\hbar_{3}[[e_{1}(v_{1}),e_{2}(v_{2})],e_{3}(v_{3})]=\hbar_{1}[[e_{1}(v_{1}),e_{3}(v_{3})],e_{2}(v_{2})]

holds in
\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{Q^{\circ},\mathsf{W}^{\circ}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]}\mathbb{C}(\mathsf{t}_{0})
. Let
\mathbf{v}=(1,1,1)
, then we claim that

(i)

the pushforward map
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}),\mathsf{w}^{\circ})_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})^{\mathrm{nil}}}\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}),\mathsf{w}^{\circ})
is injective after localization,

(ii)

the specialization map
\mathrm{sp}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}),\mathsf{w}^{\circ})\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}))
is injective.

Assuming the claims for now, it is enough to prove that (
A.6
) holds in
\widetilde{\mathcal{SH}}_{Q^{\circ},0}
, the cohomological Hall algebra for
Q^{\circ}
with zero potential. (
A.6
) is equivalent to the following symmetric form

(A.7)

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/3\mathbb{Z}}\hbar_{i+1}\left(e_{i-1}(v_{i-1})e_{i}(v_{i})e_{i+1}(v_{i+1})-e_{i+1}(v_{i+1})e_{i}(v_{i})e_{i-1}(v_{i-1})\right)=0\,.

For the dimension vector
\mathbf{v}=(1,1,1)
, each node has one dimension, and let
x_{i}
be the equivariant parameter for the node
i
, then
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}))\cong\mathbb{C}[x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{0}]
, and we have
e_{i}(v_{i})=\sum_{n\geqslant 0}x_{i}^{n}v_{i}^{-n-1}=\frac{1}{v_{i}-x_{i}}
. Note that the sign twist (
3.18
) for
Q^{\circ}
is trivial. Direct computation shows that

\displaystyle e_{i-1}(v_{i-1})e_{i}(v_{i})e_{i+1}(v_{i+1})=\frac{(x_{i+1}-x_{i-1}+\hbar_{i+1})(x_{i}-x_{i-1}+\hbar_{i-1})(x_{i+1}-x_{i}+\hbar_{i})}{(v_{1}-x_{1})(v_{2}-x_{2})(v_{3}-x_{3})}\,,

\displaystyle e_{i+1}(v_{i+1})e_{i}(v_{i})e_{i-1}(v_{i-1})=\frac{(x_{i-1}-x_{i+1}+\hbar_{i+1})(x_{i-1}-x_{i}+\hbar_{i-1})(x_{i}-x_{i+1}+\hbar_{i})}{(v_{1}-x_{1})(v_{2}-x_{2})(v_{3}-x_{3})}\,.

Then

LHS of (
A.7
)

\displaystyle=2\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/3\mathbb{Z}}\frac{-\hbar_{i+1}(x_{1}-x_{3})(x_{2}-x_{1})(x_{3}-x_{2})+(x_{i}-x_{i-1})(\hbar_{i+1}^{2}\hbar_{i}+\hbar_{i}^{2}\hbar_{i+1}-\hbar_{i}\hbar_{i-1}\hbar_{i+1})}{(v_{1}-x_{1})(v_{2}-x_{2})(v_{3}-x_{3})}=0.

In the remainder, we prove claims (i) and (ii).

The claim (i) is proven similarly to Lemma
A.5
, namely, we need to show that

(A.8)

\left(\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{Q^{\circ}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\mathsf{w}^{\circ})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})\right)^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}=\{0\}.

For
i\in\mathbb{Z}/3\mathbb{Z}
,
\partial\mathsf{w}^{\circ}/\partial X_{i}=0
gives
X_{i-1}X_{i+1}=0
, and
\partial\mathsf{w}^{\circ}/\partial Y_{i}=0
gives
Y_{i+1}Y_{i-1}=0
. Then
\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})
-invariant functions in
\mathbb{C}[\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{Q^{\circ}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\mathsf{w}^{\circ})]
are generated by
\{X_{i}Y_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/3\mathbb{Z}}
, and they have relations
X_{i}Y_{i}X_{j}Y_{j}=0
for any pair
i\neq j
. Thus,
\mathbb{C}[\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{Q^{\circ}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})}(\mathsf{w}^{\circ})/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})]
is spanned by the monomials
\{(X_{i}Y_{i})^{n}\}_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/3\mathbb{Z}}^{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
, with the only
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-invariant functions being the constant ones. This shows (
A.8
).

By dimensional reduction, we have

H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0}),\mathsf{w}^{\circ})\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(Z),

where
Z
is the locus
\{X_{1}X_{2}=0,\,Y_{1}Y_{2}=0\}
in the space of representations
R\cong\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[X_{1},X_{2},Y_{1},Y_{2}]
of quiver

1
1
1
X_{1}
Y_{1}
X_{2}
Y_{2}

By the commutativity of the specialization map and the canonical map
[
COZZ1
, Rem. 7.16]
, claim (ii) is equivalent to the claim that the
pushforward
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(Z)\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(R)
is injective. Note that
\mathbb{C}[R/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})]
is generated by
X_{1}X_{2},Y_{1}Y_{2},X_{1}Y_{1},X_{2}Y_{2}
which are all positive with respect to the diagonal subtorus
\mathbb{C}^{*}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar_{1}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar_{2}}\cong\mathsf{T}_{0}
, so
(R/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v}))^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}=\{0\}
. Since
\{0\}\in Z/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})
,
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(Z)\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(R)
is injective after
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-localization.

We can stratify
Z
by
Z=Z_{0}\cup Z_{1}\cup Z_{2}\cup Z_{3}
with

\displaystyle Z_{0}=Z\cap\{X_{1}\neq 0,Y_{2}\neq 0\},\;Z_{1}=Z\cap\{X_{1}\neq 0,Y_{2}=0\},\;Z_{2}=Z\cap\{X_{1}=0,Y_{2}\neq 0\},\;Z_{4}=Z\cap\{X_{1}=0,Y_{2}=0\}.

Write
\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{x_{1}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{x_{2}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{x_{3}}
and let the equivariant parameters of
\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})
be
x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}
, then

\displaystyle H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(Z_{0})\cong\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2},x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}]/(x_{2}-x_{1}+\hbar_{1},x_{2}-x_{3}+\hbar_{2}),

\displaystyle H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(Z_{1})\cong\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2},x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}]/(x_{2}-x_{1}+\hbar_{1}),

\displaystyle H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(Z_{2})\cong\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2},x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}]/(x_{2}-x_{3}+\hbar_{2}),

\displaystyle H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(Z_{3})\cong\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2},x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}].

All of the cohomologies in the above are free
\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}]
-modules and are concentrated in even degrees, and applying excision sequence to the stratification
Z=Z_{0}\cup Z_{1}\cup Z_{2}\cup Z_{3}
shows that
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(Z)
is a free
\mathbb{C}[\hbar_{1},\hbar_{2}]
-module. Thus
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(Z)\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})}(R)
is injective without localization.
∎

Appendix B
R
-matrices for quivers with potentials associated to
\mathfrak{gl}_{2|1}

It is well-known that
\mathfrak{gl}_{2|1}
has the following three kinds of Kac-Dynkin diagrams:

which we will refer to as the even-odd, the odd-even, and the odd-odd cases. We associate the following three quivers with potentials to the above three Kac-Dynkin diagrams:

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\quad\mathsf{W}=0.

In all three cases, let
\mathsf{T}_{0}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
act on the arrows
X
,
Y
, and
\Phi
(if exists) with weights
0
,
1
, and
-1
, respectively. As in the beginning of §
3.2
, we fix a splitting of edges
Q_{1}=\mathcal{A}\sqcup\mathcal{A}^{*}\sqcup\mathcal{E}
, where
\mathcal{A}=\{X\}
,
\mathcal{A}^{*}=\{Y\}
,
\mathcal{E}=\{\Phi\}
or empty.

In the following, we work with the cyclic stability condition (
\theta<0
) when defining quiver varieties. The framing dimension vector is take to be

\mathbf{d}=(\mathbf{d}_{1},\mathbf{d}_{2})=(1,0).

We compute the
R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)
matrix for the symmetric quiver variety
\mathcal{M}(2\mathbf{d})
with torus
\mathsf{A}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
action on the framing as
u\mathbf{d}+\mathbf{d}
. Here
R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)
is defined in Theorem
3.21
. The framed potential will be specified case-by-case.

B.1.
The even-odd case

Consider the following framed quiver with potential:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{{}}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}
