\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\qquad\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}=X\Phi Y+B\Phi A,

and the
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-equivariant critical cohomology

\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{eo}}_{\mathbf{d}}=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geqslant 0}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{eo}}_{\mathbf{d}}(\mathbf{v}),\quad\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{eo}}_{\mathbf{d}}(\mathbf{v})=H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}).

Here we let
\mathsf{T}_{0}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
act on
A
and
B
with weights
0
and
1
respectively.

The argument in
[
Dav3
, Lem. 6.3]
can be applied to the above quiver variety with potential, and we have an explicit description of the critical locus

\displaystyle\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}(\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}})=\{XY+AB=0,\;\Phi=0\}/\!\!/_{\theta}\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})=:\mathcal{M}^{\prime}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}).

Applying dimensional reduction along
\Phi
, we get an isomorphism

(B.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{eo}}_{\mathbf{d}}(\mathbf{v})\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}^{\prime}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})).

Note that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\prime}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\cong\begin{cases}\operatorname{pt},&\mathbf{v}=(0,0),\\
\operatorname{pt},&\mathbf{v}=(1,0),\\
\mathbb{C},&\mathbf{v}=(1,1),\\
\,\emptyset,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Let
b_{\mathbf{v}}
be the image of the fundamental class
[\mathcal{M}^{\prime}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})]
in
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{eo}}_{\mathbf{d}}(\mathbf{v})
via the isomorphism (
B.1
). We also use the short-hand notation
b_{0}
,
b_{1}
, and
b_{2}
to denote
b_{(0,0)}
,
b_{(1,0)}
, and
b_{(1,1)}
respectively. Then
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{eo}}_{\mathbf{d}}
is a free
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
module with a basis
\{b_{0},b_{1},b_{2}\}
.

Consider the specialization map
[
COZZ1
, Def. 7.14]
:

\mathrm{sp}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})).

By
[
COZZ1
, Rem. 7.16]
,
\mathrm{sp}(b_{\mathbf{v}})
agrees with the pushforward of the fundamental class of
\{XY+AB=0\}/\!\!/_{\theta}\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v})
into
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
, that is,

\displaystyle\mathrm{sp}(b_{0})=[\mathcal{M}((0,0),\mathbf{d})],\quad\mathrm{sp}(b_{1})=\hbar\,[\mathcal{M}((1,0),\mathbf{d})],\quad\mathrm{sp}(b_{2})=\hbar\,[\mathcal{M}((1,1),\mathbf{d})].

In particular,
\mathrm{sp}
is injective.

Proposition B.1
.

In the basis
\{b_{i}\otimes b_{j}\}_{0\leqslant i,j\leqslant 2}
, the
R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)
matrix for
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{eo}}_{\mathbf{d}}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{eo}}_{\mathbf{d}}
is

\displaystyle R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)=\frac{u+\hbar P^{\mathrm{eo}}}{u+\hbar}.

Here
P^{\mathrm{eo}}
is the super permutation matrix on
\mathbb{C}^{2|1}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{2|1}
, where
\mathbb{C}^{2|1}=\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{b_{0},b_{1},b_{2}\}
with
b_{0}
,
b_{1}
even and
b_{2}
odd.

Proof.

Since specializations commute with stable envelopes by
[
COZZ1
, Prop. 7.18]
, the stable envelopes

\operatorname{Stab}_{\pm}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},2\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A}},\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},2\mathbf{d}),\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}})

agree with the restriction of their zero potential counterparts
\operatorname{Stab}_{\pm}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},2\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}\times\mathsf{A}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},2\mathbf{d}))
to the image of specialization map. To compute the stable envelopes and
R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)
matrices, we have the following observation: if
\mathbf{v}_{2}=0
, then the quiver variety
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},2\mathbf{d})
is a special case in §
9.2
, we can directly apply the results there and get

\displaystyle R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)(b_{i}\otimes b_{j})=\frac{1}{u+\hbar}\left(u\,b_{i}\otimes b_{j}+\hbar\,b_{j}\otimes b_{i}\right),\;\text{ for }\;0\leqslant i,j\leqslant 1.

As
\mathcal{M}((2,2),2\mathbf{d})
has only one fixed component
\mathcal{M}((1,1),\mathbf{d})\times\mathcal{M}((1,1),\mathbf{d})
, so the
R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)
matrix in the
\mathbf{v}=(2,2)
case is a scalar. It is easy to compute that

\displaystyle R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)(b_{2}\otimes b_{2})=\frac{u-\hbar}{u+\hbar}\,b_{2}\otimes b_{2}\,.

Note that
\mathcal{M}((1,1),2\mathbf{d})\cong\mathrm{Tot}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathcal{O}(-1)^{\oplus 2})\times\mathbb{C}^{2}
, where the
\mathsf{A}
action on
\mathbb{C}^{2}
is trivial, and
\mathrm{Tot}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathcal{O}(-1)^{\oplus 2})
is the quiver variety with gauge rank
1
defined at the beginning of §
8.1
. We can directly apply the results there and get

\displaystyle R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)(b_{i}\otimes b_{j})=\frac{1}{u+\hbar}\left(u\,b_{i}\otimes b_{j}+\hbar\,b_{j}\otimes b_{i}\right),\;\text{ for $(i,j)=(0,2)$ or $(2,0)$}\;.

Let
X=\mathcal{M}((2,1),2\mathbf{d})
and
X_{\mathrm{iso}}
be the open subset of
X
on which
A
is an isomorphism. Then
X^{\mathsf{A}}\subset X_{\mathrm{iso}}
, and we have
X_{\mathrm{iso}}\cong\mathrm{Tot}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathcal{O}(-1)^{\oplus 2})\times\operatorname{End}(\mathbb{C}^{2})_{\hbar}\times\operatorname{End}(\mathbb{C}^{2})_{-\hbar}
, where the
\mathbb{C}^{2}
’s are
\mathsf{A}
-equivariantly identified with the framing vector space, the subscripts
\hbar
and
-\hbar
stand for the
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
weights (on the whole endomorphism space). Denote
\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\pm}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})_{Y}
the closure of attracting set of
\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}
in
Y
for
Y=X_{\mathrm{iso}}\times X^{\mathsf{A}}
or
X\times X^{\mathsf{A}}
. Note that

\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\pm}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})_{X_{\mathrm{iso}}\times X^{\mathsf{A}}}=\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\pm}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})_{X\times X^{\mathsf{A}}}\,\bigcap\,(X_{\mathrm{iso}}\times X^{\mathsf{A}}).

So the composition
H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(X^{\mathsf{A}})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Stab}_{\pm}}H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(X)\xrightarrow{\text{restriction}}H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(X_{\mathrm{iso}})
is equal to the stable envelope for
X_{\mathrm{iso}}
. Then
R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)
for
X
is equal to the product of
R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)
for
\mathrm{Tot}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathcal{O}(-1)^{\oplus 2})
,
\operatorname{End}(\mathbb{C}^{2})_{\hbar}
, and
\operatorname{End}(\mathbb{C}^{2})_{-\hbar}
:

\displaystyle R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)=\frac{1}{u+\hbar}\begin{pmatrix}u&\hbar\\
\hbar&u\end{pmatrix}\times\frac{u-\hbar}{u+\hbar}\times\frac{u+\hbar}{u-\hbar}=\frac{1}{u+\hbar}\begin{pmatrix}u&\hbar\\
\hbar&u\end{pmatrix},

and we get

R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)(b_{i}\otimes b_{j})=\frac{1}{u+\hbar}\left(u\,b_{i}\otimes b_{j}+\hbar\,b_{j}\otimes b_{i}\right),\;\text{ for $(i,j)=(1,2)$ or $(2,1)$}\;.\qed

Remark B.2
.

One can also use the explicit formula in
[
COZZ1
, Cor. 4.25]
to compute the stable envelopes and
R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)
.

B.2.
The odd-even case

Consider the following framed quiver with potential:

\hbox to102.37pt{\vbox to83.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 15.84746pt\lower-49.43436pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#FFFFFF} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#FFFFFF} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 11.57 0 C 11.57 6.39 6.39 11.57 0 11.57 C -6.39 11.57 -11.57 6.39 -11.57 0 C -11.57 -6.39 -6.39 -11.57 0 -11.57 C 6.39 -11.57 11.57 -6.39 11.57 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.00117pt}{-2.15277pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -4.15 -2.98)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\qquad\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}=Y\Phi X+ABYX,

and the
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-equivariant critical cohomology

\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{oe}}_{\mathbf{d}}=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geqslant 0}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{oe}}_{\mathbf{d}}(\mathbf{v}),\quad\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{oe}}_{\mathbf{d}}(\mathbf{v})=H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}).

Here we let
\mathsf{T}_{0}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
act on
A
and
B
with weights
0
and
-1
respectively.

Solving the equation
d\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}=0
under the cyclic stability condition, we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}(\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}})\neq\emptyset\;\Longleftrightarrow\;\text{$\mathbf{v}=(0,0)$, $(1,0)$, or $(1,1)$}.

When
\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}(\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}})
is nonempty, dimension reduction along
\Phi
and
B
yields isomorphism:

(B.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{oe}}_{\mathbf{d}}(\mathbf{v})\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}^{\prime}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})),\;\text{ where }\;\mathcal{M}^{\prime}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})=\{B=0,\Phi=0,Y=0\}/\!\!/_{\theta}\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v}).

Let
b_{\mathbf{v}}
be the image of the fundamental class
[\mathcal{M}^{\prime}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})]
in
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{oe}}_{\mathbf{d}}(\mathbf{v})
via the isomorphism (
B.2
). We also use the short-hand notation
b_{0}
,
b_{1}
, and
b_{2}
to denote
b_{(0,0)}
,
b_{(1,0)}
, and
b_{(1,1)}
respectively. Then
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{oe}}_{\mathbf{d}}
is a free
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
module with a basis
\{b_{0},b_{1},b_{2}\}
.

Similarly, we have a specialization map

\mathrm{sp}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})),

which satisfies

\displaystyle\mathrm{sp}(b_{0})=[\mathcal{M}((0,0),\mathbf{d})],\quad\mathrm{sp}(b_{1})=[\mathcal{M}((1,0),\mathbf{d})],\quad\mathrm{sp}(b_{2})=\hbar\,[\mathcal{M}((1,1),\mathbf{d})].

In particular,
\mathrm{sp}
is injective.
As in Proposition
B.1
, we have:

Proposition B.3
.

In the basis
\{b_{i}\otimes b_{j}\}_{0\leqslant i,j\leqslant 2}
, the
R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)
matrix for
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{oe}}_{\mathbf{d}}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{oe}}_{\mathbf{d}}
is

\displaystyle R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)=\frac{u-\hbar P^{\mathrm{oe}}}{u-\hbar}.

Here
P^{\mathrm{oe}}
is the super permutation matrix on
\mathbb{C}^{1|2}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{1|2}
, where
\mathbb{C}^{1|2}=\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{b_{0},b_{1},b_{2}\}
with
b_{0}
even and
b_{1}
,
b_{2}
odd.

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{6.50053pt}{-24.37477pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 8.99 -33.73)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\qquad\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}=ABYX,

and the
\mathsf{T}_{0}
-equivariant critical cohomology:

\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{oo}}_{\mathbf{d}}=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geqslant 0}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{oo}}_{\mathbf{d}}(\mathbf{v}),\quad\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{oo}}_{\mathbf{d}}(\mathbf{v})=H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}).

Here we let
\mathsf{T}_{0}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
act on
A
and
B
with weights
0
and
-1
respectively.

Solving the equation
d\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}}=0
under the cyclic stability condition, we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}(\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}})\neq\emptyset\;\Longleftrightarrow\;\text{$\mathbf{v}=(0,0)$, $(1,0)$, or $(1,1)$}.

When
\mathrm{Crit}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}(\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{W}^{\mathrm{fr}})
is nonempty, dimension reduction along
B
yields isomorphism:

(B.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{oo}}_{\mathbf{d}}(\mathbf{v})\cong H^{\mathsf{T}_{0}}(\mathcal{M}^{\prime}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})),\;\text{ where }\;\mathcal{M}^{\prime}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})=\{B=0,Y=0\}/\!\!/_{\theta}\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v}).

Let
b_{\mathbf{v}}
be the image of the fundamental class
[\mathcal{M}^{\prime}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})]
in
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{oo}}_{\mathbf{d}}(\mathbf{v})
via the isomorphism (
B.3
). We also use the short-hand notation
b_{0}
,
b_{1}
, and
b_{2}
to denote
b_{(0,0)}
,
b_{(1,0)}
, and
b_{(1,1)}
respectively. Then
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{oo}}_{\mathbf{d}}
is a free
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
module with a basis
\{b_{0},b_{1},b_{2}\}
.

As before, we have a specialization map which satisfies:

\displaystyle\mathrm{sp}(b_{0})=[\mathcal{M}((0,0),\mathbf{d})],\quad\mathrm{sp}(b_{1})=[\mathcal{M}((1,0),\mathbf{d})],\quad\mathrm{sp}(b_{2})=\hbar\,[\mathcal{M}((1,1),\mathbf{d})].

In particular,
\mathrm{sp}
is injective.
As in Proposition
B.1
, we have:

Proposition B.4
.

In the basis
\{b_{i}\otimes b_{j}\}_{0\leqslant i,j\leqslant 2}
, the
R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)
matrix for
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{oo}}_{\mathbf{d}}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{oo}}_{\mathbf{d}}
is

\displaystyle R^{\mathrm{sup}}(u)=\frac{u-\hbar P^{\mathrm{oo}}}{u-\hbar}.

Here
P^{\mathrm{oo}}
is the super permutation matrix on
\mathbb{C}^{2|1}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{2|1}
, where
\mathbb{C}^{2|1}=\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{b_{0},b_{1},b_{2}\}
with
b_{0}
,
b_{2}
even and
b_{1}
odd.
